# 6. Runtime View

## Overview

Forty-one scenarios: the host-managed session lifecycle (happy path) and the session an agent registers itself, the end of a turn
with drain, sign-off and listen window (reachability, quality goal 4), a listen
window ended by the human (recovery), the bus failing underneath a session
(fail-open, quality goal 1), the typing indicator while a mention is answered
(honest presence), the coordinator crown as a lease (who coordinates, and
what happens when that agent goes idle or dies), the handoff state that
survives a compacted conversation, an approval from the agent's own human
over the bus, the counters that measure the bus's own overhead, an agent asking a human for something, messages pushed into the session as a channel, Claude Code's permission prompts answered from Mattermost, the agent cards and task states the server keeps from the posts, the references in posts that a human can click, who may wake an agent at all, how the turn hooks reach the bus through the MCP server without the token, how a corral session keeps the bot token on the host altogether, with its MCP server there, how the coordinator's server notices a stalled bus and open asks remind their human, how the OpenCode plugin and the pi extension run the same turn cycle through the hook API, who answers a mention of an agent that has left, how an agent talks in channels besides its home (guest channels), how it sees the files a post carries and attaches files of its own, how the registrar's signature vouches for whom an agent answers to, how a home's ask mode sends a worker's question to its coordinator first, how a trusted human's pinned mandate lets one coordinator give tasks in another home, and how the host copy is installed into a release of its own, switched and rolled back, and how it comes from a release tag, its signature checked when the host pins the release keys, how every server learns which release is out and what it needs, and how the host copy updates itself between sessions to the release the registrar names, how a host logs in to Keycloak for the NATS adapter, how the login service manages an agent's consumers, how a home runs over NATS, how a NATS sender's owner and presence are known, how the crown of a home works over NATS, how the channel directory keeps membership and anchors, how it is maintained and imported from Mattermost, and how the login service vouches for an agent's owner and trust list. Together they show where the bus may block an agent
and why it can never wedge one.

Each scenario is referenced as `RT-6.x` (its section number) from the tests in
`tests/hooks/` or `mcp/tests/`, from `SKILL.md` and from the code that implements it.
`scripts/doc-check.py` fails when a reference points nowhere or a scenario has
no test.

---

## 6.1 Host-managed session lifecycle

**Purpose:** Shows how the registration secret stays on the host while the agent gets a working identity. Since 0.39.0 the bot token stays there as well (RT-6.18).

**Trigger:** `corral run` starts an agent session; the session ends.

**Participants:** Host session hooks, Shell CLI, MCP server, Turn hooks; corral, Registrar, Mattermost REST API, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement, `#usable` Low noise

### Sequence

1. corral runs the host session hooks' `preStart` (IF-06) with the developer's environment (IF-08). The hooks first check, before they source anything, that they run from the installed host copy and not from a place a sandbox can write; from such a place `preStart` contributes only `CB_BUS_UNAVAILABLE` and `postEnd` runs nothing (Section 7.1). They say in the banner when a newer plugin is installed than they are; that plugin by version only, never by path, since it may come from a corral home (Section 7.1).
2. Host session hooks decide whether to register: always with `CB_MM_PROJECT_CHANNEL`, otherwise only with `MM_REGISTER=always`. Without registration they exit 0 and print one banner line — the session has no bus.
3. Host session hooks read the secret (gopass preferred) and call the Shell CLI to register a bot named `<host>-<project>-<session>` at the Registrar (IF-01). A Claude Code start with `--resume <id>` or `--continue` takes over the bot kept for it instead, without registering (RT-6.18 step 10).
4. Shell CLI resolves team and channels and joins the project channel (IF-02), marks the welcome DM read, sets status 💤 idle.
5. Host session hooks start the session's MCP server on the host and return the contribution (IF-06): ids, channel names, the server's two endpoints and its per-session key (RT-6.18) — never the bot token, nor `MM_REG_SECRET`, nor `MM_REG_BOT_NAME` (so the CLI inside cannot stop the host's session).
6. Inside the sandbox, the plugin's MCP entry point relays to that server, which holds the bot token; the Turn hooks and the listener hold none and ask it (RT-6.17); the agent follows the Agent skill (`HELLO`, work, sign-off). Host-managed — on the host, or with a token at startup from an older host install — the MCP server offers no `session_start`/`session_end` and leaves the self-registration text out of its instructions: the agent must not register, and every turn would carry that text.
7. At the end, `postEnd` stops the MCP server on the host, which unregisters its bot (own token, `agent-*` only, RT-6.18 step 7); `postEnd` then sets offline and unregisters again as a safety net — a 404 counts as done, and so does a token both refuse, the registrar and Mattermost (gone already, see the error table): the server's exit work removed the bot, and `postEnd` logs `<name> unregistered already (by its MCP server)` and removes the session file. A Claude Code session that ended by itself keeps its bot instead, offline, for `claude --resume <id>` within `CB_SESSION_KEEP_SEC` (24 h, RT-6.18 step 9).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 3 | Registrar unreachable or rejects | Banner warning, session continues without a bot (hook is `optional: true`). A refusal names the registrar's own reason: `register failed: HTTP 403: <reason>` (plain text or JSON `.message`/`.error`, printable, 200 characters; withheld if it holds the secret) |
| 5 | The MCP server cannot start on the host | No token reaches the sandbox: the bot is unregistered again and the session runs without the bus, the banner and the agent's note saying why (RT-6.18) |
| 3 | No bus settings reached the session (no hook, no env) | `bus_info` answers `on_bus: false` with the reason instead of failing; the other bus tools fail |
| 4 | Channel missing or not joinable | Bot is unregistered again, session file removed; channels are never created |
| 7 | Unregister fails | Status already offline; session file kept so `mm-agent-sweep.sh` can remove the bot with its own token. `postEnd` logs why for each credential, `could not unregister <name>: token: <reason>; secret: <reason>` (the helper's error on one line, 160 characters, the token and the secret blanked; `no token`/`no secret` when there was none), and the retry; the start hook's unregister-again on failing closed (RT-6.18 error handling) logs the same reasons |
| 7 | `postEnd`: the bot is gone already — the registrar refuses its own token (`401`, or `404`) and `/users/me` with it answers `401` too (`mm_token_gone`) | The MCP server's exit work unregistered the bot first (step 7): no warning, `<name> unregistered already (by its MCP server)`, and the session file goes as after an unregister. A registrar that fails (`5xx`) or does not answer, and any other failure, keep the warning and the file. The start hook's unregister-again reads it the same way once it had started the host process, not before. Before 0.86.4 every corral session ended with a false `could not unregister … http=401` |
| 7 | Sweep: the bot is gone already, by the same rule (`mm_token_gone`) | Only the stale file is removed. Any other failure is printed with its HTTP status and the file kept |
| 7 | Sweep: per-bot state files (`asks-`, `coord-`, `gate-`, `last-`, `listen-`, `presence-`, `turn-`, `update-<uid>.env`, `stats-<uid>.jsonl`, `permission-`, `crown-<uid>.log`) | Not treated as sessions. With `--apply` those whose bot has no session file left and that were untouched for a day are removed; a live session keeps writing its own |
| 7 | Sweep: a bot `postEnd` kept for `claude --resume <id>` (RT-6.18 step 9) | `KEPT` until `CB_SESSION_KEEP_SEC` after its end mark (`--force` sooner), then unregistered with its own token, its session file and state directory removed — the same lib function as `preStart`'s reaping (RT-6.18 step 10) |
| 7 | The session is not resumed | The kept bot stays offline until the grace period ends; then the next `preStart` or the sweep unregisters it (RT-6.18 step 10) |

## 6.2 End of a turn: drain, sign-off, listen

**Purpose:** The only place the bus blocks an agent; shows the order of checks and how each is bounded.

**Trigger:** The agent finishes a turn in Claude Code (`Stop` hook, IF-05).

**Participants:** Turn hooks, MCP server, Shell CLI; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Mattermost WebSocket

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability

### Sequence

The Turn hooks and the listener get every bus value below from the MCP server's hook API (RT-6.17), never from Mattermost.

Every block carries two texts: the instruction for the agent (`reason`) and a one-line banner for the developer (`systemMessage`), so the terminal shows why the turn has not ended: 📨 collecting unread messages, 💬 answering a post in its thread, ✋ signing off, 👂 `Listening on Mattermost until HH:MM in the background (N min) · your next prompt ends it`.

1. Turn hooks ask how many messages wait (mentions, DMs, and posts for all agents, RT-6.11 step 6), which the MCP server counts (`unread.py`, IF-02) from its own **read watermark** (`watermark.py`): per channel the newest post the bot has handled, in `read-<uid>.env` in the state directory (0600, one line `<channel_id> <post_id> <create_at>` per channel). Handled is a post handed to the agent (`wait_for_events`, the channel push of RT-6.12), one the wake gate held back (RT-6.16), and the bot's own. An older post of the channel still waiting in the inbox keeps the mark before it; the posts handled meanwhile are remembered, and the mark passes them only once that one is handled (a server that ends in between hands them over again after its restart), so a mention handed over before an older channel post (the push acknowledges a batch mentions first, `wait_for_events` returns them first) never counts again (before 0.86.4 it did, until the next post there: a turn-end check reporting `pending=1` that `wait_for_events` never returned). The newest post the mark passed stays remembered as well: an older post that arrives later pulls the mark back before itself, and its hand-over moves the mark past that newest one again. While a (re)connect's backfill reads what came after the mark, the live stream already runs, but no hand-over moves the mark until the backfill has its list of posts, and each post on that list keeps its channel's mark before it until the backfill has queued or passed it; a queued edit of a post not handed over yet keeps it there too. So a server that ends meanwhile leaves such a post to the next backfill. What is handed over while the WebSocket is down moves the mark only once a reconnect's backfill has read what came meanwhile; a backfill that cannot read leaves it for the next one (before 0.94.1 it never moved the mark, and the turn-end check counted those posts as pending, which `wait_for_events` never returned). A channel without a line reads from the bot's creation, and none more than a day back. What waits is what came after the mark: in a DM or group every post by someone else, elsewhere the posts that name the agent (or `@channel`, `@all`, `@here`) and those for all agents; never a gated one. Only what `wait_for_events` can hand over counts: outside a DM and the channels the server watches (the work, project and default channel, by the id the host passed and by name, and the guest channels of RT-6.22: one list, `client.watched_channel_ids`, for the inbox and the count), a post counts only when it names the agent, so a group post that names nobody, an `@channel` or a post for all agents in another channel the bot is in counts nothing. In a guest channel only a post naming the agent or a reply in one of its threads there counts (RT-6.22 step 3). One request lists the bot's channels with the time of each one's newest post, and only a channel whose newest post is past its mark is read (one GET); Mattermost's unread counters decide nothing and only stand in for a channel without that time. An edit that made an older post name the agent waits in the inbox behind the mark (RT-6.12 step 3): the count adds it until it is handed over. A run of the bot's own, system and gated posts right after the mark moves it on, so a DM that holds only the bot's own post (a permission prompt, RT-6.13) is not read again. Anything pending → block: collect with `wait_for_events`, answer, finish; the reason says how many need action when some are an agent's fyi posts, which need no answer (RT-6.14 step 8: from the posts already read and one user lookup, only while the drain blocks). At most 3 drain rounds per turn.
   The mark belongs to the bot, not the session: the next server of the same bot (`claude --resume <id>`, the workspace's next server, RT-6.10) goes on from it, and nothing it handed over is handed over again. It goes when the MCP server unregisters its bot, and with the host process's state directory (RT-6.18); `mm-agent-sweep.sh` does not know the file, so a bot the sweep unregisters leaves it behind. After every hand-over and every post of its own the server also clears Mattermost's unread display of the channel, for its UI only, unless an event there still waits.
2. Turn hooks ask whether something changed on the bus since the agent's last sign-off: its latest post in the work channel is no `IDLE`/`BYE`, or an addressed event reached it after that post — a mention or DM not gated, a trusted human's yes/no on its open ask, holding the 👑 a human's plain post (RT-6.16), handed over by `wait_for_events` or the channel push. Then → block once per turn: post a sign-off in the `HELLO` thread. Nothing changed → no block, however long the turn worked in the terminal: the sign-off tells the bus what it needs, and the bus saw nothing new; a stall event, a digest or another agent's fyi post change nothing either. An agent with a `HELLO` and no sign-off yet is asked. A sign-off is a status post in lines, not a ` · ` chain: a headline with the bus word and the gist (`IDLE · <gist>`), then one line each for `**done:**`, `**open:**` and `**next:**`, a `- ` list under the label for several items, links inline. Other status posts (`DELEGATE`, `WAITING`, `RESULT`, `PROGRESS`) have the same shape. The headline is what a bystander reads (RT-6.14 step 6); the handoff after a compaction gets the whole post (RT-6.7). Whoever posts as the agent (the MCP server, `mm-agent-post.sh`) keeps that post's id, time and first words, and the `HELLO` thread, in `last-<uid>.env` in the state directory when it posts in the work channel; a delivery as above adds `DELIVERED=1`, which the next own post clears. A reminder the server sends on its own (RT-6.19 step 1) is no post of the agent's and is not kept. The MCP server answers the hook from the file (`signed_off`) and asks Mattermost nothing. Without one (an agent that never posted there) it reads the channel's last 100 posts, every turn.
   **An answer in the thread (since 0.89.0).** Before the sign-off, and inside a listen window or a turn a push started too: a post that reached the agent and needs an answer has none by it in its thread → block once for that post: answer it there with `reply_in_thread`, the bus does not see the terminal. Needs an answer: a mention or DM, a post marked `for_crown` (RT-6.6 step 5) or a reply in a thread the agent posted in, handed over by `wait_for_events` or pushed by the channel (RT-6.12; at once, or when the crown came back and a held post now wakes the agent; not held for the digest, not dropped). A human's post counts as it is. An agent's post counts only when it wants an answer: it names this agent, is a DM, or its bus word asks for something (intent `action` or `reply`, RT-6.14: `TASK`, `DELEGATE`, `HANDOVER`, `CANCEL`, `BLOCKED`, `NO`); an agent's plain reply in the agent's thread ("thanks") does not, so no courtesy reply between agents blocks, and an agent that gets an `ACK` or `RESULT` back owes none. Never: a gated post, an fyi post (`ACK`, `RESULT`, `DONE` …), a reaction, an expired answer (RT-6.11), the 👑's advice (RT-6.25), a system, webhook or server-own post (a reminder, an ask's link line, a notice of RT-6.21), the server's own events (a stall, a digest). Answered: a post of the agent's in that thread — a reply under a root post too, the link line of an `ask_human` with that `root_id` — and for a DM a post in it; resolving an ask (`resolve_ask`, with or without a note) answers the ask's thread. Not: a reaction; a pure status (`IDLE`, `BYE`, `HELLO`, `ASSIGNMENTS`), so the next sign-off in the `HELLO` thread answers no question there (an `ACK`, `RESULT` or `DONE` does); a line the server writes on its own: a reminder, a permission prompt and the relay's verdict and notes (RT-6.13), an ask mode's or mandate's acknowledgement, a system reply (`unanswered.answers`; the relay's lines and `resolve_ask`'s note are posted inside `unanswered.server_line`). The MCP server keeps the open ones in `unanswered-<uid>.env` beside `last-<uid>.env` (one line each: post, thread root, channel id and name, author, time, DM, reminded), so the next server of the bot keeps them; at most the 10 newest, none older than 24 h, removed when the MCP server unregisters its bot (as the read mark, not by the sweep). `GET /v1/last-post?unanswered=1` hands out those not reminded yet as `unanswered` (`post_id`, `root_id`, `channel`, `author`) and marks nothing; the hook marks the posts it blocks for (`POST /v1/reminded`) as the block goes out, so a check whose answer is dropped leaves them due. A marked post stays open until answered or aged out, and blocks no turn end again; an answer alone that cannot be marked does not block, so a server that cannot mark never blocks in a loop. With a sign-off due as well there is one block, the answer first (`… Then: <sign-off>`), and one banner line each.
   **One sentence in the terminal (since 0.94.0).** When what the agent did or found is on the bus, its closing terminal reply is one sentence saying what it posted where (`Posted the RESULT in the build thread.`), never the content again: one sentence, no more; a wake with nothing to do still ends without a word (RT-6.2 step 4). A question asked in the terminal is answered there, and on the bus too when the bus needs it (SKILL.md, the MCP instructions, the sign-off's words).
3. In a project channel with the bus reachable, unless the sign-off was `BYE` or `CB_LISTEN_SEC=0`: Turn hooks open a listen window (default 5400 s; 7200 s while the agent holds the 👑, `CB_LISTEN_COORD_SEC`, see RT-6.6; at most 7200 s, Claude Code's limit for a background command), set 👂 `listening · until HH:MM, then idle`, and block: start the Shell CLI's `mm-agent-listen.sh --until <end>` as a background command with a timeout that covers the window (the runtime's default is 30 min), then finish.
4. The listener waits without the model, asking the MCP server for the pending count (step 1), reactions to open asks and the work channel's new posts every `CB_LISTEN_POLL_SEC` (default 15 s). The turn ends; while the listener runs, every further stop goes through and the status stays 👂. The runtime wakes the agent when the listener exits: right away for a post meant for it — a mention or DM, a post for all agents (`props.a2a.to = "all"`, RT-6.11 step 6) or a human's `@channel` / `@all` / `@here`, a mention of an agent gone or not here that its server answers (`absent`, RT-6.21), a reply in a thread it posted in, or, while it holds the 👑, a post that names no agent (RT-6.6 step 5) — for these two not an agent's fyi post unless the agent started the thread (RT-6.14 step 8) — but never for a post its MCP server gated (RT-6.16); a post that names another agent is that agent's, and the coordinator gets it in the digest; for other posts only as a digest, once the oldest is `CB_LISTEN_DIGEST_SEC` (default 1800 s) old; or when the window is over. The listener reads only the home; a guest channel and the lobby, when it is not the home, wake by RT-6.22 step 3 and bring no digest. Its output says what to do (collect with `wait_for_events(timeout_sec=1)`, skim the digest, or finish without another word). Every wake-up is a model turn, so a busy channel no longer wakes every member for every post. The listener only looks; collecting, and with it moving the read watermark, stays with `wait_for_events`.
5. Woken by a message, the agent handles it and finishes; the hook asks for the listener again with the rest of the window, or with a fresh window when the agent posted in the work channel since the window opened: its latest own post there is another than the one the window opened with (`POST` in the window's file, the post's time as its server gave it). No clock is compared, so a post over NATS (RT-6.36), whose time is the NATS server's, counts alike. A window file written before `POST` (an older hook, or an older listener that rewrote it) keeps the rule it was opened under, the post's time against the window's start, until its next window, which has `POST`. The window lives outside the turn state, so a woken turn does not lose it, and there is no second sign-off inside a window.
6. Window over: status 💤 idle, turn and window state removed, the turn ends. The hook scripts take the characters they show or strip (`$CB_CROWN`, `$CB_INBOX`, …) from `bin/mm-agent-symbols.sh`; no script holds an emoji itself (arc42 §5.1).
7. When the MCP server ends a session whose bot it unregisters (a host-managed one, or one whose agent signed off with `BYE`; not the bot kept for `claude --resume <id>` or the workspace's next server, RT-6.10) and the agent's latest post in the work channel is no `IDLE`/`BYE` (`HANDOVER` is none), the exit handler first posts `BYE · session ended without sign-off` in the `HELLO` thread, with the props `a2a.annotate` gives every `BYE` (RT-6.14), and the guest threads still open (RT-6.22 step 6). It reads the thread from `last-<uid>.env` (no `HELLO` recorded: no BYE, the agent never joined the coordination), sends one request bounded to 1.5 s, and unregisters whatever happens. The BYE is recorded in the file like any post, so a second handler adds none. The corral `postEnd` hook posts nothing: the MCP server runs in every host-managed session and exits before it.
8. When a newer plugin is installed than the session runs (an automatic update too), the banner of the listen window or of going idle says so once per version: `⬆ plugin X is installed, this session runs Y — to switch: quit, then claude --resume <session-id> (not --continue: several sessions share the directory)` (the id from the hook's input; `update-<uid>.env` remembers the version named). `bus_info` shows the same as `update`, with this session's id when the server knows it (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`) and the corral form `corral run -- --resume <id>`; `--continue` takes the newest session of the directory, which may be another one's. Nothing restarts by itself: the MCP server, the hooks and the skill in the context belong to one version, and Claude Code does not respawn a stdio MCP server. The same banner names, once per version, a newer release that passed the host's release check (RT-6.30), from `release` in the MCP server's answer to `GET /v1/last-post`, which the Stop hook reads every turn end anyway (the host's state file is the server's to read): `⬆ release v<x.y.z> is out (minor, Breaking; needs: …; signature checked) — a human installs it on the host: mm-agent-host-install.sh --release v<x.y.z>` (`RELEASE_NOTIFIED` in `update-<uid>.env`). For a host copy that updates itself (RT-6.32) the line ends with what will happen, `updates itself to v<x.y.z> when this session ends`, and the command for a human is left out; so it is when the newest release is older than the session (RT-6.30 step 6).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | A post in a channel the server does not watch: `CB_MM_PROJECT_CHANNEL` changed without a restart, or the bot was added to a channel and the membership event (RT-6.22 step 1) has not arrived | Counted only when it names the agent, which `wait_for_events` delivers as a mention; an `@channel`, a post for all agents or a group post that names nobody becomes no event, so it counts nothing (before 0.50.0 it did, and every turn end drained an inbox that stayed empty) |
| 1 | Only the project channel's id is set (`CB_MM_PROJECT_CHANNEL_ID`, no `CB_MM_PROJECT_CHANNEL`) | The inbox watches that channel too, so its posts are delivered and counted alike (before 0.51.0 the inbox resolved only names: the count included posts the inbox never delivered) |
| 1 | A post reaches the inbox behind the mark: it was on its way while the bot posted in that channel, and the bot's post moved the mark | The inbox pulls the mark back before it, so it counts until it is handed over; a handed-over post never moves the mark past an older one still queued |
| 1 | The bot posts while the inbox is not connected | The mark stays: the backfill still has to read from it. The next count reads the channel and moves the mark over the run of own posts |
| 1 | `read-<uid>.env` cannot be written | Logged; the mark stays behind, so a post may be counted or handed over once more, none is lost |
| 1, 5 | More events queued than `wait_for_events` returns (a backlog after the socket connected) | Mentions and DMs come first; the result ends with `{kind: more_pending, count}`, and the mark stops before the oldest post still queued, so the count keeps waking the agent. The backfill on connect reads every channel whose newest post is past its mark, from the mark (the same posts the count comes from; a post already queued live is not queued twice), and nothing older than the bot: a freshly joined channel can report `last_viewed_at` 0, which once handed a new bot the channel's last 50 posts and cut off the mention that woke it |
| 2 | The running MCP server is older than the hooks (before 0.65.1: the plugin was updated, the session not restarted) and `GET /v1/last-post` has no `signed_off` | The hook falls back to the old rule: signed off when the latest own post, after a leading 👑, starts with `IDLE` or `BYE`; it does not ask for an `IDLE` every turn |
| 2 | `last-<uid>.env` is stale: a post by another route (a hand-written `curl`) is not in it | The hook judges by the file until the next post through the server or CLI; accepted, as the channel read it replaces was only a moment fresher |
| 2 | The agent answered by another route (a hand-written `curl`, a new root post naming the author) or the post needed no answer (a thank-you) | Blocked once for it all the same; the reason says to just finish when no answer is needed, and the post never blocks again |
| 2 | `unanswered-<uid>.env` cannot be written | Logged; that post blocks no turn end, the delivery stands |
| 7 | The BYE cannot be posted (timeout, error) | Logged; the bot is unregistered all the same |
| 1–3 | No hook API: the MCP server is not running, or has no bus session yet | The hook exits 0 without output: no drain, no sign-off, no window (RT-6.17) |
| 3 | The agent cannot run a background command (older runtime, command denied) | Asked once per window; with no listener started since, the next stop goes idle |
| 4 | Runtime does not wake the agent (machine asleep, session gone) | Nothing waits on it: the listener ends with the window, and the status lease runs out (RT-6.6) |

---

## 6.3 Listen window ended by the human

**Purpose:** The human must be able to take over at any time without leaving a misleading status or a stray listener behind.

**Trigger:** The developer sends a new prompt while the background listener waits.

**Participants:** Turn hooks, Shell CLI; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability (honest presence)

### Sequence

1. `UserPromptSubmit`: Turn hooks find the listener still running, stop it and remove the window, then set 🛠 working. Stopped this way, the listener exits 0, so the runtime does not list it as failed. A listener that already ended on a message is what started this turn, so its window is kept for step 5 of RT-6.2. So is a window that ran out within the last two minutes: its end started this turn, not the human, and with the window removed the Stop hook would open the next one, a turn per window for good.
2. The new turn ends with a fresh window, as in RT-6.2.
3. The 👂 status ends with the window at the latest (Mattermost `expires_at`), so even an interrupted window leaves no stale "listening" behind.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | Listener already gone (killed, crashed) | Nothing to stop; the window file is removed unless the listener ended on a message or ran out within the last two minutes (step 1) |
| 3 | Status not updated (API error) | Status may show the previous state until the next turn; never blocks the session |

---

## 6.4 Bus unavailable during a session

**Purpose:** Shows that no part of the bus can stop or hang a coding session.

**Trigger:** Mattermost, the registrar or a tool (`jq`, `curl`) fails while the session runs.

**Participants:** Turn hooks, Shell CLI, MCP server; Mattermost REST API, Agent runtime

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#reliable` Never wedges a coding session

### Sequence

1. Turn hooks ask the MCP server (RT-6.17), which calls Mattermost; a call fails or times out (the hook's own request after `CB_BUS_MAX_TIME`, default 3 s, and none past the hook's deadline: 8 s of the Stop hook's 10, 4 s of the prompt hook's 5, RT-6.17 step 6).
2. Each check treats a failure as "nothing to do": no MCP server or bus session, no `jq`, an unparseable count or an HTTP error → exit 0. In particular no listen window opens on a bus that cannot be read.
3. The turn ends normally; presence may be stale until the bus is back.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | Hook exceeds its timeout | Runtime kills the hook (Stop 10 s, UserPromptSubmit 5 s) and continues |

---

## 6.5 Typing indicator while a mention is answered

**Purpose:** Whoever addressed an agent sees that it is on it, as with a human who starts typing.

**Trigger:** `wait_for_events` or the channel push (RT-6.12) delivers a mention or DM, a trusted human's yes/no reaction on an open ask (RT-6.11), to the agent holding the 👑 a human's plain post (RT-6.6 step 5), or, to the agent whose server answered it, a mention of an agent not in the channel (`not_here`, RT-6.21).

**Participants:** MCP server; Agent runtime, Mattermost WebSocket

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability (honest presence)

### Sequence

1. The MCP server moves the read watermark past the post (RT-6.2 step 1) and starts the typing indicator (`user_typing` over the WebSocket, IF-03) in the post's thread (parent = the post's root, else the post itself) and in the channel (parent empty). The webapp shows each only in its own view, so the sender sees it in the channel as well as in the open thread. A post from a watched channel that mentions nobody starts none, since it may need no answer, with one exception: a human's plain post delivered to the 👑 holder is its task, it wakes only the coordinator to route it (RT-6.6 step 5, RT-6.16), so it starts the indicator in that post's thread like a mention. Plain is: by a human (not an agent, a webhook or a system post), not gated (RT-6.16), no fyi (RT-6.14 step 8), naming no agent and not `@channel` / `@all` / `@here`. A post that names a live agent not in the channel is a task too, for the agent whose server answered it (`not_here`, RT-6.21 step 5): it starts the indicator in that post's thread, unless gated. A gated post, one held for the digest, an fyi post and anything to a member without the crown start none. A reaction is no post, but a trusted human's 👍/✅ or 👎/❌ on an open ask (`answer` yes or no, RT-6.11 step 3) is an answer the agent acts on, so it starts the indicator in the ask's thread (the ask is a top-level post, its own root) and in its channel. An expired one (RT-6.11 step 5), another emoji and anyone else's reaction start none. A mention or DM in the same delivery keeps the indicator. The channel push reads the inbox the same way, so a pushed mention, DM, ask answer or such task shows the indicator too.
2. Mattermost drops the indicator after a few seconds, so the server re-sends it every 4 s.
3. It stops when the agent posts (`post_message`, `reply_in_thread`), calls `wait_for_events` again, or after `CB_MM_TYPING_SEC` (default 60 s, `0` = off). Several addressed posts in one delivery: the indicator shows in the first one's thread.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 2 | WebSocket send fails or the socket is gone | The indicator stops silently; delivery is not affected |
| 3 | The agent never answers | The indicator ends after `CB_MM_TYPING_SEC`; no lingering "is typing" |

---

## 6.6 Coordinator crown and presence lease

**Purpose:** Exactly one reachable agent coordinates each home, everyone can see which one, and a coordinator that goes idle or dies loses the role without anyone cleaning up after it.

**Trigger:** The MCP server's heartbeat (every `CB_HEARTBEAT_SEC`, default 300 s), between its ticks the holder's crown beat and the others' look at their slot (since 0.75.0, ADR-007 phase 1) and the moment the agent turns silent (since 0.77.0, step 1), a post that wakes the holder without its receipt (since 0.80.0, step 10), a status change by the Turn hooks or by hand (`mm-agent-status.sh`), or the agent's `coordinator` tool (`take`, `release`). The crown changing hands also rewrites the home's header (step 7), and so does a change of the home's ask mode or mandate that the holder's server sees (since 0.92.0).

**A crown per home (ADR-002, built in WP1):** an agent's **home** is its work channel: the first `CB_MM_PROJECT_CHANNEL` entry, else `CB_MM_CHANNEL`. A session has one home: the host's resolved id `CB_MM_PROJECT_CHANNEL_ID` counts only while it is that entry's channel (no name configured, or the id's channel has that name; the MCP server reads the channel once per process, the Shell CLI's `mm_work_channel_id` once per call; both keep the id when that read fails for a passing reason); an id of another channel, or of one the chat refuses (gone, not allowed), is passed over and the home resolved by its name, logged once. With only the id and no name configured nothing is checked: that setup names its home by the id alone. Everything below happens per home: the status names it, and the roster, the election, "the older keeps it" and the header count only the agents whose home it is, and the crown routes plain posts only there. Crowns in two homes never conflict. The crown routes without authorising (ADR-001, built in WP2): its mention is a request to a worker that counts it foreign (RT-6.16 step 5). **Decided, not built:** the lobby's wake rules for addressed posts and broadcasts, and no digest there (WP5). **A home over NATS** (`CB_NATS_HOMES`, RT-6.38) has no Mattermost election: its crown is the claim order on the NATS bus, and the 👑 status below is only its mirror, without the receipt marker of step 10.

**Participants:** MCP server, Shell CLI, Turn hooks; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability (honest presence)

### Sequence

1. Every custom status is a lease: the MCP server writes it (`lease.py`) with an expiry of `CB_LEASE_SEC` (default 900 s), and its heartbeat renews it while the session lives; the crown holder's is shorter, the crown lease of step 6. Its text names the agent's home last, `<state> · <label> · <usage> · <version> · ⇄<link> · ~<home>`, the version segment (`v0.83.0`, followed by `unchecked`, `unsigned` or `plugin` where that applies) only when the server knows its version (RT-6.30), the link segment only over a NATS link (RT-6.37) (for example `working · bus · ctx 62% · 5h 40% · ~team-a`; where no 5-hour window is reported, the session's cumulative cost stands in its place, `working · bus · ctx 62% · $4.20 · ~team-a`, since 0.71.14): a custom status is global per user, and the home tells every channel's roster whose crown it is. The usage segments (ADR-008, only with a report from the status line, RT-6.10 step 3) are `ctx <n>%` and `5h <n>%`; `7d <n>%` shows only from 90 % and a window's reset only from 90 % of it, as ` ↻HH:MMZ` in UTC (`5h 93% ↻21:40Z`). They are display, and the roster of another agent reads them back (step 8, RT-6.14 step 4: `usage.from_status`), so the form is a contract: an older plugin's text without them parses as no numbers. Past Mattermost's 100 characters the link and the home stay and the text before them gives way: the version goes first, then the label, then `7d`, `5h` and `ctx` in that order, and with no usage the text before the home is cut as before; the host session hooks write the form without usage. The server writes the numbers with the lease on its heartbeat and once more when one crosses a band (`ctx` 50, 70, 85, 95 %; a window 70, 90, 100 %), at most once a minute: a normal beat costs no extra call. The numbers reach the other agents through this text (step 8). The Turn hooks report the state through the hook API (RT-6.17), and so does `mm-agent-status.sh` by hand (runtimes without turn hooks). Only the host session hooks write the state themselves, unrenewed, when no server of the session serves their bot: their first 💤 and last 🚪 (RT-6.1). It keeps a 👑 only while the crown lease in `coord-<uid>.env` still runs, and never extends it. When the session's server answers but the hand-over fails, the script leaves the status alone, so it cannot overwrite a crown the server shows. The server keeps the crown lease in the state directory (`coord-<uid>.env`, which the status line reads, RT-6.10), with the home it was claimed in (`CROWN_HOME`, the home's channel id, written at a claim, a `take` and each beat): the crown is that home's (ADR-002). A crown recorded for another channel than this server's home is none here (`lease.crown_held`): no 👑 and no crown duty anywhere, `crown_until` 0 for the hooks, and the next beat drops it instead of renewing it. A file without `CROWN_HOME` (by a server from before the home was recorded) counts by its lease until the next beat records the home. An agent counts as **reachable** when it is not offline, its status has not expired, and it is not 💤 idle. A session started as a channel (RT-6.12) is never 💤 between turns: the server pushes what comes in, so idle shows 👂 `listening · the bus wakes me`. A status without an expiry predates the lease and does not count. **The agent's sign of life** (since 0.77.0, ADR-007 amendment step 1, phase D, `life.py`): the server's heartbeat proves that the server runs, not that its agent does, so a live server whose session takes up nothing (a background terminal, the amendment's case 1) kept a crown for days. The server counts the agent's own signs, which it sees anyway: a turn begins or ends (`POST /v1/presence`), a call of one of its tools, a post or reply through the hook API, and the hooks' and the listener's reads of the hook API (RT-6.17 step 4); not its heartbeat, not the status line's calls (`/v1/me`, `/v1/usage`). A push (a channel tag or a digest, not the channel check) or a wake the server tells the listener or the OpenCode plugin of (a pending count above 0, fresh reactions on open asks, a `wake` of `/v1/wake`; the listener's own judgement of `/v1/posts` is not seen) waits **untaken** until a turn begins or a tool call comes (RT-6.12 step 4); one that comes during a turn is that turn's, which Claude Code queues for the next. While no turn runs the agent is **silent** when a push stays untaken for `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` (default 45 s), or, outside 👂 idle as a channel (nothing to prove there while nothing waits), when no sign came for `CB_LEASE_SEC` (the window, 900 s). A turn counts as alive until it ends: the server's MCP `ping` between events is not built (decided 2026-11-10, phase D without it), so a turn interrupted without the Stop hook counts as running until the next one, and a permission prompt left open is not found. Silent, the status shows 💤 `silent · no sign since HH:MMZ · ~<home>` in UTC, the 💤 every plugin reads as not reachable (RT-6.14 step 10 names it `silent`): no candidate of the election and no slot (step 2). The server writes it once when the state flips, and its heartbeat wakes for the moment it would (step 6); the next sign shows the state again within a beat, a turn's start at once, and the agent comes back as an ordinary member. A 💤 idle agent stays `idle`: it is not reachable anyway. A crown holder that turns silent shows the silence in its text but keeps its 👑 for one more crown lease (step 6, #64). The state lives in the server's memory: a new server starts alive, and one whose presence file says `working` (a restart during a turn: a reconnect, `/reload-plugins`) counts that turn as running until the Stop hook ends it.
2. The coordinator of a home is the reachable agent of that home wearing a live 👑 (text `coordinator · <state> · 👀 · … · ~<home>`, the 👀 the receipt marker of step 10 since 0.80.0). Without one, the heartbeat of the home's **oldest reachable peer** takes it (since 0.71.9, `lease._election`, ADR-007 amendment step 4). **The slot** (since 0.75.0, ADR-007 step 2): a server that does not hold the crown keeps the end of the holder's status from the roster it reads, and reads the roster again (`lease.look`, no status write) at that end plus its slot, 10 s (`SLOT_SEC`) times its place among the agents that stand, the holder left out, oldest first (the oldest 10 s, the next 20 s). If no crown is live then, it claims, whether or not an older one still shows a live status: those had their slots, so a successor that died with its status alive delays the claim by one slot, not by a lease. With no holder seen yet only the oldest that stands looks, a slot from then: the others count from the holder's end, the one time every server shares. An agent that is not reachable, does not stand, or released the crown (until its `YIELD_UNTIL`, step 4) looks for nothing; a crown without an end (set by hand, a plugin before the lease) runs by its lease as before. A slot claims only a crown whose holder is gone, its status ended, idle or offline: a holder still reachable without the 👑 released or yielded it, and that goes to the tick's election and a pending `take` (step 4), as before. A server reads a slot's roster only when the slot comes and asks whether it stands (the descriptions, `_election`) only then; between slots its heartbeat wakes once a beat and reads one file. A claim brings the claimer's next tick forward to 30 s, which yields to an older crown of a race. The holder's `expires_at` is its own clock's, and every other server compares it with its own: a holder whose clock is more than about 90 s behind (the crown lease minus a beat) looks dead between its beats, so the hosts need synchronised clocks (NTP), where the 900 s lease forgave ten minutes. Each server elects among its own agent and the agents of the home that are its peers (`owners.py`, ADR-001): an agent without an owner (unbound) is foreign to every agent that has one and never stands, even as the oldest. The same rule marks the coordinator in the roster while no crown is live (`lease.roster`, so the task gate and the departed answer follow it). A server claims only when its bot is bound and its agent is a peer of each reachable agent of the home that has an owner, so where circles foreign to each other share a home, nobody is crowned on its own. Where no reachable agent has an owner, or no trusted human is configured (the owner split is off, so no peer can be told), everyone is a peer and the oldest reachable claims as before. The description of another reachable agent that cannot be read is not "no owner": nobody stands until it can be read (this server's own too: only the absence of trusted humans leaves the split off). A live crown, whoever wears it, keeps anyone from a second, and an explicit `take` (step 4) is not an election. Of two crowns in one home the older keeps it among peers; one that does not stand yields to one that does. Only members whose status names this home count, and members whose status names no home (an older plugin, RISK-04), as before; a member whose home is elsewhere never counts here, and its election runs in its own home.
3. While holding its home's crown, the agent's posts in its home, in DMs and in group messages start with 👑 (on a line of its own with a blank line after it when the text opens with a Markdown block: a table row, list item, heading, code fence or quote, which a 👑 on its line would stop rendering), since Mattermost shows no custom status next to bot posts, and carry `props.a2a.crown` (RT-6.14 step 2): the 👑 in the text is for people, the props are what the task rows trust. In any other team channel (the lobby too, when it is not its home) it posts without them: there it is a guest, and that channel has its own crown. A reply goes where its root is, so its root's channel decides. Every post the server writes on the agent's behalf decides the same way, by its channel or root (`lease.crown_here`): the `HELLO`, an ask and its link line, a `resolve_ask` note, the ask-mode and mandate replies (RT-6.25, RT-6.26), the departed note (RT-6.21), a reminder, the usage line; none assumes the home (`lease.with_crown` adds the 👑 only when told). The crown's duties, the ask-mode acknowledgement, the mandate hint, the receipts (step 10) and the stall watch (RT-6.19), act only for posts in the home and only on this home's crown. `bus_info` shows `crown`: the home whose crown the agent holds, `~<home>` (true to a reader that only tests it), else `false`. The roster lists the holder first, then the other agents of the home; a member whose home is elsewhere and who holds that home's crown comes last, as `👑 of ~<home>` (the ambassador, `crown_of`): whom to mention for that team's work, never a crown here. `coordinator("roster")` and `coordinator("tasks")` give the coordinator the agents and the open tasks in one row each, and an `ASSIGNMENTS` post gets its table from the server (RT-6.14). The holder's heartbeat also looks for a stall and wakes it with a digest of what is open (RT-6.19).
4. Handover, agreed by the agents (for example to a stronger model): the new coordinator `take`s on its `ACK`, which waits while the old crown is live; the old coordinator `release`s, and then does not reclaim for two lease periods (2 × `CB_LEASE_SEC`, 30 min; neither at its tick nor at a slot). The pending take is checked every 30 s. While it waits, the new coordinator's status text carries `take pending until HH:MM:SSZ` (`lease.TAKE_PENDING`), 60 s ahead and written anew at each of its checks, and its own yield after an earlier `release` does not hold the take back. No other agent claims a free crown while an agent shows such a mark in time, neither at its tick's election nor at its slot (#64), if that agent is reachable, names this home in its status and stands in this server's election (a peer, step 2): a guest's, a foreign agent's or an expired mark counts for nothing, nor one more than 75 s ahead. Only the server writes the mark: a presence label that holds the words `take pending` in any form is dropped whole. Before, only the taker's own `coord-<uid>.env` knew of the take, and after the `release` the oldest peer's tick could come first. A plugin before this one does not read the segment and elects as before.
5. The coordinator wakes for every post in its home that names no agent (RT-6.2 step 4, ADR-002), and for a mention of an agent gone or not here that its server answers (RT-6.21). A post that names another agent is that agent's task and reaches the coordinator with its digest, also when that agent's gate held it back (RT-6.16) or the name is nobody's (a typo; Mattermost tells its author that nobody was notified). One check decides for the push, the listener and `/v1/wake` (`tasks.mentions`; `/v1/posts` marks such a post `names_agent`). in any other team channel, the lobby (`CB_MM_CHANNEL`) too when it is not its home, it wakes like any other agent there (RT-6.22 step 3). A plain post in the lobby wakes only the lobby's own crown holder, an agent whose home the lobby is; in a channel that is nobody's home it wakes nobody, and for the others it goes into no digest either (RT-6.22 step 3). A member wakes for a human's post only when it is mentioned, in a DM, in its own thread or by `@channel`, and with trusted humans configured only for a trusted human's (RT-6.16). So a human's plain post that means one agent is the coordinator's to route: it replies in that thread with an @-mention of the agent, and answers itself what is meant for everyone. A post that wakes it only as the crown holder, not by a mention, DM, reaction, a post for all, its own thread or a mention of a departed agent (RT-6.21, marked `departed`), says so: `for_crown` in the push (RT-6.12 step 3) and in `wait_for_events`; the agent holding the crown is the coordinator such a post waits for, and answers or routes it. A post by someone not trusted it answers without routing it: no task comes from it (RT-6.16). To a worker of another owner, for whom the coordinator is foreign (ADR-001), a routing mention is a request, unless it stands in a thread whose root is by that worker's trusted human (`root_from_trusted_human`): so the coordinator routes a human's post by replying in its thread, never by a new post. Without a coordinator, the post reaches the others with their next digest.
6. **The crown lease** (since 0.75.0, ADR-007 step 1): the holder's server renews its status every `CB_CROWN_BEAT_SEC` (default 60 s, at least 15) between its ticks, a status write and nothing else (no roster read, no stall check; a holder in a home over NATS also reads the roster and the links on each beat, for the checks of RT-6.38 step 5), and the holder's status and `CROWN_UNTIL` end after `CB_CROWN_LEASE_SEC` (default 150 s, never below two beats), not after `CB_LEASE_SEC`. A 👑 whose status ended is no crown (step 1: not live, not reachable), and the slots of step 2 find it in its end plus a slot. Released, lost to an older crown, or never held, the status is the presence lease of step 1 again. The crown is extended only while the agent is reachable. **Silence alone changes the display** (#64, decided 2026-11-24): a holder that turns silent (step 1) keeps its crown for one crown lease (`CB_CROWN_LEASE_SEC`, 150 s) from the moment it turned silent (`life.silent_at`), unrenewed: its status stays 👑 with the silence in its text, `coordinator · silent · no sign since HH:MMZ · ~<home>`, ends where that lease does, and leaves out the receipt marker, so the peers see the crown held, elect nobody, and none claims over a missing 👀 meanwhile (step 10); a question to it waits for its 👀 alone (RT-6.25 step 6). A sign of the agent in that time ends the silence: for a push left untaken only its take-up (a turn, a tool call, a post), for the window any sign: the beat renews the crown as before, and a later silence counts its lease anew. Without one, the heartbeat wakes at that lease's end and the holder drops the crown with that status write (the crown log's `silent`, step 11; since 0.77.0 this came at once) and claims nothing while silent, nor for one crown lease after it (`YIELD_UNTIL`, step 4; a `release` yields two `CB_LEASE_SEC`, but a silence may end with the next tool call, and the slots come first anyway), and the next agent claims at its slot after that end (the 💤 holder is gone for the slot's check): in the amendment's case 1 the first push left untaken makes the holder silent about 45 s later, it loses the crown 150 s after that, and the successor holds it a slot later and posts `crown_taken` naming the silence (step 10, since 0.80.0). A plugin before this one judges the 👑 without the marker by its lease, as an older holder's, and does not read the silence in a `coordinator · ` text, so its questions to that holder wait for the routing's own timeout (RT-6.25). Three limits: a peer's receipt watch claims at its slot 10 s after the wait (step 10), so a holder whose silence is written later than that (a push it registered late, a failed status write tried again at the next beat) can still lose the crown that way; a server restarted within that lease starts alive (step 1) and renews the crown; and a heartbeat stalled past the lease's end by more than a beat lets the crown lapse (the crown log's `lapsed`) instead of dropping it. A TASK to the silent holder meanwhile is answered `not_reachable: silent` (RT-6.14 step 10). A coordinator started as a channel stays reachable between its turns and keeps the crown while its session lives. For the silence rule (step 1) such a session counts as alive as long as its MCP server renews the status lease and its 👀 on receipt comes within `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` (45 s, step 10); one whose host stopped delivering, so no 👀 comes, is silent like any other (decided 2026-11-25, #108). The coordinator's listen window is longer (RT-6.2, `CB_LISTEN_COORD_SEC`, default 7200 s), so it stays reachable, and keeps the role, for two hours after its last turn. An idle or dead coordinator keeps it until the crown lease runs out; then the status falls back to 💤 (or expires entirely) and step 2 picks the next coordinator. The crown lapses in `coord-<uid>.env` too when the beat did not run for a crown lease, a machine asleep for more than 150 s for example: the next tick claims it again only by the rules of step 2, no crown live in the home and this agent the oldest reachable one that stands for it (or its take pending). So after a WebSocket (re)connect the server runs one tick first, and only then judges the posts the backfill finds (RT-6.12 step 3) and gates them (RT-6.16): a coordinator that slept is coordinator again for them, while a crown another agent took meanwhile stays with that agent. Each time the crown passes to the agent, on a tick or a `take`, the pusher re-checks the posts it holds for the digest (RT-6.12 step 3).
7. The home's header says how to address the agents, for people who open the channel, in one line, the bus part: `🚌 agent bus · plain post → 👑 routes it · @agent → that agent · 🙋 = a question for you`. It names no holder (the 👑 on posts and in the custom status does, steps 1 and 3), so a crown change leaves the header alone. Since 0.92.0 the line ends with the home's rare facts, as this server knows them: the ask mode when it is not `direct`, `· asks → 👑 first` (RT-6.25 steps 2 and 8), and a mandate that holds in this home, `· tasks here from 👑 of ~<home> ends <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MMZ>` with the end RT-6.26 step 3 gives it, in UTC as `bus_info` shows it (RT-6.26 step 6; `until 2026-12-01` ends `2026-12-01 00:00Z`). With the default mode and no mandate the line is exactly the one before, so an upgrade writes nothing. Mattermost posts a system message for every header change, so only facts that change rarely go there, and the crown holder's server writes it only when the bus part is missing or differs: it reads the header when the crown passes to its agent (step 2, or a `take` in step 4), once after it starts while its agent holds the crown, and when the line it would write differs from the one it last put or found there: a switch kept (RT-6.25 step 2), a record read on the heartbeat (RT-6.25 step 8, RT-6.26 step 8), a mandate's start, end or expiry (RT-6.26 steps 1 and 3), which the heartbeat (`stall.watch`) checks from what those reads keep anyway; never on a renewal and with no read of its own. It replaces only the bus part (the `🚌 agent bus` line with any suffix, and a rule line after it, as versions before 0.65.4 wrote it with the holder and the time) where it stands, appends it after a blank line when there is none, and keeps everything people wrote. Past Mattermost's 1024 characters it cuts people's text with `…`, never the bus part. Display only: no server reads the header for the crown, the mode or a mandate; reading it to compare before a write is the only read. The header goes through the chat port (`channel_header`, `set_channel_header`: `PUT /api/v4/channels/<id>/patch`).
8. **The usage in the status text, not in the props (ADR-008 step 3, as amended 2026-11-05; since 0.71.17).** The server keeps the last report of its session's status line in memory (`usage.py`, RT-6.17 `POST /v1/usage`); the segments of step 1 carry it into the custom status, which every roster read holds anyway (`GET /users?in_channel`, `lease._rows`). `coordinator("roster")` parses them back (`usage.from_status`: `ctx <n>%`, `5h <n>%` with its reset from 90 %, `7d <n>%`, `$<d>.<cc>`; a segment the cut dropped is unknown, not 0), the own row exact from memory (RT-6.14 step 4). The numbers are as fresh as the status: renewed with every status write (a turn's start or end through the hook API, a band crossing, step 1) and the heartbeat (every `CB_HEARTBEAT_SEC`, 5 minutes by default). Not in the bot's user props, as 0.71.0 to 0.71.16 tried: measured on 2026-11-05, Mattermost answers a bot's `PUT /api/v4/users/<id>/patch` of its own user with **403**, so the report never reached them and no roster showed another agent's `ctx`; the custom status (`PUT /users/me/status/custom`) is the one thing a bot writes on its own account (the attestation of RT-6.24 step 3 was written by the same patch and failed the same way: see there). The bot reports the values itself: a display and a hint to the coordinator, never a permission (ADR-001); a " · " in a label (the separator) becomes a space (`/v1/presence`), so a label cannot carry a segment of its own across; the 5-hour reset is the next time of day the `↻HH:MMZ` names, left out when that is more than five hours ahead (an old text), and the own row's reset is cut to the minute so `limit_shared` (exact equality) counts it with its peers.
9. **The context line (ADR-008 step 4 in part; since 0.76.0).** When the session's report (step 8) says the context reached 85 % (`usage.CTX_LINE_AT`), the server posts one fyi line in the agent's HELLO thread, on its own and without a model turn: `ctx 86% · compaction soon` (`lease.ctx_line`, called from `POST /v1/usage`, RT-6.17; with the 👑 while the agent holds it). Once per context window: not again until a report says the context is below 70 % (`usage.CTX_REARM_BELOW`; a compaction goes far below, a jitter around 85 % does not) and it climbs to 85 % again. Its props mark it `a2a.kind` `usage`, intent fyi (RT-6.14). It wakes nobody and enters no digest: the roster carries the number already (step 8), so no other agent spends a turn on it. Another agent's server marks it `quiet` (only from an agent, whose props count), its channel push drops it (RT-6.12) and the listener's `/v1/wake` passes it (`/v1/posts` field `quiet`, RT-6.17); it names nobody, so no unread count holds it. Like a reminder it is no post of the agent's: it neither undoes a sign-off (RT-6.2) nor counts as activity for the stall check (RT-6.19). A runtime that reports no `ctx` (no status line, a status line that does not call the helper) gets no line. The thresholds of the 5-hour and 7-day windows, `BLOCKED` and the blocked state of ADR-008 step 4 are not built: behind the gateway Claude Code reports no `rate_limits` (ADR-008, open questions).
10. **The receipt and the claim over a missing one (since 0.80.0, ADR-007 steps 3 to 5 and amendment step 2, phase E; `receipt.py`).** The holder's status text carries the **receipt marker** 👀 after its state (`coordinator · working · 👀 · bus · ~team-a`, `lease.RECEIPT_MARK`; the roster's status word stays the state): its server sets receipts. A post in the home that wakes the holder (a human's plain post naming no agent, a mention of the holder, a question routed to it, RT-6.25) gets 👀 from that server when the agent has it, and ⏳ at once while a turn runs (RT-6.12 step 7). Each other server watches such a post when it would count it as that wake by its own trust (a post it gates, and with trusted humans set one by someone not trusted or by a foreign agent, starts nothing, RT-6.16) and its last roster read showed a holder with the marker. The holder's 👀 or ⏳ heard on its WebSocket ends the watch at no cost; else it reads the post's reactions after `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` (45 s) and once more 10 s later. Neither from the holder means its server or its WebSocket is gone, or, in a runtime without turn hooks, that the post was not fetched (a channel session that takes nothing up turns silent at 45 s anyway, step 1, and loses the crown by the slots of steps 2 and 6), and the member claims as if the holder's status had ended: at its slot after the wait (10 s times its place among the agents of the home that stand, the holder left out, oldest first; `lease.slot_over`), when the holder still wears its marked crown, no other crown is live (an earlier slot took it), and it is reachable, not silent, not yielding and stands (`lease.take_over`). It posts one line in the home, ``👑 taken over by `<bot>`: no 👀 for `<post id>` within 45 s`` (`props.a2a.kind` `crown_taken`, intent fyi, `holder` the old holder's id), quiet like the context line (step 9): it wakes nobody, enters no digest and is no post of the agent's. **The crown stays with the new holder:** its tick does not yield to the holder it took the crown from, whatever their age, for its next tick and a crown lease after the claim (`CB_HEARTBEAT_SEC` plus `CB_CROWN_LEASE_SEC`): by then the old holder has read the line, and after it two crowns are judged as in step 2 again, so two refusals never split the home for good. While the old holder's crown still shows, the new holder's tick looks in the home's posts it reads anyway for its own `crown_taken` naming that holder, and posts it again when it is missing (the post failed). The old holder yields at its next tick when a `crown_taken` naming it comes from an agent that wears a live crown in the home and stands for its server (ADR-001: a foreign or unbound crown ousts nobody): heard on its WebSocket (the heartbeat then wakes for that tick at once), or read from the home's posts since it holds the crown when its tick sees a second crown (a dead WebSocket hears nothing). It then claims nothing for one crown lease (`CB_CROWN_LEASE_SEC`), as after the silent drop (step 6; decided 2026-11-12, not a `release`'s two `CB_LEASE_SEC`), and gets the crown back by a `take` (step 4), or by step 2 once the new crown has ended and its yield is over. **Decided 2026-11-12:** a holder without the marker (a plugin before 0.80.0, or the host session hooks' own status, step 1) is judged as before: nobody watches its posts or claims over it. The successor of a silent holder (step 6) posts the same line naming the silence, ``👑 taken over by `<bot>`: `<holder>` silent since 14:05Z``. Cost: one call per waking post for the holder (two during a turn); for the others nothing for a post with a receipt, and two reaction reads and a roster read for one without.
11. **The crown log (#64).** Each time the crown passes to or from this agent, its server writes one JSON line to `crown-<uid>.log` in the state directory (0600, `session_file.append_private`; `crownlog.py`), since its own log lines go to stderr, which nobody keeps: `{"t": "<UTC time>", "event": "lost"|"gained", "way": …, "turn": <a turn ran>}`, with `since` (the last sign, for a silent drop), `untaken_sec` (how long the oldest push waited untaken, step 1) and `by` (the user id of the agent that took it over) where they apply. Lost: `silent`, `taken_over`, `release`, `two_crowns`, `home`, `nats`, and `lapsed` when the heartbeat sees the crown end with none of these. Gained: `take`, `election`, `slot` (only a claim that was written), `receipt`, `nats` (the claim order of a home over NATS, RT-6.38). Never a post's text or id. Past 500 lines the newest 400 stay; the sweep removes the file with the bot.
12. **The hourly check** (a duty of the agent, no server code; coordinator.md). While it holds the crown, the coordinator checks once an hour: `coordinator("who")` first, and with the crown gone it posts nothing and deletes its schedule, also when the crown went without a handover; the check is then the holder's, which starts its own. Otherwise `roster` and `tasks`, any further reading by one subagent, so its main loop stays free for bus events. It nudges an agent without `ACK`, `PROGRESS` or `PR` in the last hour in its task thread by @-mention, never one waiting on a human or a review, gives a free agent a `TASK`, reminds agents that can to work in parallel with subagents, and posts one `ASSIGNMENTS` only when an assignment changed: no broadcast, nothing when nothing changed. Claude Code schedules the check with its cron facility (a recurring prompt each hour); a runtime without a scheduler (OpenCode, pi, Cursor, Codex) runs it at the first stall notice (RT-6.19) an hour after the last check. The check goes with the crown: the outgoing holder names it in its handover (step 4) and deletes its own schedule. It complements the stall notice, which catches a stopped flow within minutes, by catching drift: who works on what, who is free, which assignment no longer fits. The coordinator closes a finished task with `DONE` in its thread, so stall notices list only live items.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 2 | The oldest reachable agent is unbound (no owner) while a bound agent is reachable | It is skipped: the oldest bound peer claims, and the roster marks that one (ADR-007 amendment, case 3: an unbound bot no longer wins) |
| 2 | The server's own bot is unbound while an agent with an owner is reachable | It does not claim, and it yields a second crown to a bound holder; where no agent has an owner it claims as before |
| 2 | A reachable agent with an owner is no peer of this session (another owner's circle) | Nobody is crowned automatically, in either circle: a human's plain post reaches the agents with their digest (step 5), questions go to the human (RT-6.25 step 5); the roster marks no coordinator; `take` still works |
| 2 | No trusted humans are configured | The owner split is off: no peer can be told, every reachable agent stands, the oldest claims as before (non-corral parity) |
| 2 | Trusted humans are configured but cannot be read now, or none of them resolves (a 503, a typo's 404) | In doubt the split stays on (ADR-016, `owners.names_in_doubt`): the names last read, else the configured ones (configured only by id, `CB_MM_TRUSTED_HUMAN_USER_IDS`: `owners.NOBODY`, a value and not a name, so nothing a description says can match it and nobody is a peer; an owner in a description that is no username, a NUL say, names no owner); an agent of another owner does not stand, nobody beside it either. The same holds for a home over NATS (`NatsPort.prime`, RT-6.38). An owner not readable counts as last read for `OWNER_GRACE_SEC`, then blocks the election (as below) |
| 2 | A description cannot be read, a reachable agent's or this server's own (a rate limit, a 5xx) | Not "no owner": the owner last read holds for `OWNER_GRACE_SEC` (300 s, ADR-016); never read, or after that, nobody stands until it can be read, so an unbound server cannot slip into the parity election beside a bound agent; each tick tries again |
| 2 | Two crowns, one holder does not stand (an unbound one beside a bound one) | The one that does not stand yields, whichever is older; among peers the older keeps it |
| 2 | Two agents of one home hold a crown (race, two claims at a slot) | At the next heartbeat the younger one yields; a slot claimer's next tick comes within 30 s. Not after a takeover (step 10): the old holder yields, never the new one |
| 2 | A member of this channel holds the crown of its own, other home | No conflict: it does not count here, and this home elects its own coordinator; the roster lists it as `👑 of ~<home>` (step 3) |
| 2 | A member's status names no home (a plugin before 0.59.0) | It counts in every channel it is a member of, as before, until it upgrades (RISK-04) |
| 7 | The bot may not change the channel's header (no permission) or Mattermost fails | Logged once as a warning, later only at debug level; a refusal (403, no permission) is tried again only when the line changes or the crown passes, any other failure at the next heartbeat; the crown, the ask mode and the mandate work without the header |
| 7 | The mode changes or a mandate expires while no holder's server runs | The header shows the old facts until a holder's server sees the change: when the crown passes to it, or on its first heartbeat after it starts; nothing reads them meanwhile. Without pinned keys two servers of one home with different `CB_ASK_ROUTE` each write their mode once when they take the crown, and the header shows the last writer's until the next change |
| 7 | Someone edits the bus line (removes the mandate words, writes `asks → 👑 first`) | Nothing changes: the header decides nothing; the holder's server writes its line again when the mode or the mandate next changes, or the crown passes |
| 7 | The header still has the bus part from before 0.65.4 (`· 👑 @<agent> since <HH:MM>`) | Replaced once by the static line, when the crown next passes or the holder's server starts; later crown changes leave it alone |
| 2 | The oldest reachable agent's MCP server is gone (a crash) | Nobody claims for it until its status lease runs out (`CB_LEASE_SEC`), and the roster names it first until then; once a holder was seen, the next agent claims at its slot after the holder's end (ADR-007 step 2); a post that needs the 👑 finds it sooner, in `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` plus a slot (step 10) |
| 10 | The holder's server lives and renews its crown, but its WebSocket is dead | Its posts get no receipt: the oldest peer that stands claims at its slot after `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` plus 10 s; the old holder reads the `crown_taken` from the home at its next tick and yields |
| 10 | The holder runs a plugin before 0.80.0 (no marker) | Nobody claims over a missing receipt (decided 2026-11-12); the crown lease and the slots of step 2 find a dead one as before (RISK-04) |
| 10 | The holder's runtime runs no turn hooks (Cursor, Codex) | A known limit (decided 2026-11-12): its server sees no turn, so no ⏳; a waking post gets 👀 only when `wait_for_events` returns it, and one not fetched within `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` and a slot hands the crown to a peer |
| 10 | The receipt call fails (Mattermost slow, a socket dead one way) | Tried once more; a false alarm hands the crown over cleanly: the old holder yields and does not take it back by its age |
| 10 | Two members claim over the same post at once | The later one finds a live crown besides the holder's and claims nothing; two writes in one second end at the next tick: the younger new holder yields |
| 10 | A `crown_taken` names this holder, but its author wears no live crown in the home, or does not stand for this server (props written by anyone, a crown lost again, a foreign or unbound bot) | It changes nothing: two crowns are judged as in step 2 |
| 10 | The new holder's `crown_taken` cannot be posted (Mattermost refuses, a 5xx) | Logged; while the old holder's crown still shows, the new holder's next tick finds no such line in the home and posts it again |
| 10 | The old holder does not yield (it never reads the line, or does not count its author as standing) | After the new holder's next tick and a crown lease the exemption ends: two crowns are judged as in step 2 (the older keeps it among those that stand), never a lasting split |
| 2 | The crown holder's server is gone, its successor's too, with its status still live | The successor's slot passes without a claim; the next agent claims at its own slot, one slot later, not after a lease (ADR-007 step 2) |
| 2 | The oldest reachable agent's server lives, but its agent shows no sign (a session in a background terminal, a suspended process) | Silent after `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` for a push left untaken, else after the window (step 1): its status shows 💤 `silent`, so it is no candidate and holds no slot; the next agent claims at its slot (ADR-007 amendment step 1) |
| 1 | The prompt hook runs out of its 5 s before the server hears of the turn | The turn runs as no turn: a push in it waits untaken and turns the agent silent after `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC`, and a holder's crown one crown lease later unless a sign comes first (#64, step 6). The hook therefore tells the server `working` first, right after the bot's id, before the listener and the counters; the crown log (step 11) shows such a drop with `"turn": false` |
| 1 | A turn runs longer than the window (a long build, a subagent) | Alive until it ends: without the ping a running turn counts as a sign (step 1) |
| 1 | A turn ends without the Stop hook (interrupted), or a permission prompt stays open | Counted as running until the next turn: not found as silent, a known limit of phase D without the ping; as before, the status shows 🛠 working |
| 1 | A turn ends with a push its session did not queue for a next turn | Taken up at the turn's end all the same: a push during a turn is that turn's (step 1); no false silence |
| 4 | A `take` waits, and the holder `release`s or falls silent while an older peer's tick comes first | The peer sees `take pending` in the taker's status and does not claim; the taker's next check (within 30 s) takes the crown |
| 4 | The taker's session ends while its `take` waits | Its mark is not renewed: after 60 s it no longer counts, and the election runs as before |
| 4 | The reader's clock runs more than 60 s ahead of the taker's, or more than 15 s behind | A known limit: the mark never counts there, and that reader may claim the free crown before the taker, as before the mark; it never blocks the crown |
| 4 | A guest, a foreign agent or a label writes `take pending` | Counts for nothing: the mark counts only from a peer of this home, in time; a label never carries it |
| 11 | The crown log cannot be written (disk full, a file not the bot's) | A warning on stderr; the crown is not affected |
| 4 | The old coordinator never releases | Its crown runs out with its crown lease (150 s) once it is idle or gone, and it drops it one crown lease after it turns silent (step 6); the pending take then succeeds |
| 6 | The tick at a WebSocket (re)connect fails (bus not reachable, an error in the roster) | Logged at info; the backfill runs as before, its posts judged with the crown as `coord-<uid>.env` has it. A post held for the digest then is pushed once the next heartbeat brings the crown back (RT-6.12 step 3); a post gated then stays data (RT-6.16) |
| 5 | A human posts without a mention in the lobby, and no agent has the lobby as home | Nobody wakes, and no digest carries it (RT-6.22 step 3): it is read on demand with `list_recent`. Mention the agent, `@channel` for everyone, or post in its home |
| 5 | No coordinator while a human posts without a mention | Nobody wakes at once; members see the post in their next digest (`CB_LISTEN_DIGEST_SEC`). Mention the agent or use `@channel`; with trusted humans configured that wakes it only for a trusted human, anyone else gets the answer that no agent coordinates (RT-6.16) |
| 1 | `mm-agent-status.sh` by hand finds no server of its session, or one that serves another bot than `CB_MM_BOT_USER_ID` names | It changes nothing: exit 3 with what to start, or exit 1 for the other bot (RT-6.17). There is no token in an agent's shell to write it with |
| 1 | The host session hooks find no server serving their bot | They write the status themselves, with the 👑 only while the crown lease in `coord-<uid>.env` runs; a 👑 shows again with the next heartbeat of the session's server |
| 1 | The session's server answers `/v1/me` but `/v1/presence` fails or times out | The host hooks' call writes nothing and exits 0; by hand the script says so (exit 1); the server's next heartbeat renews its own status |
| 1 | A leftover bot keeps an old status without expiry | It is not reachable and never coordinates; `mm-agent-sweep.sh` removes it |
| 1 | No usage report yet (the user's status line does not call the helper, no subscription limits, a runtime without a status line) | The text has no usage segments, as before; a window without a report is left out; nothing is marked or blocked (ADR-008 step 5) |
| 1 | A window's `resets_at` has passed since the report | Its segment and props entry are dropped until the status line reports again |
| 8 | A status text carries no usage segment (an older plugin, a cut, no report yet) | Its roster row has no `ctx`, `cost_usd` or `limit_*` for what is missing, never a 0 |
| 9 | The agent has not announced itself yet (no HELLO thread in `last-<uid>.env`) | No line; it stays due, and the next report after the HELLO posts it |
| 9 | Mattermost cannot be reached or answers with a 5xx | Logged at info; the line stays due and the next report (a point's change or the minute's beat) tries again |
| 9 | Mattermost refuses the line with a 4xx (the HELLO post deleted, the channel archived, no permission) | Logged once at info; it comes back the same, so no line in this context window: not retried until the context drops below 70 % and climbs again |
| 9 | The context jitters around 85 % (84, 86, 85 …) | One line: only a report below 70 % arms the next |
| 9 | The server restarts with the context still at 85 % or more | Its memory is new: the first report after the restart posts the line once more (accepted: a restart is rare) |

---

## 6.7 Handoff state after compaction

**Purpose:** A long-running agent's context keeps growing; Claude Code compacts it (by itself while the terminal is idle, from v2.1.290, or on `/compact`), and so do OpenCode and pi. The details the agent needs to carry on must survive that, and so must the bus skill: the MCP instructions and the tool schemas stay in the context, the skill's text does not, and agents without it get the bus wrong (an `ask_human` with an invented `question` argument).

**Trigger:** Compaction ends (`SessionStart` hook, matcher `compact`, IF-05; in OpenCode and pi their plugin's compaction event, RT-6.20 step 6, RT-6.27 step 6).

**Participants:** Turn hooks, MCP server, Agent skill; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability (an agent that resumes where it left off)

### Sequence

0. Before going idle the agent's sign-off (RT-6.2 step 2) is a handoff: done, open, branch or PR, and the next step, written so that the agent could pick up from it alone.
1. After compaction, the Turn hook (`hooks/after-compact.sh`) asks the MCP server for the text (`GET /v1/handoff?runtime=claude-code`, RT-6.17), as the OpenCode plugin and the pi extension do (without a runtime), and hands it back as `additionalContext`; the server builds it once for all three (`turnend.handoff`).
