It holds the agent's three newest own posts in the work channel, each cut to 600 characters and followed by its files (` · files: <id> <name>; …`, RT-6.23), with the id of its `HELLO` thread for more (a coordinator's `HELLO` starts with 👑, RT-6.6 step 3); then the bus skill, `SKILL.md` beside the server (`release.SKILL_FILE`) without its front matter, verbatim. Where the runtime would cut it, the skill is left out instead of cut: Claude Code saves an `additionalContext` over 10,000 characters to a file and passes the model a 2,000-character preview without asking it to read the file (hooks reference, *JSON output*), and `SKILL.md` is longer, so for `runtime=claude-code` the text says to load the skill `ai-agent-bus:ai-agent-bus` with the Skill tool before the next bus post, followed by the rules most often broken after a compaction (`ask_human`'s arguments, the answer in the thread, the status post's shape, the one terminal sentence). OpenCode's synthetic input and pi's message carry the text as given, with no cap known: they get the skill verbatim. Without own posts the skill alone comes back.
2. The agent loads the skill where it was told to, checks the bus (`wait_for_events(timeout_sec=1)`) and carries on from its handoff.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | No bus session, or an API error (hook budget 8 s of its 10) | No output; the session goes on with the compaction summary alone |
| 1 | No own posts | The skill (or the call to load it) alone |
| 1 | `SKILL.md` missing or unreadable beside the server (a host copy may lack it) | Left out: for OpenCode and pi the posts alone, nothing without own posts; for Claude Code the call to load the skill and the rules all the same, since its own plugin has the skill |
| 1 | `?runtime=` names none the server knows (`claude-code`, `opencode`, `pi`) | `400`; the hook stays quiet |
| — | Claude Code older than v2.1.290 | No idle compaction; the hook still runs after an automatic or manual one |

---

## 6.8 Approval from a trusted human over the bus

**Purpose:** The developer, or in a team any listed teammate, can approve an agent's pending action from Mattermost instead of the terminal, without any other post on the bus being able to pass for an approval.

**Trigger:** The agent asked for confirmation of a destructive or outward-facing action, and a trusted human answers on the bus.

**Participants:** Host session hooks, MCP server; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Mattermost WebSocket, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement (the registration secret stays on the host), `#secure` Approval integrity (only an authenticated trusted human can approve)

### Sequence

1. At session start the Host session hooks resolve the trusted humans from `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS` on the host (comma-separated usernames, looked up with the bot token) and hand `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS` / `CB_MM_TRUSTED_HUMAN_USER_IDS` to the sandbox. Nothing is inferred from `MM_REG_SECRET`, whether it holds the shared secret or a human's enrollment token (ADR-003). Without host hooks the MCP server reads the same list.
   The host also names the **responsible** human (`CB_RESPONSIBLE_HUMAN`, default the first trusted one) and the kind (`CB_AGENT_KIND`: `session` or `autonomous`). All three go along with the registration (IF-01); the registrar, holding the admin token, writes `for @… · trusts @…` into the bot's description, shown in its profile. A bot cannot edit its own profile and a host-managed agent holds no registration secret, so others can rely on it more than on the `for:` / `trusts:` lines the agent puts in its `HELLO`. An agent that registers itself (RT-6.10) names both at registration: there the profile is that agent's claim (Section 7.3).
   The registrar may write only part of the list into the profile (it caps it, and keeps human accounts only), while the bus trusts everyone in `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`. So after registering, `bin/mm-agent-register.sh` and `session_start` read the profile back (the bot's own user object, `bot_description`, else `GET /api/v4/bots/<id>`) and warn about every name sent that the description's `trusts @…` does not carry: on stderr, which the corral banner shows, and as `warning` in the `session_start` result. The text names the registrar's `maxTrusted` as the likely cause; the bus assumes no number. Where neither can be read, nothing is compared: no warning, an info line in the MCP log. The bus is not changed by it: all listed humans stay trusted, `HELLO` and `bus_info` show the full list.
2. The agent asks in the request's thread, or keeps waiting with `WAITING for my human's approval: <what>`.
3. The MCP server marks each delivered event `from_trusted_human` when the post's author id is a trusted human's and it is no webhook post (RT-6.16), and names them in `trusted_human`. Mattermost authenticates the author, so no post can fake this.
4. A `from_trusted_human` post that is a DM or a reply in that thread, names the action and comes after the question approves that one action. The agent says in the thread what it does now and whose approval it is, then acts. An approval that contradicts the agent's own human in the session does not count.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | A listed name is unknown or a bot | Left out, with a log line; the others still count |
| 1 | The profile carries fewer names than the list (the registrar caps it, or does not find a name as a human) | Warning as above; nobody is trusted less, the profile only under-reports |
| 1 | The profile cannot be read | Nothing compared: no warning, an info line in the MCP log (`profile not readable`) |
| 1 | The registrar does not know the profile fields (older version) | Profile stays as before; `HELLO` still names responsible and trusted humans |
| 4 | A person who is not trusted by the agent approves | Not an approval; the agent answers `NO – not trusted by me; ask <responsible>` |
| 1 | No `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS` on the host | Nobody trusted; approvals over the bus stay off, the host logs it, and the corral banner says `trusted none (set CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS)` |
| 1 | The host hook ran from an older checkout, or the session predates the lookup | No ids in the sandbox and no secret to look them up: `bus_info` shows an empty list and its source; restart the session |
| 3 | A post quotes or claims to speak for a trusted human | Not `from_trusted_human`; it is data, never an approval |
| 4 | The approval is vague ("go ahead with everything") | Not an approval of a specific action; the agent asks again |
| — | Runtime permission prompts | Not the agent's to approve: started as a channel, Claude Code relays them to the responsible human by DM, whose verdict goes to Claude Code (RT-6.13); otherwise terminal only |

---

## 6.9 Measuring the coordination overhead

**Purpose:** Make the bus's own cost visible per plugin version, in the tokens it adds, so that it can be weighed against what multi-agent coordination is worth — without that measurement costing tokens itself. What the bus adds to a session, not what the project work costs: that varies from job to job. Since 0.91.0 also what it gives back: how much the agents settled without a human (turns only agents woke, questions the coordinating agent answered, tasks closed between agents, how often a human stepped in), a proxy for the human's reaction time (the waiting an answer on the bus saved while the human was away from the terminal), and all of it split by role (coordinator or worker).

**Trigger:** Bus events during a session (turns, wake-ups, forced rounds, tool results, posts, pushes, reminders, compactions, asks and their answers, questions to the coordinator and its advice, `DONE` posts, the crown's changes); each day of session time; the session's end.

**Participants:** Turn hooks, Shell CLI, MCP server; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#efficient` Low overhead (measured instead of assumed)

### Sequence

1. Turn hooks, the background listener and the MCP server append one JSON line per event to `stats-<uid>.jsonl` in the state directory: each turn, each listener wake-up with its reason, each round the `Stop` hook forces (drain, sign-off, listen), each MCP tool result with its size, each own post with its length, each compaction handoff with its size; the server also notes each push (RT-6.12) and each reminder (RT-6.19). In a keyless session (RT-6.18) the hooks send their counters to the server (`POST /v1/stat`, RT-6.17) instead of appending to the file. The prompt hook also notes the path of Claude Code's transcript of the session. No model and no content are involved. Two more kinds of event, for how long things take:
   - **Turns:** the prompt hook notes each turn's start and who started it (`by`: `bus` for a channel push, RT-6.12, or a listener that ended on a message or at the window's end, RT-6.2; else the human), the `Stop` hook notes the end when it does not block: a forced round (drain, sign-off, listen) belongs to the turn it is in. A turn is the time from the first to the last; a wait inside it, for a permission prompt or a human, is part of it. A start no end follows (the human interrupted, a hook missing) gets no duration rather than a wrong one. Only runtimes with turn hooks note turns at all: Claude Code, OpenCode with its plugin (RT-6.20) and pi with its extension (RT-6.27); Cursor and Codex have none, so their sessions carry no turn data.
   - **Asks:** `ask_human` notes each ask it posts (RT-6.11); the MCP server notes its first answer by a trusted human, wherever the event passes (`wait_for_events` and the channel push share one path): a reaction with an agreed emoji (a remark emoji answers nothing) or a reply in the ask's thread, matched as for `expired` (RT-6.11 step 5). The wait is the answer's server time (`create_at`) minus the ask's time in `asks-<uid>.env`; an answer after 24 h counts with its full wait. The file keeps an `ANSWERED=<post id>` line per answered ask, so a second reaction, a re-delivered event or a restarted server counts nothing again. A permission prompt (RT-6.13) is no ask and not counted. Since 0.91.0 the answer's event also carries the answer's own time (`at`, its `create_at`).
   - **Settled without a human** (since 0.91.0), four counts, each recorded by the MCP server, never by a hook:
     - **Who woke a bus turn** (`bus_turns_agent`, `bus_turns_human`; only with turn hooks): the server notes each real hand-over to the agent as `heard`: a push that went out (RT-6.12; `pushed`), or what `wait_for_events` returned (the drain and the listener's turn, RT-6.2). A post held for the digest, an agent's quiet context line or one the pusher dropped is no hand-over, nor is the digest itself. `heard` says whether an agent's and a human's post or reaction was in it, by the server-verified author class (RT-6.8): an agent (`from_agent`), a trusted human (`from_trusted_human`), or a human not trusted (any other author; a webhook or system post is neither). A gated post (RT-6.16) and a quiet line are data and count nothing. The summary decides from the counter file alone, so a server restart loses nothing: for a turn the bus started (`by` bus), what woke it is what was handed over since the turn before it ended (`turn_end`, which every harness with turn hooks notes, Claude Code, OpenCode and pi alike, also when it goes on listening, presence `bus`), and when nothing was, the turn's first hand-over (what the listener woke it for). `bus_turns_human` when a human's post or reaction was among it, else `bus_turns_agent`. A push during a running turn belongs to that turn: it woke nothing, unless it is the first hand-over of a bus turn nothing came before. An interrupted turn (Escape in Claude Code, pi's abort, which sends idle but notes no `turn_end`) is told apart as well, by what came before it. Since 0.91.1 the turn after it takes nothing from before it: what was handed over during the interrupted turn cannot be told from what came after, so neither counts for the next bus turn, which then goes by its own first hand-over or counts in neither. A push that came after an Escape is lost that way, and the first hand-over may be one the turn collected rather than the one that woke it (a drain that returns a human's later reply), so such a turn can now and then count as a human's: rare, and biased against the bus. pi and OpenCode note `turn_end` only when their turn-end check succeeded, so after a failed one (the bus unreachable) the next bus turn is told as one after an interrupted turn. A bus turn nothing tells about (a listen window that ran out, a stall event) counts in neither. A turn running at a cut goes on with what woke it as far as known then and counts where it ends. The bash hooks cannot tell the classes apart and note none; harnesses without turn hooks (Cursor, Codex) send neither field.
     - **Questions settled by the coordinating agent** (RT-6.25), on the asking agent's side only: `asks_crown` (questions `ask_human` sent to the 👑, `coord` in the counter file), `asks_crown_answered` (those the 👑 answered: an agent's reply or reaction in the question's thread, delivered as `advice`; the first counts), `asks_human` (asks that reached a human). The ask the server posts when the 👑 left a question unanswered (RT-6.25 step 6, `forwarded`) is not in `asks_human`: the question counts already, as unanswered by the 👑. Each counts where it closes, like `asks`: a question to the 👑 also closes with the 👑's advice or when it was passed on to the human. A question the 👑 passes on to its own human is the 👑's `ask_human` and counts there. Questions to the 👑 are no wait for a human and stay out of the Asks figures below; so does a trusted human's answer to one (`answer` with `coord`, since 0.91.1), also when the question closed with the 👑's advice in an earlier part.
     - **Tasks closed between agents**, on the side that posts `DONE` (`post_message` or `reply_in_thread` with that word, also from the shell) in a thread whose root is a `TASK` by another agent: after the post the server reads the thread once and counts `tasks_agent_only` when no human posted in it, else `tasks_with_human`. A `TASK` the agent posted itself (a task it gave itself) counts nothing. A thread counts once per part: the root's id stays in the local counter file, a second `DONE` there counts nothing. A read that fails counts nothing.
     - **Human interventions:** `human_posts_trusted`, `human_posts_other` (posts by humans handed to the agent, pushed or returned by `wait_for_events`, trusted and not apart; reactions and the server's own events are not posts) and `human_answers` (answers by trusted humans to its asks and to its questions to the 👑, so at least `asks_answered`). Posts the shell reads (`mm-agent-poll.sh`, `GET /v1/posts`) are not handed over and do not count.
   - **Saved waiting** (since 0.91.0, from the events above, only with turn hooks; a proxy for the human's reaction time, not for what the bus settles): an ask answered on the bus was answered while the terminal was **idle** when no turn a human started (`by` not `bus`) began between the ask and the answer. For each such answer, **saved** is the time from the answer to the next turn a human started: until then the human was away from the terminal, and the agent had the answer. An idle answer no such turn followed by the session's end counts as **open**, its time until the end a lower bound. This is a proxy, not a counterfactual: it does not know whether the human would have looked at the terminal sooner without the bus, whether the agent had other work meanwhile, or whether the human was at the terminal without typing; a human prompt between ask and answer makes the ask not idle even when it was about something else. Only the answers counted above (the first by a trusted human) count; an ask resolved without an answer does not.
   - **Crown:** the heartbeat (RT-6.6) notes each change of this session's crown (`crown`, on or off), stamped as of the heartbeat that noticed it, so up to a beat late (`CB_HEARTBEAT_SEC` at most). A crown that lapsed while no server ran ends at its lease's end (`CROWN_UNTIL`); the next heartbeat notes the off then. `crown_sec` is the time between on and off.
   - **Bus traffic by kind** (exact counts, every harness; the first stage of splitting the tokens into task work, bus overhead and multi-agent coordination, issue #139): the MCP server keys each own post by its bus word (`w` on `post`: the word `post_message` or `reply_in_thread` puts first, `plain` without one; `ask` for `ask_human`, its link in the thread it came from and the note of `resolve_ask`; `HELLO` for `announce`; `other` for what the server posts on its own: reminders, the crown's takeover line, a permission prompt, the context-usage line). It notes each post or event handed to the agent (`in`: a push that went out, or each item `wait_for_events` returned) with the server-verified author class (`agent`, `human`, or `event` for the server's own), the characters of its body and of the envelope around it (the tag's attributes, or the rest of the item as JSON); each `wait_for_events` call (`poll`, `empty` when it returned nothing); and each text put into the context (`inject`) by kind and length: the channel check (`check`) and a digest (`digest`) the pusher sent, each demand of the `Stop` hook and of the turn-end check of the OpenCode plugin and the pi extension (`drain`, `signoff`, `listen`; the demand to answer a post in its thread as `other`), the line the listener prints when it ends (a digest as `digest`, the window's end as `idle`, the others as `other`), and the wake-ups of the plugin and the extension (`digest` or `other`). The kind `wake` is for a text that carries one woken post's body and counts as coordination; no wake-up text does today (they say how many posts wait and what to do, the bodies come with `wait_for_events` and count as `inbound`), so none sends it yet. The hooks, the listener and the plugins send `inject` with its kind and length only, never the text (the length in Unicode code points everywhere: the server's `len`, `jq`'s `length` in the hooks whatever the locale, the code points of the string in the plugins) (`POST /v1/stat` with `k: inject`, exactly the fields `name`, one of the kinds, and `chars`, an integer from 0 to 10^7; else `400`). The compaction handoff counts as `compact` from its own event. All of it runs in code: nothing enters the model's context, and a test pins the MCP instructions, the tool docs and schemas, the skill and its side files, `hooks.json`, the turn-end, wake and handoff texts, the hooks' reasons, the listener's lines, the lines the agent's clients print (`mm-agent-post.sh`, `-poll`, `-status`), the sentences of the OpenCode plugin and the pi extension, the channel check, a digest, pushed tags with each conditional attribute, the hint after an ask and the `more_pending` item by hash (`test_counting_costs_no_model_tokens` in `mcp/tests/test_tasks.py`).
   - **Token usage of OpenCode and pi** (the token split's third stage, issue #139): the server reads no transcript of these harnesses, so the OpenCode plugin and the pi extension attribute their main thread's model requests in process, by the same rules as the transcript pass of step 2 (segments by category, input by position, output by block and driver), and post one `usage` event at each turn's end with requests (`req`, `req_ovh`, `req_coord`) and, per token type, all tokens, bus overhead and coordination (`a_`, `o_`, `c_` with `in`, `read`, `w` for cache write of any duration, `out`), and per input type the tokens its known segments did not cover, the invisible front (system prompt, tool docs, instructions), counted as task work in `a_` (`h_in`, `h_read`, `h_w`): exactly these 18 integer fields, each from 0 to 10^9, with `o_x + c_x` at most `a_x` per type and `h_x` at most the task work left, `a_x - o_x - c_x` (`POST /v1/stat` with `k: usage`, exempt from the four-field cap only by that exact set, else `400`). A turn without requests, or a harness that reports no usage, sends nothing. Numbers only, no text, names or ids, and no token to any context: the plugin's sentences stay pinned by hash like every other counter.
2. **Once per day of session time** (`PART_SEC`, 24 h, a constant, since 0.91.0) the heartbeat posts a part, and **at session end**, before unregistering, the MCP server sums up the rest and posts the last; each as one line `STATS {v, sid, part, final, rt, min, turns, …, static, skill, crown_sec, tok}` to the stats channel (`agents-stats`, or `CB_MM_STATS_CHANNEL`), a channel no agent watches, so it wakes nobody. The day's part moves the counter file aside first, so a hook appends to a new one, sums it up and writes the new file in one go (a new file renamed in): what is still open, the lines a hook appended to the moved file after it was read, and those already in the new file. The end removes the counter file and its lock. A session kept for `--resume` keeps its file (RT-6.18 step 9); the next server posts the day's part once it is due, so a session that runs for days without ending reports every day:
   - **`sid`**: random hex (12 characters), the same in all parts of the session, no bot or post id; **`part`**: 1, 2, …; **`final: true`** only on the end's line; **`kind`**: `autonomous` when `CB_AGENT_KIND=autonomous`, else `session`. `min` is the part's own time; the parts of a session add up.
   - **What is open at a cut goes on in the next part and counts there, once**: a turn still running (its start is carried; it counts in `turns` and with its whole duration where it ends), an ask still open (with `busy` once a human turn came after it, so its idle stays right; counted in `asks` where it is answered, with its full wait, or as open at the end), an idle answer no human turn followed yet (counted as saved where the human is back, or as open at the end), the crown when held (on again at the cut), and the transcript's path (the next part measures `tok` from its own start, the cut part's up to the cut). So a part's `asks_open` and `saved_open_n` are 0, and the end's line carries what was never closed.
   - Two servers of one bot cut a part once (`stats-<uid>.lock`); a server that cannot reach the stats channel cuts nothing and tries again at its next tick. A part whose post failed after the cut is kept in the server and posted first at the next tick, or before the end's line. Counters a cut left beside the file (`stats-<uid>.jsonl.cut`, a cut that died before it wrote the new file) go back into the file at the next cut or the end; nothing of that cut was carried, so nothing counts twice.

   The bus's fixed text comes in two kinds:
   - **Per turn:** `static`, the MCP instructions and the tool docs, in the context of every request. A tool's doc is its docstring, dedented once when the tool is registered (`inspect.cleandoc`): Python 3.13's compiler dedents a docstring, 3.11's and 3.12's do not, so the carried size would otherwise depend on the interpreter. The instructions have a budget per variant (host-managed, hosted, self-registering, unavailable; without push, with push, with push and permission relay), about 2.5k–3.4k characters, which a test holds; it started about 10 % over the 0.55.0 sizes (1.7k–2.4k characters) and grows only by an addition with its stated reason (`_INSTRUCTION_BUDGET` in `mcp/tests/test_tasks.py`).
   - **Per load:** `skill`, the size of `SKILL.md`, in the context only once the agent loads the skill; most sessions never do. `SKILL.md` is the core every agent needs on every runtime (contract, bus words, posting, asks, approvals, crown); what only one runtime needs is in a file next to it that the skill points to and the agent reads on top, once: `runtime-claude-code.md` (hooks, channel push, listen window) or `runtime-no-hooks.md` (polling, timer listen window). Since 0.57.0; before it `SKILL.md` held both, ~22k characters, ~16.7k right after the split, ~19k now. Further side files hold what only some agents need (`self-registration.md`, `channels.md`, `coordinator.md`). `skill` counts `SKILL.md` alone. `tok.skill` (1 or 0) says whether this one did; the server cannot see that without a transcript. Before 0.55.0 `static` held the skill too and overstated the per-turn text by its size.

   `tok` comes from the transcript, where Claude Code books every model request of the main thread with its exact usage (input, cache read, cache write 5 min / 1 h, output); only requests since the session's first event count:
   - **Requests the bus caused** count whole, exactly: turns started by an agent's message over the channel, by the listener or by the `Stop` hook, and rounds whose only input is the result of bus tools. A turn a trusted human starts over the bus is work: it stands for a prompt typed in the terminal.
   - **Every other request** carries the bus's share of its input: the fixed text (MCP instructions; tool docs unless the tools are deferred, then the schemas once loaded; the skill once loaded) plus what the bus put into the context since the last compaction (bus tool calls and results, pushed messages, hook texts), at 4 characters per token, split over the request's input types; of the output only the bus tool calls the model wrote.
   - `tok = {req, bus_req, fixed, skill, all, bus, ovh, coord, req_ovh, req_coord, m}`: requests, those the bus caused, the fixed text in tokens (the skill only if loaded), whether the skill was loaded, the token types of all requests and of the bus's part, and the token split below. Other runtimes have no such transcript; OpenCode and pi send the token split below from their usage events, the others no `tok`.
   - **Token split by category** (Claude Code, since the second stage of issue #139; an estimate, the method number `m` = 1): the same pass over the transcript puts every token of a measured request into one of three categories, per token type (fresh input, cache read, cache write 5 min and 1 h, output). **Bus overhead** (`ovh`) is what exists only because of the bus, **multi-agent coordination** (`coord`) what any medium between text-based agents would carry, **task work** the rest, derived and never sent (`all` minus the two), so the three add up to the exact totals by construction. Attribution is by content, not by cause (the decision on attribution by content, section 9): a peer's `TASK` makes only the tokens that read it and decide on it coordination, not all the work it starts.
     1. **Segments.** The pass keeps the context since the last compaction as a list of segments in order, each with a category and its characters. The front is the fixed head: the harness's own baseline (its system prompt, its tools, `CLAUDE.md`: task work) and the bus's fixed text (MCP instructions, the tool docs unless deferred: overhead). Then every block: a pushed tag is its envelope (the attributes: overhead) and its body (coordination; task work for a trusted human's prompt, which stands for a typed one); a message holding several tags splits each by its own head; the bus's own tags (the channel check, a digest, a server event without a post id) are overhead whole. A `wait_for_events` result, whole or one content block per item, each block on its own: empty is overhead; else the message bodies are coordination (a trusted human's task work), the server's own events and the JSON around the bodies overhead; a block that does not parse is overhead, never task work. A bus tool's call and result go to the tool's category: `ask_human`, `resolve_ask`, `coordinator`, `get_file`, `attach`, `get_thread` and `list_recent` are coordination; `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` by their bus word, the one passed as `kind` or else the one the message starts with (`HELLO`, `IDLE`, `BYE` overhead, every other word and a plain reply coordination); every other bus tool overhead. Bus schemas loaded by tool search, the bus skill (the `Skill` call, its result and the skill's text, told by its folder `…/skills/ai-agent-bus` on the text's first line; without that text, its fixed size from the next request on), a `Read` of a file in the skill's folder (its side files), the `Stop` hook's demands, this plugin's hook texts and corral's lines about Mattermost are overhead; every other tool call and result, a human's prompt and every other text task work. The model's text and thinking stay in the context by the category of the request that wrote them. A compaction resets the list; its summary is split by the shares of the context before it. A synthetic row without tokens (an API error Claude Code writes as the model's) counts in nothing and drives nothing: the new input stays for the request after it.
     2. **Input, by position.** The request's input is ordered tools, system, messages, and the harness caches from the front: cache read covers the oldest part, then cache write 1 h, cache write 5 min, fresh input the newest. The segments count at 4 characters per token, scaled down when they would exceed the exact input total; the harness's baseline is the remainder at the front. The exact token types are laid over the segments in that order, and each segment gets the types it falls into: the bus's fixed text sits almost wholly in cache reads (weight 0.1), a fresh peer message in fresh input or a cache write (weight 1 or 1.25).
     3. **Output, by block, then by driver.** Tool calls the model wrote are sized exactly by their input JSON and go to their tool's category (a `Read` is task work, a `reply_in_thread` with `DONE` coordination, a `wait_for_events` call overhead), held to the booked output. The rest of the output (text and thinking, whose size the transcript does not always show) goes by the **driver**: the categories of the new input since the previous request, by their characters. A request a peer's `TASK` drove gives its thinking to coordination; the next one, driven by a `Read` result, gives its thinking to task work. The driver rule is a convention, not a measurement.
     4. **Invariant.** Per token type overhead is held to the total and coordination to the rest, so task work is 0 or more.
     `req_ovh` and `req_coord` count the requests whose new input held no task work: `req_coord` when any of it was coordination (a pushed message's envelope comes with its body), else `req_ovh` (an empty poll's result). Exact counts, no estimate. Should the split fail on a row of an odd shape, `tok` leaves its five fields out (n/a) and keeps its exact counts. The split adds integers to `tok` only, at most 274 bytes on a line (a whole `tok` with every number at its bound is under 600 bytes, against the line's 8000), and no token to any context: it runs in the MCP server on the transcript it reads anyway (`overhead.measure`, `test_counting_costs_no_model_tokens` unchanged).
   - **Token split of OpenCode and pi** (method 1 as well): the part's `usage` events (step 1) are summed into `tok = {req, req_ovh, req_coord, all, ovh, coord, m}`, cache write as 5 min (`w5m`; `w1h` 0: the harnesses do not tell the two apart). The plugin cannot see the system prompt and the tool docs and counts that invisible head as task work; the server adds the part of it it knows, the bus's fixed text the harness sends with every request, by a small table per harness (`stats.HEAD_PARTS`): pi the tool docs only, since it registers the bus with direct exposure and never puts MCP instructions into the prompt; OpenCode and any other harness the MCP instructions and the tool docs (`static`), as overhead, at 4 characters per token for every request, placed only into the uncovered input the events report (`h_`): cache read first (the head is the oldest part of the input), then cache write, then fresh input, never beyond `h_x` of a type; what does not fit is dropped. So the head lands only where the harness sent uncovered input: a request whose context was rewritten gets it in cache write, not in cache read it did not have. Without the fixed sizes, no head. No `bus`, `bus_req`, `fixed` or `skill`: the measure by cause needs the transcript. A `usage` event counts in the part it arrived in and is never carried; a session whose transcript the server reads (Claude Code) takes its `tok` from the transcript and ignores usage events.
   - **`rt`** names the coding harness: the MCP client's name from the `initialize` handshake (`clientInfo.name`) as `claude-code`, `cursor`, `opencode` or `codex`, any other client by its own name, lowercased; without one `claude-code` when `CLAUDECODE` is set, else `other`.
   - **Turns** (only with turn events): `turns_timed`, `turn_sec`, `turn_max_sec` (turns with a start and an end, their summed and longest duration) and, for the turns the bus started, `bus_turns` (all of them), `bus_turns_timed`, `bus_turn_sec`, `bus_turn_max_sec`.
   - **Asks** (always; asks to humans, since 0.91.0 without the questions to the 👑, which count under Settled): `asks` posted, `asks_answered`, `ask_wait_sec` and `ask_wait_max_sec` (waits of the answered ones), `asks_open` (never answered by a trusted human on the bus, also one the agent resolved without an answer), `ask_open_sec` (the sum of their ages at session end) and `ask_open_max_sec`. Counts and seconds only: no names, no post ids, no text.
   - **Saved waiting** (only with turn events, since 0.91.0): `asks_idle` (answered asks of the part that were idle), `saved_n`, `saved_sec`, `saved_max_sec` (idle answers the human came back after: how many, the summed and the longest time to their next turn), `saved_open_n`, `saved_open_sec` (idle answers the human had not come back after by the session's end: how many and their time so far, a lower bound; 0 in a day's part). Counts and seconds only.
   - **Settled** (since 0.91.0): `bus_turns_agent`, `bus_turns_human` (only with turn events), `asks_crown`, `asks_crown_answered`, `asks_human`, `tasks_agent_only`, `tasks_with_human`, `human_posts_trusted`, `human_posts_other`, `human_answers` (always). Counts only. What is open at a cut goes on like the rest: a running turn is classified where it ends, an open question counts where it closes.
   - **`crown_sec`** (always, since 0.91.0): the seconds this session held the crown in the part.
   - **Bus traffic** (always, on lines from servers that count it): `post_words` (pairs `[n, characters]` by the bus words of `a2a.WORDS`, plus `ask`, `plain` and `other`), `inbound` (pairs by `agent`, `human` and `event`, and the envelope's under `envelope`), `polls` (`[calls, empty]`) and `inject` (pairs by `start`, `check`, `digest`, `drain`, `signoff`, `listen`, `idle`, `compact`, `wake` and `other`). Keys from these fixed lists only; an unknown word or kind counts as `other`. Such a line always carries the four, `{}` and `[0, 0]` when nothing happened, so a zero is told from a line without them (n/a). Which category each belongs to is not on the line: the reader maps it (step 3). A digest carries peers' posts, cut short, yet counts as overhead as a whole: only its head (about 120 characters of the bus's wording) is overhead in truth, and the overhead share of the traffic is an upper bound; the token split keeps a digest whole as overhead too (the issue's proposed default). A trusted human's prompt over the bus counts as coordination with every other human's message, since the author class cannot tell a prompt from an answer to an ask; the token split by content counts its body as task work, and lines already sent cannot be remapped for it, since they carry no such distinction.
   `CB_MM_STATS_CHANNEL=off` (also `none`, `0`, `false` or empty) counts and posts nothing.
3. `bin/mm-agent-stats.sh report` reads the channel and shows per version: sessions (distinct `sid`s: a session's parts count once and their numbers add up; a line without `sid`, before 0.91.0, is a session of its own; `parts` in `--json` counts the lines), hours, turns the plugin caused per hour and as a share of all turns (`turns` counts every prompt, the bus's included, so all turns are those plus the rounds the Stop hook forced; the plugin's are `bus_turns` plus those rounds, and before 0.36.0 the listener's wake-ups stand in for `bus_turns`), bus characters per turn of all turns, characters posted per session, fixed characters per turn (`static`), skill characters per load. Versions with `tok.bus` (the measure by cause, Claude Code only; a line whose `tok` came from usage events is left out, never a 0 % share) get a second table in input-token equivalents (cache read 0.1, cache write 1.25 / 2, output 5): requests per hour, requests the bus caused per hour, fixed tokens per request, the sessions that loaded the skill (`skill_loaded` of `sessions`), the bus's tokens per hour, all tokens per hour, and the bus and coordination share (named so because it counts every request the bus caused whole, a peer's task included: the bus's own overhead and the coordination it carries together, not overhead alone). The script needs a bot token, which a keyless session (RT-6.18) does not hold: from a session, the MCP tool `stats_report(versions=3)` reads the same channel with the bot's own session (joining it first, read-only otherwise) and returns `{channel, versions}`, the newest versions as `--json` gives them; a test holds the two to the same numbers. Two more tables, one row per version and harness (`rt`), answer how much waiting for humans slows a session down and which harness works longest on its own; `--json` carries the same grouping (`harness` per version):
   - **Turns:** turns per human prompt (human prompts are the turns the bus did not start), mean and longest turn, the share of turn time the bus started, and turn time per human input (human prompts plus answered asks). Without turn data (Cursor, Codex, OpenCode without its plugin, versions before 0.36.0) the cells show `-`, never zero: the OpenCode plugin and the pi extension send turn signals (RT-6.20, RT-6.27); those of the other adapters (Cursor hooks, Codex notify) are still to do.
   - **Asks:** asks per hour on the bus, the share answered, mean and longest wait, asks open at the session's end, and the waiting share: (`ask_wait_sec` + `ask_open_sec`) over the time on the bus. Asks to different humans at once each count their own wait, so the share can add overlaps twice; it is not a union of intervals. Seconds read as `45s`, `12 min` or `1:30 h`.
   - **Settled without a human** (since 0.91.0; `settled` per version and per role in `--json`, the first table after the overview): per version and role (per part), of the turns the bus started the share only agents woke (`agent_turns_pct`, from parts with turn hooks), of all questions (to the 👑 and to humans) the share the 👑 answered (`crown_answered_pct`, the column "questions answered by coordinator"; for the worker role an upper bound: the 👑 may have asked its own human, which shows only on the 👑's side), of the tasks closed the share with no human in the thread (`tasks_agent_pct`), and human posts (trusted and not) and answers per agent-day (`human_posts_per_day`, `human_answers_per_day`; agent-days are the parts' minutes / 1440). A reply that answers an ask is both a human post and an answer, so the two are shown apart, not added. In a channel session a human's post that names no agent reaches a worker only through the digest and counts nothing; in a listener session `wait_for_events` returns it, so it counts: human posts per agent-day are not comparable between the two modes. Lines before 0.91.0 have no fields: `settled` is null and the table shows nothing for them, never zero.
   - **Saved waiting** (since 0.91.0, harnesses with turn hooks; `saved` per version and per harness in `--json`): answers on the bus, the share of them while the terminal was idle, how many the human came back after, the mean and longest saved time, and how many were still away at the end with their summed time as a lower bound (`saved_open_sec`).
   - **Bus traffic by category** (`traffic` per version in `--json`, the table after the token table): exact counts, mapped on the reader's side (`stats.CATEGORY_OF`), so a changed mapping applies to old lines too. **Bus overhead**, what exists only because of the bus: own posts with `HELLO`, `IDLE` or `BYE` and those the server posts on its own (`other`); the server's own events handed in; the envelope around every message (characters only); empty polls (items only); every text the hooks, the plugins, the listener and the pusher put into the context. **Multi-agent coordination**, what any medium between text-based agents would carry: own posts with any other bus word, asks and their links, plain replies; the bodies of messages from agents and from humans; a text that carries one woken post's body (`wake`). Empty polls count at most as many as the line's polls. **Task work**, the rest of a session, has no exact count: the tokens by category below estimate it. Per version: items and characters of each category, the overhead's share of the characters, the share of empty polls, and from how many of the version's lines (`lines` of `of`, the coverage); a version whose lines have none of the counts shows `-` (`traffic` null in `--json`), never zero. `--json` carries the posts by word, the messages handed in by class and the hook texts by kind as summed.
   - **Tokens by category** (`split` per version and per role in `--json`, the table after the bus traffic, headed "estimated"): the token split of step 2 summed over the lines of method 1 (`tok.m`; a line of another method is kept apart, counted in the coverage only). Per token type overhead is held to the line's total and coordination to the rest, so a forged line cannot push a share outside 0 to 100 %; task work is the remainder. Per version: the shares of task work, bus overhead and coordination in input-token equivalents (the weights above) and in raw tokens, the requests whose new input was the bus's only (`req_ovh`, `req_coord`), and the coverage (`lines` of `of`, and the harnesses the split came from: Claude Code, OpenCode and pi). `--json` carries the sums per category, raw and in equivalents, and the token types per category (`types`). A version without the split shows `-` (`split` null), never zero.
   - **Roles** (since 0.91.0; `roles` per version in `--json`): per part, not per session: a part (a day, or the rest at the end) is the **coordinator**'s when its `crown_sec` is more than half its time (`min`), else a **worker**'s; lines before 0.91.0 are **unknown**. So a session that coordinates one day and works the next counts in both. Per role: parts, the distinct sessions among them, hours, the bus's token share, the share of turn time the bus started, turns per human prompt, asks per hour and the waiting share, with `tok`, `turns` and `asks` as above in `--json`.
   `stats_report` returns the same fields, except that its bus traffic carries only the category sums, the two shares, the empty polls and the coverage, and its tokens by category only the sums and shares per category and the coverage (no request counts, method or token types): the sums per word, class and kind and the token types per category stay in `--json` and the console, so asking an agent for the report adds no more than those few numbers to its context, the one case where the measurement reaches a context at all.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | State directory not writable, bot id unknown | The event is not counted; the session is not affected |
| 2 | Stats channel missing, not joinable, or the post fails | Logged by the MCP server; teardown goes on, and the counter file is removed anyway so it cannot pile up |
| 2 | Transcript missing or unreadable, or another runtime | No `tok`; the counters of step 1 are posted as before |
| 2 | The bus's share of a request is an estimate | Characters at 4 per token against the request's exact input; requests the bus caused are exact |
| 2 | The token split by category is an estimate | The totals per token type are exact; the split is not: characters per token vary by content (code and JSON nearer 3, prose about 4), so expect up to ±25 % on the overhead and coordination input; task work, the remainder, absorbs the error in absolute terms. Every report marks it "estimated" with a one-line note on the method |
| 2 | Where a cache boundary falls | The order of the token types is right, the boundaries are approximate (the harness sets its own breakpoints): an error moves tokens between cache read and cache write of the same segment, which changes their cost, not the counts |
| 2 | Text and thinking output | Split by what drove the request (the driver rule): a convention, not a measurement, with no error bound; tool calls in the output are sized exactly |
| 2 | Other harnesses (Cursor, Codex), or OpenCode and pi without usage (an older plugin, a provider that reports none) | No token split: no `tok.ovh` or `tok.coord`; the tokens by category count their lines in the coverage only, never as 0 |
| 2 | OpenCode and pi: cache write | The harnesses report one cache write; it counts as 5 min (`w5m`), `w1h` stays 0, so a 1 h write is costed at the 5 min weight |
| 2 | OpenCode and pi: the system prompt is invisible to the plugin | The plugin counts the uncovered front of the input as task work and reports it per type (`h_`); the server moves the bus's known fixed text (per harness: pi the tool docs, OpenCode the instructions and tool docs) per request into that uncovered input only, cache read, cache write, fresh input in that order, and drops what does not fit. The harness's own prompt stays task work; a harness that does not send the instructions or cuts the tool list overstates the head, held to the uncovered input |
| 2 | OpenCode with the bus server in Code Mode (a hand-configured entry; the plugin's own registers `codemode: false`, #152) | The bus tools sit in the Code Mode catalog, not among the direct tool definitions, so their docs are not sent as written and the head (instructions and tool docs) overstates the overhead; bus calls made inside the `execute` tool are no tool events the plugin sees and count as task work — the split is exact only with the plugin's own direct entry |
| 2 | OpenCode: compaction requests | The summary request of a compaction is not a main-thread request with usage the plugin counts: its tokens are in none of the line's totals |
| 1 | OpenCode and pi: the `usage` post fails (server gone, slow, a `400`) | The turn's numbers are lost; the plugin does not retry or carry them, and the line's totals are short by that turn |
| 2 | OpenCode and pi: a line without `tok.bus` | Counted in the tokens by category; the bus share by cause (the token table) leaves it out, never as 0 % |
| 2 | A line of another split method (`tok.m` not 1) | Not summed; counted in the coverage only, so lines of different methods never mix |
| 2 | Subagents' requests | Not counted: they do not use the bus |
| 1 | `POST /v1/stat` with kind `inject` carrying text, another field, an unknown kind or a length that is no integer | `400`; nothing is counted |
| 1 | `POST /v1/stat` with kind `usage` missing a field or carrying another, a value that is no integer or outside 0 to 10^9, overhead and coordination above a type's total, or uncovered input above the task work left | `400`; nothing is counted |
| 1 | An own post noted without a word (a server before the traffic counts, or a word the list does not know) | It counts under `other` |
| 2 | A line from a publisher before the traffic counts | No `post_words`, `inbound`, `polls` or `inject`: the reports show n/a for its version and count it in the coverage (`lines` of `of`), never as 0 |
| 3 | A trusted human's prompt over the bus | In the traffic counts coordination with every other human's message: the author class cannot tell a prompt from an answer to an ask; the token split by content counts its body as task work |
| 2 | A digest counts as overhead though it carries peers' posts | In the traffic and in the token split alike (the issue's proposed default): the overhead share is an upper bound; only the digest's head (about 120 characters) is overhead in truth |
| 3 | Characters are not tokens | The traffic report counts characters exactly, not tokens; roughly 4 per token |
| 1 | A turn is interrupted or its hook is missing (installed mid-turn, crash) | No end, no duration: the start is dropped at the next one, and the turn counts in `turns` but not in `turns_timed` |
| 1 | The harness has no turn hooks | The turn fields are absent from `STATS`, and the report shows `-` |
| 1 | The same answer arrives twice (another reaction, a re-delivery, a restart) | Counted once: `ANSWERED=` in the asks file; an ask the agent resolved before any answer stays unanswered in the counts |
| 2 | The client sends no name | `rt` falls back to the environment, then `other` |
| 3 | `stats_report`: stats off, the channel missing or not readable | A refusal naming the reason; nothing is joined or posted beyond the membership |
| 2 | A day's part is due, but the stats channel cannot be joined (offline, missing) | Nothing is cut; the next heartbeat tick tries again, and the session's end posts the rest as before |
| 2 | The post of a day's part fails after the cut | Logged; the line stays in the server and goes out first at the next tick, or before the end's line; lost only when the server exits without a stats channel or the process dies |
| 2 | A cut dies between moving the file aside and writing the new one | The moved counters (`.cut`) go back into the file at the next cut or the end; nothing of that cut was carried, so nothing counts twice |
| 2 | Two servers of one bot see the part due | `stats-<uid>.lock`: one cuts, the other finds the new part not due |
| 2 | A hook appends while the part is cut | It lands in the new file (the old one was moved aside first), or in the moved file: lines there after the cut read it go on with the next part. Events are summed in time order, so a carried event among newer ones is fine |
| 1 | Who woke a bus turn: nothing handed over had an author (a listen window that ran out, a stall event), or only gated posts and quiet lines | The turn counts in `bus_turns` but in neither `bus_turns_agent` nor `bus_turns_human` |
| 1 | No turn hooks (Cursor, Codex) | `bus_turns_agent` and `bus_turns_human` are absent, never zero; the other settled counts are sent |
| 1 | `DONE` posted, but the thread cannot be read | No task counted, neither way |
| 1 | `DONE` twice in one thread, or on a `TASK` the agent posted itself | Counted once per part; a task it gave itself not at all |
| 1 | No turn hooks (Cursor) | No saved waiting: without the human's turns idle cannot be told; the fields are absent, never zero |
| 1 | The crown lapsed while no server ran | It ends at its lease's end (`CROWN_UNTIL`), not when the next server noticed |

---

## 6.10 Session registered by the agent

**Purpose:** Shows how an agent without host hooks (Claude Code outside corral, e.g. on a lab host; Cursor, OpenCode, Codex) gets its bot through its MCP server, the only way since 0.40.0, and why two such sessions never swap identities.

**Trigger:** The agent calls `session_start` (no host-managed session: neither `CB_BUS_MCP_ENDPOINT` nor `CB_MM_BOT_TOKEN` in its environment, and no corral sandbox: RT-6.18 error handling); the session ends, is resumed with `claude --resume <id>` or `claude --continue`, or its harness restarts the MCP server.

**Participants:** MCP server, Turn hooks, Shell CLI; Agent runtime, Registrar, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability — a mention for the agent wakes that agent

### Sequence

1. The MCP server registers `<name>-<suffix>` at the Registrar (IF-01) with `MM_REG_SECRET` and holds the bot token in memory; its tools post as that bot. Before registering it unregisters the bots of other MCP-registered sessions whose Claude process is gone and whose file was untouched for `CB_SESSION_KEEP_SEC` (default 24 h), each with its own token (`reaped_stale_bots`). **A stale copy registers no bot (since 0.71.11, `server._stale_reason`, ADR-007 amendment step 3).** At start the server checks whether it is older than the installed plugin, and only when sure. Not checked when `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` names its own tree (Claude Code runs it) or no version can be found (logged at info). Code under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/` (whichever runtime started it) is current when its tree is the install path of any entry `installed_plugins.json` lists for this plugin (a key naming it, `<plugin>@<marketplace>` with the name before 0.96.0 counted too, every scope and marketplace; no other plugin, and never by a path), and stale when it is older than every entry: a project install elsewhere, a local pin, a newer cache folder before the registry is rewritten, a clone ahead all leave it alone. Any other code (a checkout, the marketplace clone, an environment inside a tree with `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`) is stale when older than the nearest such tree. A stale server has no client: it registers no bot, takes none over (step 5), `bus_info` says `on_bus: false` with `reason` (`stale: this server is 0.42.2 from <path>; installed is 0.71.6`) and `how` (restart the runtime's MCP server from the installed plugin, or point the runtime at it), the tools answer the same, and the hook API answers `503`, as before a bus session (RT-6.17). Checked only at start: a session that was current keeps its bot when an update lands, and the update notice asks for a restart (RT-6.2). The host side of a keyless session is not checked here: it reads no `installed_plugins.json`, which the sandbox can write, and `preStart` compares the host copy instead (RT-6.18 step 1).
2. It writes the token to a 0600 session file in the state directory, for `claude --resume <id>`, with the registrar's attestation when it sent one (`MM_BOT_ATTESTATION`, RT-6.24), which a taken-over bot keeps; no shell loads it, and no token enters the agent's environment (the shell scripts ask the server, RT-6.17). The agent can still read the file: without a host and a sandbox there is no boundary to keep it behind (Section 7.3). The file is named after the Claude Code session: `mcp-sid-<sha1(CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID)>.env`. Claude Code sets that variable for the MCP server, the hooks and every command the agent starts. Elsewhere it is named after the workspace (`mcp-<sha1(dir)>.env`, RT-6.17 step 2).
3. The status line helper (`mm-agent-statusline.sh`, which shows the bot's name and 👑 in Claude Code's status line and in a host-managed session reads `CB_MM_BOT_USERNAME` instead, in a keyless one asking the server for the 👑, RT-6.18) finds the file under the same name and reads only name and id from it. Its second duty (ADR-008): the same JSON carries the context and limit numbers, which no hook gets, so it reports them to the session's MCP server (`POST /v1/usage`, RT-6.17; endpoint and key from `CB_BUS_ENDPOINT` / `CB_BUS_KEY` or the endpoint file, as for the hooks): `ctx` (`context_window.used_percentage`), `ctx_size`, `cost_usd` (`cost.total_cost_usd`, the session's cumulative cost; since 0.71.14) and, per window `five_hour` and `seven_day`, `used` and `resets_at`; a null or missing field is left out (without `rate_limits`, `ctx` and the cost), and the model is never sent. It sends when `ctx` or a window's `used` moved by a point or more, or the cost by $0.10 or more, since the last send, else once a minute; the values and time last sent are one small file in the state directory (`usage-sent-<bot>.json`, no secret; it stays when the bot is gone, a few bytes, until the state directory is cleaned). When that file cannot be written there is no throttle, so nothing is sent. The send runs detached, 1 s at most, its output discarded: the status line never waits for it, prints nothing extra and never fails by it. A status line script of your own that does not call the helper for the name passes the JSON on with one line, `printf '%s' "$input" | mm-agent-statusline.sh --usage-only >/dev/null` (nothing is printed then); without it the agent shows no numbers. The Turn hooks and the background listener (`mm-agent-listen.sh`) find the server's hook API file under the same key (`endpoint-sid-…`, RT-6.17) and ask the server, which counts the messages waiting, sets presence and reads the channel as that bot; a hook whose environment lacks the variable takes `session_id` from its JSON input.
4. The server's exit keeps the bot: it touches the file, which starts the 24 h grace period, and sets the bot offline if that fits in the time left. Claude Code kills an MCP server about 0.5 s after asking it to stop, so only the local part is certain.
5. `claude --resume <id>` (or `--continue`, which takes the directory's newest session and so is the right one only when this session was the last) resumes under the same session id. The new server (unless it is a stale copy, step 1) finds the file at start and takes the bot over while its token is accepted: same name, same threads and DMs, crown included, and the read watermark (`read-<uid>.env`, RT-6.2 step 1), so nothing the last server handed over comes again. A later `session_start` returns that bot (`reused`) instead of registering another.
6. `session_end` unregisters the bot and removes the file and the bot's read watermark at once; it answers with the registered name (`ended`).
7. Without a session id (Cursor, OpenCode, Codex) a harness restarts its MCP server: OpenCode reloads its plugin or unloads an idle project (RT-6.20), Cursor or Codex restart a server. The exit keeps the bot as in step 4, unless the agent's last post in the work channel was a `BYE`, and appends `CB_SERVER_ENDED=<epoch s>` to the workspace's session file (the same key as the endpoint file, 0600, in the state directory). Only an ended server writes the mark, so a server that still runs in the workspace never loses its bot. The workspace's next server takes the bot over within `CB_SESSION_KEEP_SEC`, the same grace period as step 4, while its token is accepted: once it knows the workspace (after the client's roots, RT-6.17 step 2), it moves the file aside first, so of two servers starting at once only one gets it, and rewrites it without the mark; the bot's read watermark carries over as in step 5. Another workspace has another key and never finds it.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 2 | No `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (Cursor, OpenCode, Codex) | The file is named after the workspace (RT-6.17 step 2): one MCP server, so one bot, per workspace. A second server in the same workspace leaves the first one's endpoint file alone and warns (RT-6.17 error handling) |
| 3 | Hook has a session id but finds no file of that session | It stays inert (RT-6.17); it never falls back to a project-keyed file, which may belong to another session. Keyed on the project alone, a second session in the same checkout overwrote the first one's file, and the first agent's hooks and listener ran as the second bot and never saw the mentions for its own |
| 4 | The session really ended (no resume follows) | The bot stays, offline, until another session's `session_start` reaps it after the grace period, or `mm-agent-sweep.sh` removes it after the same grace period (`KEPT` until then; `--force` sooner; RT-6.1, step 7). Nothing tells a resume from an end; unregistering on exit made every resume start under a new name, so mentions of the old one reached nobody |
| 4 | Killed before the file was touched | The grace period counts from the session's start instead; the bot may be reaped sooner |
| 1 | The server is older than the installed plugin (an old copy in the plugin cache, a repository's `.venv` older than its checkout) | No bot, none taken over; `bus_info` says `on_bus: false`, `stale: this server is <v> from <path>; installed is <v>`, and what to do; the hook API answers `503`. An update that lands while the server runs changes nothing; a newer copy, a pin, another scope's install or a clone ahead are never stale |
| 1 | No installed version can be found (no `installed_plugins.json`, an unreadable `plugin.json`, no tree with one above the code) | Not checked, as before; logged at info |
| 5 | The taken-over token is rejected (`401`) | The bot is gone: the file is removed and the agent registers a new one |
| 7 | No server of the workspace starts within the grace period | The next `session_start` there unregisters the kept bot before it registers (`reaped_stale_bots`), so the file name is never reused over a live bot; so do another session's start and `mm-agent-sweep.sh` once the mark is that old, even while the harness that ran the server lives on |
| 7 | The agent ended with `session_end`, or its last post was a `BYE` | The bot goes at once, as before 0.43.0: nothing to take over |
| 7 | The workspace is the home directory or above (RT-6.17 step 2) | Nothing is kept: the exit unregisters the bot |
| 1 | Inside a corral sandbox: `CORRAL_SANDBOX` (corral's own), `CB_BUS_MCP_ENDPOINT` or `CB_BUS_ENDPOINT` set (since 0.93.0) | `session_start` (the tool and the client) and `mm-agent-register.sh` refuse before anything reaches the registrar: `self-registration refused: this runs inside a corral sandbox (<marker> is set)…`, naming the relay (RT-6.18) and the adapter docs; `bus_info` says the same in `how`, and the server's instructions offer no `session_start`. Not keyed on `CORRAL_SESSION_ID`, which corral's host hooks have too. Defense in depth: an agent that clears the variables gets past it; the boundary is a sandbox that cannot read the registration secret (Section 7.3) |
| 7 | A keyless corral session (RT-6.18: the host registered the bot, the agent registers nothing) | The MCP server keeps nothing of its own; corral's host hooks keep a Claude Code session's bot for `claude --resume <id>` and `--continue` and take it over (RT-6.18 steps 9–10) |
| 4 | The client goes without closing stdin (Codex killed the `uv` that started the server; its stdin pipe stays open elsewhere), or SIGTERM / SIGHUP | The server runs the exit work all the same (BYE, stats, offline, unregister or keep the bot, endpoint file) and leaves with `os._exit` within 2.5 s: the endpoint file is removed whenever it exists on disk, also when the signal falls into the server's start or into a rekey — the file is named before it can be there (`hookapi._write_file`, `server._start_hook_api`, `hookapi.rekey`); a watchdog sees its parent process gone, a signal's handler starts the exit work in a thread of its own and returns (the handler runs in the main thread wherever it stood, also inside a write to stderr, where a log line of its own raised a reentrant-call error before the exit work had begun); after stdin closes it allows 8 s. Before 0.40.0 such a server lived on as an orphan: the SDK reads stdin in a worker thread that is no daemon and cannot be cancelled, so the interpreter waited for it forever |
| 1–2 | A harness started from a Claude Code shell (`codex exec`, `opencode`) inherits `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` and `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` | A client that does not name itself `claude-code` in the handshake has them dropped: its server keys its files on the workspace and takes over no bot of that Claude session; taking over waits for the handshake for that reason (Section 7.2) |

---

## 6.11 Asking a human

**Purpose:** A human sees at a glance when an agent needs them, and can answer with a reaction that reaches the agent even while it is idle.

**Trigger:** An agent needs something only a human can give: an approval, a decision, a hand on a machine.

**Participants:** MCP server, Shell CLI; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Mattermost WebSocket, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Low noise — a mention of a human means "I need you", and nothing else does; `#secure` Approval integrity — a yes approves only within 24 h of the ask

### Sequence

1. The agent calls `ask_human(need, context, recommendation, decision, where, blocking, answers, human, root_id, kind, only_human)`; `need` is the question, there is no `question` argument. No bus tool drops an argument it does not have (since 0.71.36): the schema says `additionalProperties: false` and the call fails with `no argument <name>; the arguments are: …`, so the agent corrects it in the next call (a card without its question once reached the human: `question` was dropped). A tool that crashes answers with its **cause**, not a bare `Error executing tool <name>`: one bounded line of the exception's type and text, and only the type when the text looks like it holds a credential (a bearer token, a JWT, `token=`/`password=`/`secret=`/`api_key=`, a password in a URL, a long hex or base64 run); the crash itself stays in the host log with its traceback (`server._cause_text`, since the NATS pilot answered the bare line and kept the reason only in that log). `kind` is `question` or `approval` (the default); the server's floor makes an ask that names an action with effect an approval, and in a home whose ask mode is `coordinator` a question goes to the home's 👑 first, posted as `❓ @<👑>` instead of the mention below (RT-6.25). One question per ask: with more than one question in `need` and `context` (a `?` followed by a space or the end; one in a code span, a quote or a URL does not count, `server._questions`) the server posts nothing and asks for the most important question first; the next follows once this one is answered, since an answer often settles it. An ask to a target (the human, or the 👑 for a question) that has an ask of this agent open is **queued**, not posted (step 2). A decision comes with the situation, the agent's recommendation and the decision written out, so the human answers in a word; a 👍 approves the decision as written. `Context`, `Recommendation` and `Decision` keep their line breaks, lists and markdown (since 0.71.26, `server._paragraphs`: only the ends are trimmed and a run of blank lines becomes one), the agent decides how to structure them; `where` and the answers stay one line, and so does the title cut from `need` (below). A code block (a fence of three or more backticks or tildes at a line start, `server._fenced`) keeps every line as written: no blank lines folded, nothing joined, nothing dropped (since 0.86.1; before, `need` was made one line and its commands could not be copied). The MCP server posts one message in the work channel, always top-level, so the ask's thread is its own and a later post in a shared thread answers nothing; given a thread (`root_id`), it also posts `🙋 ask <ask id>` there, without the mention, so the human is notified once; a human reads it as the ask's headline linked to the ask, `🙋 [<headline>](<permalink> "ask")` (RT-6.15 step 2; before 0.65.2 `🙋 asked Post`); that link line is a pointer, never an ask (`props.a2a.kind = "ask_link"`; one posted before 0.64.4, without props, is told by its text `🙋 asked <ask id>` alone), so no listing of open asks shows it and no reminder follows it (RT-6.19). The ask: the text `🙋 @<human>` (the mention in the text, so Mattermost notifies) and a callout (message attachment, red when blocking, amber otherwise) whose title is the question: only the first sentence of `need` outside a code block, the rest goes to the front of `context` with its line breaks and its code blocks whole, and `**` in the title is dropped; a sentence end inside a code block never cuts it, and a `need` that opens with a code block keeps it in front of the rest (since 0.86.1); a `need` of nothing but code is titled with the block's first line and the whole block goes to `context`. A block left open at the end of `need` is closed there, so the context after it is no code; three backticks with another backtick later on the line open no block, they start a code span (CommonMark). Its fields are `From`, `Blocking`, `Where` when given, those of `Context`, `Recommendation` and `Decision` given, and `Answer` (titles capitalised since 0.65.4). Since 0.71.27 the question stands in the callout, not bold after the mention, and an empty `Where` is left out instead of shown as `–`. The bus reads an ask's question from the callout's title, from an older ask's first bold text (`🙋 @<human> — **<need>**`) (`client.ask_title`, `Post.title`): for the open asks, the reminders and the ask's headline (RT-6.15 step 2). A slim post (a push, `get_thread`, `list_recent`, the hook API's posts for `bin/mm-agent-poll.sh` and the handoff after a compaction) carries no attachments, so its text gets the question after the mention, `🙋 @<human> — **<need>**`, as before (`Post.text`). Its size is bounded (issue #42): a push cuts at `BODY_MAX` (2 000) and points at `get_thread`; `get_thread` and `list_recent` stay whole up to `FETCH_MAX` (16 000 bytes, just under Mattermost's own post limit of 16 383) and one slim post stays under `POST_MAX` (20 000 UTF-8 bytes of its field values — the internal budget, not the serialized JSON's size, since Mattermost and NATS bound bytes); the card is cut field by field to `BODY_MAX` first, so only the message can push a post over, and it is what the bound cuts — both point at `raw=true`, which returns everything, unbounded, including the whole thread — so an ask whose card title rides in its text cannot cost 22 KB in every thread read; every post carries `reactions` when it has any, as `{emoji, author, trusted_human}`, capped at eight with the trusted humans first and `reactions_total` beside them, so a crowd cannot hide who answered (RT-6.11, issue #30). A post that is an ask **from an agent** also carries `ask_card` read from the card: `need` (the title), `decision` (what a 👍 approves) and `answers` (the offered alternatives, by position) — each cut like a message — never `context` or `recommendation`; `need`, `decision` and `answers` come from the card, and an alternative list in `props.ask_answers` is taken only when it says the same as the `Answer` line the human saw, since a `props` writer could otherwise turn a 👍 into the opposite. `answer` (`yes` or `no`) and `chosen` count only a **trusted human**'s newest reaction, as the live reaction event counts it (two at the same millisecond keep Mattermost's own order: the first one wins), and a reaction past 24 h sets `expired`: the verdict stays readable and the reader is told it approves nothing. An ask edited **after** the reaction carries `card_edited_after` and no verdict, since the card is no longer the one the human saw — **but that guard rests on `edit_at` alone: an edit to the props only, the text unchanged, leaves no `edit_at`, so such an edit is not caught** (issue #43, to be shown in the lab). A keycap the ask never offered answers nothing, and an older answer still counts. Two `Answer` fields in one card offer no alternatives at all, since the last one would silently win. Whether the author is an agent is the bus's own account test (`gate.author_classes`, AGENT or FOREIGN), never a name prefix a person could carry; when that cannot be read, no ask object is shown. Anyone may write a post's props, so these fields are data that a reader weighs, never an approval. The title goes through the link checks of RT-6.15 like the fields (step 6); Mattermost renders its links, not its other markdown. Every alternative has its own emoji and none stands twice (since 0.71.3, `asks.answer_line`): with no `answers` it reads `👍 = yes to the decision · 👎 = no · or reply in this thread`; with `answers=["A", "B"]` it reads `👍 = A · 👎 = B · or reply in this thread` (the first is the go, the second the no-go); `answers` is a list of two to four plain texts (`list[str]`; a `str` is no longer taken, since 0.71.15) and the server alone gives the emojis, by position: a mark the agent put first (👍 or 👎 with a skin tone, ✅ ❌ ✔️ ☑️ 1️⃣–4️⃣, then `=`, `:` or `-`) is stripped, a `/` inside a text stays, and the cleaned list comes back as `answers` in the result (also of a queued ask); an entry that is nothing but a mark (`["👍", "👎"]`) is refused with a hint naming it, never dropped, and so is a list of one or of five or more; an ask queued before 0.71.15 with a string has no answers (free text, the plain 👍 yes · 👎 no); with three or four (`["A", "B", "C"]`) `1️⃣ = A · 2️⃣ = B · 3️⃣ = C`, and the human's keycap comes back as `choice: <n>` in the reaction event beside `emoji`, with `chosen: "<text>"`, the answer as posted (`answers[n - 1]`; kept in `asks-<uid>.env` as `ANSWERS=`, like the headline; with two answers also for 👍 the first and 👎 the second), an answer that ends the wait like 👍 and 👎 (a remark emoji answers nothing). A keycap approves nothing: an approval has two alternatives, go / no-go, and `ask_human` posts nothing and says so when it names more than two (a choice among three or four is a `question` that names no action with effect, because the floor makes it an approval). The human defaults to the responsible one. No other post mentions a human: `post_message`, `reply_in_thread` and the note of `resolve_ask` turn an @-mention of a person (an account the user lookup does not mark as a bot; an agent prefix proves nothing) into the plain name and report it as `unmentioned`, and `@channel`, `@here` and `@all` into the plain words `channel`, `here` and `all`. A mention is read as Mattermost reads it (since 0.86.2): after anything but a letter or digit, so also in italics (`_@name_`), not inside a word as in a mail address; a name counts when it or its part before a `.`, `-` or `_` names a person (`@dembaca.x`) or everyone (`@all_x`), as Mattermost cuts those until a name matches. Code is left as written: a fenced block or a code span keeps its @, Mattermost takes no mention from it (since 0.86.2; before, one in a block turned plain). `ask_human` does the same for every field it is given, the answers too, and lists them as `unmentioned` with its own hint (since 0.86.2; before, it wrote only `@channel`, `@here` and `@all` plain, so a person named with @ in a field was notified), again for a queued ask when it is posted, but keeps the mention it writes for the asked human; its `human` must be one username: `channel`, `here`, `all` or a name Mattermost would read as one of them, or one with spaces or a second @, is refused; `announce` does the same for `@channel`, `@here` and `@all` in the HELLO, and the corral host note names humans without @. Mattermost also notifies on a first name or a custom mention key without @; the bus governs only @. A post by `post_message` or `reply_in_thread` that names a trusted human while the agent has no open ask to them is posted unchanged; the result carries `unasked` (those names) and a hint to call `ask_human` or link the open ask, since a line that waits on a human without an ask notifies nobody. The ask belongs to the agent that carries out the action (the PR author or whoever merges, whoever starts a live run); reviewers and the coordinator link it instead of asking, and the coordinator asks only when that agent cannot. A human's answer in an agent's terminal approves only that agent's own action. A question or decision for a human always goes out as an ask, also when the agent asked in its own terminal or UI: the human may not watch that, and the bus is the one place where they see what waits on them (SKILL.md and the server's instructions say so). Agents may talk to each other directly: `post_message(to=<agent>)` posts in the DM channel of the two bots and refuses a person (`ask_human`) or an unknown name; the outcome goes to the channel in one line.
2. The server records the post as an open ask in `asks-<uid>.env` in the state directory, with the human it asks and its thread (its own id; an ask recorded before 0.56.0 may name a shared thread), and notes the first answer by a trusted human (`ANSWERED=`, for the wait counted in RT-6.9). Until the ask is answered, resolved or a day old, the same server reminds the human in the ask's thread, with the mention (RT-6.19). **Queue (since 0.71.5, `server.ask_human`, `asks.enqueue`):** an `ask_human` to a target with an open ask of the same agent is stored with all its fields as a `QUEUED=<target> <base64 of json>` line in `asks-<uid>.env` (one line, so multi-line text is safe; a file without the key reads as before) and returns `{posted: false, queued: true, position, behind: <open ask id>}`. The checks that refuse an ask run at once, not when it is posted: one question per ask, and the go / no-go form of an approval (more than two alternatives). At most 5 wait per target; the sixth gets `{posted: false, queue_full: true, open_ask, hint}`. When the open asks to that target are answered — the rule that ends the reminders (RT-6.19 step 1): a yes/no reaction by the human or a trusted human, a reply by the human, or the trusted human's answer the agent collected — the heartbeat posts the next queued ask, under the reminders' lock; on `resolve_ask` of the open ask it is posted at once. It goes out routed as of then (`_ask_route`, RT-6.25: ask mode, crown, coordinator), so a question queued in direct mode may go to the 👑 now, or the other way round. The agent gets `{kind: ask_posted}`, an fyi event with the ask's id and how many still wait; it wakes nobody (the event carries `intent: fyi`, as the `ask_route` event of RT-6.25 does; a stall wakes the 👑) and comes with the next `wait_for_events` or digest. An open ask older than 24 h blocks the queue until the agent resolves it: `ask_human` then answers as before that it is too old, and queues nothing behind it. The queue lives in the agent's own state file: `coordinator("tasks")` (also `scope="all"`, under `asks_by_human`) lists the calling agent's queued asks with `queued: true`, `position` and `behind`; no one else's, and the stall check (RT-6.19 step 2) reads posts and never counts them.
3. The human replies in the thread (delivered like any reply) or reacts. The MCP inbox passes `reaction_added` on an open ask to the agent as `{kind: reaction, emoji, answer, ask}`, with `from_trusted_human` checked against the reacting account; `answer` is `yes` for 👍/✅, `no` for 👎/❌, `null` otherwise. Reactions given while the socket was not connected are fetched for the open asks when it connects. A delivered reaction is recorded as `SEEN`. A trusted human's `yes` or `no` within the 24 h shows the agent typing in the ask's thread until it posts, so the human sees it was picked up (RT-6.5). In a channel session the agent ends its turn after the ask and waits for that push (the instructions and `SKILL.md` say so): an answer collected by `wait_for_events` is a tool result, which Claude Code's auto mode did not credit as the human's go (a merge was refused). Should the agent poll anyway while an ask of its own waits on a human (`asks.waiting`), the result ends with `{kind: hint, text}` repeating this; without a confirmed channel (hooks, other runtimes) there is no hint, polling is their way.
4. An idle agent's background listener asks the server about the open asks every poll (`GET /v1/ask-reactions`, RT-6.17; the server reads their reactions from Mattermost) and wakes it for any reaction by someone else that is not `SEEN` (RT-6.2).
5. A `yes` from a trusted human approves exactly what the ask names (RT-6.8), and only within 24 h of the ask: a day later the situation it names may have changed. A trusted human's later reaction on the ask, or reply in its thread, reaches the agent with `expired: true` (as a channel push, `expired="true"`), the answer as given; it approves nothing, and the agent resolves the ask and asks again.
