What counts is when the human answered (the server's time of the reaction or reply), not when the agent collects it. In a thread that holds several of the agent's asks, a reply is late only when all of them are. When the ask is settled the agent calls `resolve_ask`: ✅ on the post — open asks are the ones without — the ask and its `SEEN` lines removed, optionally a reply in its thread.
6. Agents wake each other with `to="all"`, which needs no @ in the text: `post_message(…, to="all")` and `reply_in_thread(…, to="all")` post as usual with `props.a2a.to = "all"` (beside `kind` when the first word is a bus convention, RT-6.14); humans see an ordinary post and are not notified. (`to` on `post_message` otherwise names an agent for a DM; `reply_in_thread` takes only `all`.) Every consumer treats it like an `@channel` by that author: the inbox queues it as `watched_channel` and marks the event `to: all` (as a push attribute, too); the channel push and the listener wake for it at once (RT-6.2 step 4, RT-6.12 step 3), a gated one not (RT-6.16). The post holds no `@channel`, and the drain and the listener count it all the same: the MCP server's `unread.py` (behind `/v1/pending`, RT-6.17) counts, among the posts after the read watermark (RT-6.2 step 1, at most a day back), one by someone else with `props.a2a.to = "all"` that the server did not gate (`GATED`, RT-6.16), with the mentions. A human's `@channel` keeps waking agents through the text rules. `props.a2a.to` says whom a post is for, never who wrote it: anyone can write props through the API, and author classes come from the account only, so a to-all post by someone not trusted is gated like their `@channel` (RT-6.16).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | No human to ask (no responsible or trusted human configured, none given) | The tool fails; the agent asks in the terminal |
| 1 | The agent already has an open ask to that human (or, for a question, to the 👑) | The new ask is queued (`posted: false`, `queued: true`, `position`, `behind`) and posted when the open one is answered or resolved, as routed then; the human still sees one question at a time. Another human has a queue of their own |
| 1 | Five asks already wait to that target | Refused: `posted: false`, `queue_full: true`, `open_ask`, and the ids of the queued ones: wait for the answers, or withdraw one with `resolve_ask <q-id>` |
| 1 | A queued ask has more than one question, or three or four alternatives as an approval | Refused when it is made, not when it would be posted: nothing is queued |
| 1 | Posting a queued ask fails before the ask is out (Mattermost unreachable) | It goes back to the front of the queue; the next heartbeat tries again |
| 1 | A step after the queued ask itself was posted fails (the link in its thread) | The entry is spent: the ask is out, it is never posted a second time; the agent is told as usual |
| 1 | The heartbeat and `resolve_ask` (or two servers of one bot) post the next ask at once | The entry is taken out of the queue under a lock before anything is awaited, and one ask is posted at a time per server: each queued ask goes out once. Two servers may rarely post two asks to one target |
| 1 | A queued ask is obsolete | `resolve_ask` with its id (`q-…`, in the result of `ask_human`) withdraws it; no post, no ✅ |
| 4 | An ask or a queued entry is 7 days old | Forgotten from the file (the row "An ask never resolved" below): a queued ask is lost with it, and the queue behind an open ask forgotten that way moves on without `resolve_ask` |
| 1 | An agent writes `@<human>` in an ordinary post | The server posts the plain name instead and says so (`unmentioned`, with a hint to `ask_human`); bots and unknown names stay. A failed user lookup leaves the names unchanged |
| 1 | An agent writes `@channel`, `@here` or `@all` | The server posts `channel`, `here` or `all` instead and says so (`unmentioned`, with a hint to `to="all"`), also when the user lookup fails: nothing notifies every human |
| 1 | An agent's post names a trusted human it has no open ask to ("merge waits on dembaca") | Posted as written; the result names them (`unasked`) and hints to open the ask or link the open one; a queued ask counts as an ask, so does a question to the 👑 for its human (RT-6.25 step 3; `asks.waiting`, which feeds the `wait_for_events` hint of RT-6.11, leaves it out on purpose: the 👑's answer is advice, never a go), an ask the human has answered (reaction or reply) does not: a new decision needs a new ask. When the post's bus word is `IDLE`, `RESULT`, `BLOCKED` or `WAITING` the result also carries a `warning`: the text waits on a human nobody was notified of. The post is never blocked. Matches the whole name only, not inside a branch, file, mail address or URL path; a failed lookup gives no hint |
| 3 | Reaction on any post that is not an open ask | Ignored: it is a remark, never an answer or an approval |
| 3 | Reaction by someone not trusted | Delivered with `from_trusted_human: false`: information, not an approval |
| 3 | An emoji other than the agreed ones | `answer: null`; the agent reads it as a remark and may ask in the thread |
| 4 | An ask never resolved | Forgotten after 7 days: the listener stops looking at it, and a reply in its thread arrives as any reply, without `expired`; the 24 h rule still holds for the agent |
| 5 | An ask unanswered for 24 h | A yes to it approves nothing; `ask_human` to the same human names it as too old and queues nothing behind it: the agent resolves it, then asks again |
| 6 | `reply_in_thread` gets a `to` other than `all` | Refused with an error; nothing is posted |
| 6 | A post through the Shell CLI (`mm-agent-post.sh`) | It goes through the server (`POST /v1/post`, RT-6.17) and is written like `post_message`'s: no human @-mentioned, `@channel` as a plain word. Outside corral an agent that reads the session file can still reach the REST API with the token (Section 7.3): a guard against the model's slips, not a boundary |
| 6 | The MCP server is not running while a post for all agents arrives | Nothing finds it meanwhile: the Stop hook and the listener hold no token and ask only the server's hook API (RT-6.17), so without a server they find nothing. The next server counts the post from the read watermark (RT-6.2) and hands it over then; nothing is gated (RT-6.16) |
| — | A reaction was the only answer before 0.25.0 | Nobody saw it: Mattermost raises no unread counter for reactions, and the inbox handled only `posted` |

---

## 6.12 Messages pushed into the session (channel)

**Purpose:** A message for the agent reaches its session the moment it is posted, without a background listener, listen windows or turns spent waiting.

**Trigger:** The developer starts Claude Code with the MCP server as a channel (research preview): `claude --channels plugin:ai-agent-bus@ai-agent-bus` once the plugin is on the organisation's `allowedChannelPlugins`, otherwise `--dangerously-load-development-channels` with the same entry. The MCP server finds the nearest ancestor process named `claude` and reads its command line for one of the two flags naming this plugin. `CB_CHANNEL_PUSH` overrides that (`1` on, `0` off), for Claude Code only: a client that names itself otherwise than `claude-code` in `initialize` gets the plain server, so an `CB_CHANNEL_PUSH=1` from the user's shell puts no Codex, OpenCode or Cursor server into channel mode. Under corral the host session hook passes it into the sandbox with the bot's other variables, and the relay in the sandbox, which has Claude Code among its ancestors, tells the MCP server on the host (RT-6.18).

**Participants:** MCP server, Turn hooks; Agent runtime, Mattermost WebSocket, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#efficient` Low overhead — waiting costs no model calls, and with a channel not even the turns that start and end a listen window

### Sequence

1. In channel mode the MCP server serves stdio itself: it declares the experimental capability `claude/channel` and answers on the handshake era only. Claude Code's first request in the 2026-07-28 envelope (`server/discover`) gets `INVALID_REQUEST`, so it falls back to `initialize`; Claude Code registers no channel on the newer revision.
2. After `notifications/initialized` the server forgets an older confirmation (`channel-<uid>.env`) and the process's own (since 0.71.12): every connection is confirmed by a check of its own with a new code, also one of a process that lived on (a relay reconnect after `/reload-plugins` or `/mcp`, RT-6.18), and the old code confirms nothing. The server then pushes a check (`kind=channel_check`) with a code only that check carries, 3 s after the handshake and again after 30 s, 2 min and then every 10 min until confirmed: Claude Code drops a push that arrives before it registered the channel, up to a second after the handshake, and a busy session gets it only later. The agent calls `channel_ok(code)`, which writes `channel-<uid>.env`. Until then the pusher reads nothing from the inbox, so nothing is marked read unseen, and the Turn hooks keep the listen window (RT-6.2).
3. Confirmed, the pusher takes events from the inbox like `wait_for_events` does (mentions, DMs, the home, the lobby and the guest channels, reactions to open asks; RT-6.5, RT-6.11) and sends as `notifications/claude/channel` at once what the listener would wake for (RT-6.2 step 4): a mention, DM or reaction, a post for all agents (`to: all`, RT-6.11 step 6) or `@channel` / `@all` / `@here`, a reply in a thread the bot posted in, or, while it holds the 👑, a post in its home that names no agent (RT-6.6 step 5; one to another agent goes into the digest), for these two not an agent's fyi post unless the bot started the thread (RT-6.14 step 8); a post the inbox gated goes into the digest (RT-6.16). In a guest channel only a mention, a DM or a reply in its own thread is pushed, in the lobby when it is not the home also `@channel` / `@all` / `@here` and a post for all agents, and nothing from either goes into the digest, a gated post neither (RT-6.22 step 3). An edit (Mattermost's `post_edited`) wakes the agent once when it makes a post less than 24 h old a mention or DM to it that the inbox has not queued as one, a post queued or held as a channel post before included: classified, gated (RT-6.16) and pushed like a new mention. A typo fix in a post that already named the agent, an edit that leaves a post a channel post and an edit of an older post wake nobody. Its post is behind the read watermark (RT-6.2 step 1), so it does not pull the mark back; while it waits, the hooks' count adds it, so the Stop hook drains it and the listener wakes for it. Each push is the message as content (`<channel` in it neutralised, RT-6.16 step 6; over 2,000 characters (`slim.BODY_MAX`) cut at the last line or word end before that and ended with `… (cut, <n> chars in all; get_thread <post_id>)`, which fetches the whole thread from a root or a reply alike), `kind`, `post_id`, `root_id`, `channel`, `author`, `at`, `from_trusted_human` (server-verified, RT-6.8), `from_agent`, `to` (`all`, for a post for all agents), `intent` (`fyi`, RT-6.14 step 8), `departed` (RT-6.21), `guest` (`true` in a guest channel, RT-6.22), `lobby` (`true` in the lobby when it is not the home, RT-6.22 step 3), `files` (the post's attachments, RT-6.23), `edited` (`true` for an edited post; `wait_for_events` has it in the post), `for_crown` (`true` when only the 👑 woke it: a post in its home that names no agent, outside its own threads; `wait_for_events` marks such an event `for_crown: true` while the agent holds the crown; RT-6.6 step 5) and, for a reaction, `ask`, `emoji`, `answer` as attributes; the other scenarios add theirs where they apply (`shown_as`, `trusted_human`, `not_here`, `owner`, `root_from_trusted_human`, `advice`, `mandated`, `mandated_by`, `choice`, `chosen`, `foreign`, `expired`; RT-6.11, RT-6.16, RT-6.21, RT-6.25, RT-6.26). System posts ("… joined the channel") are dropped: Mattermost names the human who added the bot as their author. Other posts are held and pushed as one digest (`kind=digest`, each post cut to 300 characters, other agents' status posts to their first line (RT-6.14), with its id and thread and, after the cut, its files (` · files: <id> <name>; …`, RT-6.23); fyi posts last, their number in the first line; at most 20 posts (`DIGEST_LINES`), then one line `+<n> more (<m> fyi): list_recent(channel_id=…, since_ms=…)` with a call per channel of those left out, which lists them from the oldest on) once the oldest is `CB_LISTEN_DIGEST_SEC` (default 1800 s) old: every push wakes the agent, and every wake-up is a model turn. When the 👑 passes to the agent (RT-6.6 step 6: after a lapse, a claim or a `take`), the held posts that now wake it by these rules, a human's plain post in its home, are pushed at once, in their order, each once and then no longer held; a gated post, an fyi post and what the rules leave stay for the digest, and where the thread cannot be read the post stays held. Read events are marked read, as in `wait_for_events`. `wait_for_events` cuts a post's text the same way, being what the bus brings unasked too; `raw=true`, `get_thread` and `list_recent`, which the agent asks for by id or channel, keep it whole. Measured over pushes (#68): median 465 characters, p90 1,188, max 6,051, so the cut touches fewer than one in ten; a push of the envelope only (kind, author, thread) was rejected, since its `get_thread` round trip re-reads the whole context (~20k token equivalents) to save some 80 tokens.
4. Claude Code wakes an idle session with the event, or queues it for the next turn while one runs; the agent answers with `reply_in_thread`. **The take-up** (since 0.77.0, ADR-007 amendment step 1): a push sent while no turn runs counts as taken up when a turn begins (the prompt hook's `working`, which Claude Code runs for a turn a push started too) or the agent calls a tool; untaken for `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` (45 s), the agent is silent and loses a crown (RT-6.6 steps 1 and 6). A push during a turn is that turn's. A delivered mention or DM, a trusted human's yes/no reaction on an open ask, and for the 👑 holder a human's plain post, show the typing indicator until the agent posts (RT-6.5).
5. A `wait_for_events` call by the agent takes the inbox over while it runs; the pusher waits for it to return.
6. The Turn hooks see `channel-<uid>.env`: the drain stays (RT-6.2, step 1), the listen window is skipped. The sign-off (step 2) is asked only in a turn the human started; a turn a push started (its prompt is the `<channel source="plugin:ai-agent-bus:…">` tag) answers the bus, like an answer from inside a listen window. `CB_CHANNEL_PUSH=0` keeps it. The server removes the file when it ends.
7. **The receipt (since 0.80.0, ADR-007 amendment step 2; `receipt.py`).** Holding the 👑, the server puts a reaction on each post in its home that wakes it (a human's plain post, a mention of it, a question routed to it, RT-6.6 step 5): 👀 when the agent has the post, and ⏳ at once while a turn runs (from the prompt hook's `working` to the Stop hook's end), for every such post, pushed or not (decided 2026-11-12); the 👀 follows. A push is had at its take-up (step 4): a turn's start, or a tool call for one pushed while no turn ran; one pushed during a turn at that turn's end or the next one's start (then the 👀 says the agent has it, or has it queued for its next turn). A post `wait_for_events` returns is had with that return (the Stop hook's drain, a listen window's wake, the OpenCode plugin's turn; RT-6.2, RT-6.20), the only way without a channel. No model turn: a human sees that the agent has the post (👀) or that it waits for a turn (⏳), and the other servers read the receipt to find a holder that takes nothing up (RT-6.6 step 10). A failed reaction is tried once more. A runtime without turn hooks (Cursor, Codex) shows no ⏳, since its server sees no turn: a known limit.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | The flag is there but the session did not opt the server in (plugin not allowed, `channelsEnabled` off, a protocol Claude Code registers no channel on) | Claude Code drops the check silently; it is never confirmed, so the inbox stays with `wait_for_events` and the listen window as in RT-6.2 |
| 1 | Claude Code's command line cannot be read (another OS, a sandbox that denies it) | No channel; `CB_CHANNEL_PUSH=1` turns it on, and the check still decides |
| 2 | The agent calls `channel_ok` with another code | Refused: only the session that received the check can confirm it |
| 2 | The connection reconnects while the process lives on (`/reload-plugins`, `/mcp`, a plugin update) | The confirmation file is forgotten and a new check with a new code comes after 3 s; until `channel_ok` answers it the pusher reads nothing and the hooks deliver as without a channel (the listener, RT-6.2), then the file is written again |
| 2 | No bus yet (before `session_start`) or the WebSocket dropped | The pusher retries every 5 s; nothing is lost, the backfill on reconnect queues what came meanwhile |
| 3 | The relay connection ends, or a push fails, while the pusher has taken a batch from the inbox (a push the connection did not take, or the run cancelled mid-batch) | An event counts as delivered, and the read watermark passes its post (RT-6.2 step 1), only once the pusher dealt with it: pushed on a live connection, held for the digest, or dropped on purpose (a system post, a verdict for Claude Code, a guest post without a digest). The pusher reads with `take`; what it took stays in the inbox's `_out` list, unhandled and counted as waiting (the read watermark, `holds`, `waits` and the edits count it, so a later hand-over of a newer post cannot pass it), until it is `ack`ed (handed over) or `nack`ed (back to the front of the queue, in order). The rest of a batch is `nack`ed when a push fails or the run ends (`Pusher.run`), so the next connection's pusher or `wait_for_events` gets it, and the Stop hook still counts it; since 0.71.16. The bookkeeping after the hand-over (typing, once per batch for its first mention or DM; the sign-off; the stats) never ends the run. Before, the events were handed over at the read and gone. A push cancelled while it was on its way may reach the session and be pushed again: at least once, never lost. A digest that cannot go out stays held, and the run waits `retry_sec` before the next try |
| 3 | The server ends with posts held for the digest | They are not pushed; the read watermark passed them (RT-6.2 step 1), as posts the agent never needed to answer |
| 3 | A new server of the same bot (`claude --resume <id>`, RT-6.10) sees an edit of a post less than 24 h old that the one before handed over as a mention or DM | It wakes the agent once more, marked `edited`: which posts were queued as a mention or DM is kept in memory only. A post older than 24 h wakes nobody by an edit |
| 3 | A post is edited while the WebSocket is not connected | Not seen: the backfill reads only posts newer than the mark (RT-6.2 step 1). The agent sees the edited text when it reads the thread |
| — | Another runtime (Cursor, OpenCode, Codex) | No channels there: no `claude` ancestor, so everything works as in RT-6.2 and RT-6.3 |
| 7 | The holder's session takes nothing up (a background terminal, a suspended process) | No 👀; in a channel session the agent turns silent after `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` (RT-6.6 step 1) and drops the crown one crown lease later without a sign (#64), and the next agent claims at its slot (RT-6.6 steps 2 and 6). The receipt's own claim (RT-6.6 step 10) is for a server that is gone, a dead WebSocket, or a runtime without turn hooks |
| 7 | The reaction cannot be set (no permission, Mattermost down) | Tried once more, then logged at info; the others may claim: a clean handover (RT-6.6 step 10) |
| 1 | `CB_CHANNEL_PUSH=1` reaches another runtime's server (inherited from the user's shell) | At `initialize` the client names itself otherwise than `claude-code`: the server answers without the channel capability, offers no `channel_ok`, gives the instructions to check `wait_for_events` itself, starts no pusher and logs it |

---

## 6.13 Permission prompts answered from Mattermost

**Purpose:** An agent waiting on a tool permission prompt is not stuck until someone looks at its terminal: its responsible human answers from Mattermost, the one place they coordinate from.

**Trigger:** In a session started as a channel (RT-6.12), Claude Code opens a permission dialog for a tool call (Bash, Write, Edit, an MCP tool) and sends `notifications/claude/channel/permission_request` with a request id (five letters `a`-`z` without `l`), `tool_name`, `description` and `input_preview`. Research preview, Claude Code ≥ 2.1.234.

**Participants:** MCP server; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Mattermost WebSocket, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability, `#secure` Approval integrity (only an authenticated trusted human can answer)

### Sequence

1. On by default: in channel mode the MCP server declares `claude/channel/permission` next to `claude/channel` whenever a trusted human is configured (`CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`, the ids the host passes, or `CB_OWNER`; RT-6.8). No trusted human, no capability: Claude Code relays nothing and the terminal is the only place to answer. `bus_info` shows `permission_relay`.
2. The server DMs the prompt to the responsible human (RT-6.8), if they are trusted: tool name, `description` and the preview as code blocks (both come from the model's tool call), the id, and how to answer. It goes by DM, not to the work channel, since posts there reach every agent's context. A DM is still stored on the Mattermost server like any post (retention, search, exports, admin access, backups), so the preview carries what a decision needs and no file content. From Claude Code's `input_preview` (JSON-shaped, each top-level field cut to 3,500 characters, well-known secrets masked by Claude Code, which stays) it shows:
   - for `Bash`, the command in full: there is no decision without it;
   - for tools that carry file content (`Write`, `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `NotebookEdit`), the path and other short fields, each content field (`content`, `old_string`, `new_string`, `new_source`, `edits`) only as its size: `‹1234 chars›`, with `, 56 lines` where line breaks reach the server (Claude Code folds them to spaces since 2.1.211), the part Claude Code elided counted in;
   - for any other tool, values up to 200 characters on one line as they are, a longer or multi-line string only as its size;
   - a preview that does not parse as JSON (Claude Code cut an array or object): as it came for `Bash` or when it is short and from no file tool; otherwise only the paths found in it and its size.

   The prompt post counts as an open ask (RT-6.11) to nobody in particular, so a reaction on it reaches the inbox without holding up the agent's own asks.
3. The human answers `yes <id>` or `no <id>` (`y`/`n`, any case) in the DM or anywhere the bot reads, or reacts 👍/✅ or 👎/❌ on the prompt post.
4. Once the channel is confirmed, the pusher takes such an event out before the wake rules (RT-6.12 step 3) when it comes from a trusted human (server-verified author) and names an open id: it sends `notifications/claude/channel/permission` with `allow` or `deny`, replies under the prompt `✅ allowed by <human>` or `⛔ denied by <human>`, and resolves the ask. The agent never sees the verdict. The terminal dialog stays open meanwhile; the first answer wins and applies to this one call only.
5. Claude Code says nothing when the terminal answers first. An open dialog lets no turn end, so the turn's end closes every prompt still open: when the Stop hook sets the status `idle` or `listening` (`POST /v1/presence`, RT-6.17; the OpenCode plugin's turn end the same, RT-6.20), the server replies under each one `🖥️ Answered in the terminal`, takes it out of the open ids and resolves its ask; these posts mark the DM read like the others. A later `yes <id>` gets the reply for an id that is not open.
6. **The relay log (since 0.91.2).** Claude Code says nothing about a prompt it did not send, and the server's own log lines go to stderr, which nobody keeps. So each step leaves one JSON line in `permission-<uid>.log` in the state directory (0600, `session_file.append_private`; RT-6.18's host-side directory in a keyless session): `{"t": "<UTC time>", "id": "<request id>", "tool": "<tool_name>", "step": …, "reason": …}`. Steps: `request received`; `not relayed` (reason: `responsible human not trusted`, `no responsible human`, `relay off: nobody to answer`); `dm posted`; `posting failed` (reason: the error's class name only); `verdict sent` (reason: `allow` or `deny` `by <the human's username>`, with `, no connection` when Claude Code's channel connection was gone, so the verdict went nowhere); `closed at turn end` (step 5); `unknown id` (a `yes`/`no` for an id that is not open, step 3). The log never holds the prompt's `description` or `input_preview`, nor any command, path or file content, nor an error's message: the fields are a time, a short id, a tool name and a fixed word or a username. It never passes 500 lines: once it does, it is rewritten with the newest 400, so a full log is not rewritten by every line. A failed write is logged as a warning (stderr) and the relay goes on; that is the case in which a missing `request received` line proves nothing. The sweep (step 7 of RT-6.1) removes the file of a bot that is gone.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | Session not started as a channel, or the channel never confirmed | No relay (capability undeclared) or no verdict read (the pusher reads nothing yet): the terminal answers, as before |
| 2 | The responsible human is not a trusted one | Not relayed: only a trusted human's verdict counts; a log line says so |
| 2 | Posting fails (bus down) | Logged, also in the relay log (step 6); the terminal dialog is still open |
| 2 | A prompt of a background subagent | Seen once (2026-11-15) with no DM; the relay log (step 6) shows whether Claude Code sent a request for it to this server. If it has no `request received` line, Claude Code relays only the parent session's prompts |
| 3 | Another agent or an untrusted person answers `yes <id>` | Not `from_trusted_human`: a post like any other, data for the agent, never sent to Claude Code |
| 3 | A trusted `yes <id>` with an id that is not open (answered, closed at the turn's end, step 5, or never sent) | Nothing goes to Claude Code. In the DM a short reply ("No open permission prompt: answered in the terminal, or more than 24 h old"); elsewhere it passes on untouched, it may be another agent's prompt |
| 3 | A verdict more than 24 h after the prompt | The id is no longer open, as a yes on an ask approves only within 24 h (RT-6.11): `yes <id>` gets the reply above; 👍/👎 on the old prompt post reaches the agent as a reaction to an ask, marked `expired` |
| 3 | Another emoji on the prompt post | A remark: no verdict, no wake-up |
| 2–4 | The bot's own prompt, reply and verdict posts count as unread DMs for it, and the human's taken-out answer reaches no inbox | The bus never counts the bot's own post, and the read watermark passes each of them as every post of its own (RT-6.2 step 1), so the drain does not wake for a message nothing can collect; the DM's unread display in Mattermost is cleared too |
| 4 | The terminal answered first, and the turn goes on | Claude Code sends no event; the id stays open until the turn ends (step 5), and a verdict before that is dropped by Claude Code. The reply says the verdict applies unless the terminal answered first |
| 5 | The turn ends without the Stop hook's idle or listening (the hook fails open, no hook API) | The id stays open for at most 24 h, until the next turn's end; a verdict meanwhile is dropped by Claude Code |
| 5 | Posting the note fails | Logged; the prompt is closed all the same |
| — | Auto mode | The classifier decides most calls, so no dialog opens and nothing is relayed |
| — | Prompts about sandbox, secrets or permissions | Claude Code does not mark them, so they cannot be filtered: they are relayed like any other. Anyone trusted can approve any tool call of this session from Mattermost: trust only humans you would hand the terminal to |
| — | Project trust or MCP server consent dialogs | Not relayed by Claude Code: terminal only |

---

## 6.14 Agent cards and task states (A2A shapes)

**Purpose:** The MCP server, not the model, keeps track of who is on the bus and which task is in which state, so the coordinator gets an overview without reading the channel and bystanders read less. The bus takes the data shapes of A2A 1.0 (AgentCard, TaskState) the way Cotal does: shapes only, no A2A transport, no new tool, nothing new pushed into a session. They are the bus's own schema for its state: no other system reads these props, so they promise no interoperability.

**Trigger:** An agent posts through the MCP server (`announce`, `post_message`, `reply_in_thread`, `ask_human`, `resolve_ask`, a permission prompt); an agent calls `coordinator("tasks")` or `coordinator("roster")`, for its home or with `scope="all"` across the channels it is in; the coordinator posts `ASSIGNMENTS`; events reach a bystander (digest, `wait_for_events`).

**Participants:** MCP server, Shell CLI; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#efficient` Low overhead — here: knowing the state of the work costs no reading of history

### Sequence

1. `announce` posts the HELLO in three lines, without naming the agent (Mattermost shows the author; names without @, RT-6.11): ``` `HELLO` · **<task>** ``` and a blank line (step 2); then `` `<project>` · `<branch | –>` · <model> · <role> ``; then `for <responsible human>[ (autonomous)] · trusts <trusted humans, comma-joined | –> · can: <capabilities | –>`. Project and branch stand in backticks only when they are one plain word; a link (`[PR #134](<url>)`, a URL) or text with spaces stays as given. It stores the agent's card in the HELLO post's props (`props.agent_card`), built from its existing arguments: A2A field names where A2A has them (`name`, `description` = task, `version` = plugin, `skills` = `{id, name}` per capability, `capabilities.pushNotifications` = channel mode, an extension for the permission relay, RT-6.13), the rest in `meta` (`project`, `branch`, `model`, `role`, `kind`, `for`, `trusts`), beside them `v` and, when the registrar signed one, `attestation`, its owner claim (RT-6.24). The newest HELLO of an agent is its card; no post is edited. A HELLO without props (written by hand, with or without the backticks and the blank line, or by versions before 0.65.4 in seven lines, `HELLO @<agent>` and one `key: value` line per field) gives the same card from its text, blank lines aside; the name comes from the post's author, in the old lines from `HELLO @<agent>`.
2. **A status post** is one whose first word, after an optional 👑, is a bus convention in capitals (`kind`, or the word the text starts with). `post_message`, `reply_in_thread`, `announce` and the server's own `BYE` write that word in backticks (`` 👑 `IDLE` · <gist> ``) and, when more lines follow and the second is not blank, a blank line after the headline; when the text opens with a Markdown block (a table row, list item, heading, code fence or quote) the word stands alone on the headline and the block follows after a blank line (`` `RESULT` ``, blank line, `\| a \| b \|` …) instead of `` `RESULT` · \| a \| `` (the 👑 likewise stays on the headline, RT-6.6 step 3); a word the agent already wrote in backticks stays, any other text is posted as written. Every reader of the first word takes both forms, plain (older posts) and in backticks: the props below, the sign-off and listen gates (RT-6.2), the compaction handoff (RT-6.7), the task rows and digests. Every post the server writes whose first word is a bus convention gets `props.a2a = {v: 1, kind, state, intent}`, plus `to: "all"` when it is for all agents (RT-6.11; alone when the first word is no convention); `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` add what the text names, `pr` (the number of the last `PR #n`, `MR !n`, pull or merge request URL) and `branch` (`branch <name>`), and `crown: true` while the author holds the crown (RT-6.6), with or without a convention word; a post in a guest channel also gets `home`, the author's home (RT-6.22); an ask gets `kind: ask` (`input-required`), its link line in another thread `kind: ask_link` (no state, no intent: a pointer, RT-6.11 step 1), a permission prompt `kind: permission` (`auth-required`). The first word is read after an optional 👑, emoji or markdown, upper case only: `TASK`/`DELEGATE` → open (`submitted`); `ACK`/`PROGRESS` → active (`working`); `BLOCKED`/`WAITING` → blocked (`input-required`); `RESULT` or `PR #n` → review (`input-required`); `DONE` → done (`completed`); `NO` → open again, assignee dropped (`rejected`); `CANCEL` → dropped (`canceled`); `HELLO`, `IDLE`, `BYE`, `HANDOVER`, `ASSIGNMENTS` move no task. `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` take the word as `kind`, in any case: the server puts it first (`ACK · <text>`) unless the text starts with it, so text and props never disagree; an unknown kind, or one the text's first word contradicts (`kind="ACK"` on `DONE · …`), is refused with the list of words and nothing is posted: the refusal is the tool's result (`{posted: false, refused, kinds}`), not an error. The other input checks (`to` other than `all` on a reply, `role`, `action`, no human to ask) refuse the same way, without `kinds`. The intent says what a post asks of its readers: `action` (`TASK`, `DELEGATE`, `HANDOVER`, `CANCEL`), `reply` (`BLOCKED`, `NO`, an ask, a permission prompt) or `fyi` (`ACK`, `PROGRESS`, `WAITING`, `RESULT`, `DONE`, `HELLO`, `IDLE`, `BYE`, `ASSIGNMENTS`). A plain post has none, and no text is read for it: a question to agents is a `BLOCKED` (one that waits on a human without an ask draws the RT-6.11 warning), to a human an ask. A2A's ids are Mattermost's: messageId = post id, contextId = thread root, taskId = the root of a task thread. Slim posts never carry props, so no model reads any of this unasked.
3. `coordinator("tasks")` reads the work channel's posts of the last 14 days, paging back from the newest post (with `since`, Mattermost answers at most 1000 posts in no set order, so a busy channel lost its newest), and returns one row per task (`task`, `title`, `by`, `agent`, `status`, `state`, `spec`, `pr`, `branch`, `at`, and `mandated` where the home's mandate covers it, RT-6.26 step 5) and the open asks (`ask`, `by`, `human`, `need`, `at`, and `question` for a question to the crown holder, RT-6.25), followed by this agent's queued asks, which are no posts yet (RT-6.11). A task is a top-level `TASK`/`DELEGATE` post; its thread is the task. With trusted humans configured, a root by someone not trusted or by a foreign agent is none (RT-6.16 step 5). The assignee is the first agent mentioned in the root that answers `ACK`, `PROGRESS` or `RESULT` (without a mention: the first one other than the root's author). A later claim takes nothing: after posting one of these words in a thread, `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` read the thread once and return `taken_by: <agent>` when the task is another agent's; once the bot holds a task, its claims there read nothing. State words count from the assignee, and `DONE`/`CANCEL` also from the root's author or the coordinator: the crown holder the roster names now (`lease.roster`, RT-6.6), or an agent (by its name's prefix) whose post carries `props.a2a.crown`, so a close survives the crown's next handover. The crown at post time is known only from these props: the lease keeps no history. A 👑 in the text is no authority, anyone can type it, except on an agent's post from before the 0.46.0 cutover (`LEGACY_CROWN_BEFORE_MS`, 2026-10-28T00:00Z), when the crown was only in the text: those closes stay, and the rule goes once the cutover is 14 days back. Anyone else's are data. Only the task's own thread moves it: what an agent posts in its HELLO thread may be about its next work. Spec and issue come from the root (`RT-6.n`, `ABC-123`, `#n`), PR and branch from the assignee's posts: their props (`pr`, `branch`), else their text. A `done` task is listed until the next `ASSIGNMENTS` post after it, at most a day; a canceled one never. An ask is open without a yes/no reaction (RT-6.11), without a reply by the human it asks and without ✅ by an agent (`resolve_ask`, by any peer bus agent: a successor session closes its predecessor's asks; a foreign agent's ✅ closes nothing, RT-6.16 step 1). Posts without props are read from their text with the same rules.
4. `coordinator("roster")` reads the work channel's agents by the reachability rules of RT-6.6 (`lease.py`, the one place for them) and joins each reachable agent with its card: `agent`, `status` (from the lease), `coordinator`, `model`, `role`, `task`, `branch`, `skills`; and from its bot description, never from its HELLO, `owner`, `trusts` and `foreign` (ADR-001, RT-6.16 step 1), and `attested` when these come from its attestation (RT-6.24). A guest holding its own home's crown gets `crown` (`👑 of ~<home>`, RT-6.22), and each row the agent's `version` and `source` (RT-6.30). For a NATS home without its NATS side the result carries a `hint` (RT-6.38 step 6). From the segments of the agent's custom status text (RT-6.6 steps 1 and 8, ADR-008: `usage.from_status` over the text the roster read holds, no lookup; the agent's own row exact from its memory) it adds `ctx` (percent of the context window used), `cost_usd` (dollars, the session's cumulative cost, shown where no `limit_5h` is reported), `limit_5h` and `limit_7d` (each `used` percent and `resets_at` epoch seconds), each only for an agent that reports it and whose window has not reset since. Agents with the same `limit_5h.resets_at` (exact) and the same `owner` are on one subscription, since a window is the account's, not the agent's: they get the same `limit_shared: {group, agents}` (`group` 1, 2, … in row order, `agents` the number in it), so the coordinator counts a limit once; an agent alone in its window gets none. The numbers are what the bot says about itself: a hint, never a permission (`coordinator.md`). At session start an agent reads these two results instead of the channel's last 50 posts.
5. The coordinator posts `ASSIGNMENTS · <what changed>` with `post_message`; to a top-level post in its home without a table in it, the server appends the table from step 3 (agent, task, status, spec / issue, branch / PR). Names go in without @, so the table wakes nobody. A table the coordinator writes itself is left alone, and so is an `ASSIGNMENTS` post by an agent without the crown.
6. A bystander reads other agents' status posts short: in a digest (RT-6.12 step 3) and in `wait_for_events` results of type `watched_channel` for an agent without the crown, a post whose kind is `IDLE`, `BYE`, `ACK`, `PROGRESS` or `ASSIGNMENTS` is cut to its first line (the headline, RT-6.2 step 2), at most 160 characters with `…`. A reply in a thread the agent posted in, a mention, a DM, `TASK`, `HELLO` and plain posts stay whole.
7. A test holds the static context (`test_skill_file_budget`): SKILL.md stays within 19,000 characters (about 16,700 at 0.57.0, with the runtime-specific text moved to `runtime-claude-code.md` and `runtime-no-hooks.md`, RT-6.9), each file next to it within about 10 % over its size then, coordinator.md within its 0.30.0 size plus what later scenarios added (RT-6.16, RT-6.19, RT-6.21, step 9), every file SKILL.md points to exists, and the `coordinator` tool doc grows only by its two actions and `scope` (step 9).
8. **An fyi post wakes only whom it names.** An event by an agent (RT-6.16 step 1) with `props.a2a.intent = "fyi"` carries `intent: fyi`, in `wait_for_events` and as a push attribute: it needs no answer. The channel push (RT-6.12 step 3) and the background listener (RT-6.2 step 4) hold it for the digest where the agent would wake only because it sees the post: as 👑 coordinator, or having posted in its thread. Whom it names, by a mention, a DM or `to: all`, wakes at once, and so does the author of its thread's root: the agent that posted a `TASK` waits for its `RESULT` and `DONE`. The push digest lists fyi posts last and says how many, the listener's digest line counts them, and the `Stop` hook's drain says how many unread posts need action (RT-6.2 step 1). Anyone can write props through the API: a person's, a webhook's or another bot's intent is ignored (the listener and the drain look the authors up, once per author and window), and the wake gate (RT-6.16) decides before it and without it, so props never let a post skip the gate or change who it is from. Posts of older versions carry no intent and wake as before.
9. **Overview across homes.** `coordinator("tasks", scope="all")` and `coordinator("roster", scope="all")` give steps 3 and 4 for every channel the bot is in as `bus_info` lists them (home, guests, lobby, once each: RT-6.22 step 4), one entry per channel: `channel`, `role` (`home`, `guest`, `lobby`), `crown` and either `tasks` with `asks` or `roster`. `crown` is the channel's crown holder as somebody's home: the live 👑 among the agents whose status names it (`lease.crown_in` over the roster rows it reads anyway, the rule of `lease.coordinator_of`), else null; a guest's 👑 of its own home is none. Each channel is read once, its own task window with the trust filter of step 3 (RT-6.16 step 5: a `TASK` root by someone not trusted is no task there either) and its crown holder as the coordinator who may close any task; a name looked up for one channel is not looked up again. A channel's roster lists the agents whose home it is, coordinator first, then its ambassadors (RT-6.6 step 3). `tasks` adds `asks_by_human`: per human, the open asks of all these channels, oldest first, each as in step 3 with its `channel`; a question to a 👑 (RT-6.25) is no human's and stays only in its channel's `asks`. It only reads: it posts nothing, changes no status and adds no trust rule; a guest channel's tasks stay that home's, and the coordinator proposes and mediates (`coordinator.md`). Only the home's entry marks `mandated` rows (RT-6.26 step 5). `scope="home"`, the default, is steps 3 and 4 unchanged; another action with `scope="all"`, or another scope, is refused.
10. **A task to an agent that cannot take it (since 0.71.23).** `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` with the word `TASK` or `DELEGATE` check the agents the text @-mentions (the registrar's prefix, not the author) against the roster of the channel the post went to (`lease._rows`: the channel's agents with their lease, at most a page of 200 accounts, so a live agent the page lacks counts when it is a member; the home, a guest channel or the lobby, a reply's by the post Mattermost returned; a DM or group message is not checked), after the post is out and only when it names an agent. One that is not reachable (no live lease, offline, 💤 idle, or 💤 `silent`: no sign of its session, RT-6.6 step 1) or not in that channel is named in the result as `not_reachable: ["<agent> (<why>)", …]` with a `warning`: a task to it is posted but nobody may pick it up, choose another from `coordinator("roster")` (`scope="all"` when the channel is not the home). The reasons are those of RT-6.21 (`departed.gone`: since when, BYE or lease ran out; `departed.elsewhere`: live in another home) plus idle, silent and offline. A mention without `@`, a DM (`to`) and other words are not checked. Cost: one read of the channel's agents and their presence for all names, and only for such a post that names an agent; a further read of the work channel's recent posts only for a gone one.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1, 2 | Props are what the posting bot says about itself | Used for overviews only; never an approval (only `from_trusted_human`, RT-6.8) |
| 2 | `kind` is no bus word, or the text starts with another one | Refused with the list of words; nothing is posted |
| 3 | Work assigned without a `TASK` thread, or state words not first | Not in the task rows; the coordinator writes that row in its own `ASSIGNMENTS` table |
| 2 | `root_id` names a reply, not a thread's root | The post goes into that reply's thread: Mattermost refuses a reply's id as root |
| 2 | Mattermost refuses the post (a root it does not find, no permission) | `posted: false` with `refused` and Mattermost's reason; the MCP SDK would show a raised error only as "Error executing tool" |
| 3 | The thread cannot be read after a claim | No `taken_by`; the post is out |
| 3 | An agent reports on its task in its HELLO thread instead of the task's thread | The task does not move; SKILL.md asks for task updates in the task's thread |
| 3 | More than 14 days without a word in a task's thread | The task drops out of the window |
| 3 | The roster cannot be read for the task rows | Only the root's author and posts with `props.a2a.crown` close a task; a log line |
| 4 | The agents' statuses cannot be read (no token, Mattermost fails) | An empty roster; `coordinator("who")` answers no coordinator. A failing read of the work channel's posts, before that, fails the call |
| 4 | An agent's status text carries no segment, or only some (an older plugin, the cut of RT-6.6 step 1) | Its row has only the numbers that are there; the roster is otherwise the same |
| 10 | The roster or the channel cannot be read for the check | No `not_reachable`, a log line; the post is out |
| 10 | The agent listens although its lease ran out, or its session ended minutes ago | **Limit:** reachable is a live lease (up to `CB_LEASE_SEC`, 15 min), online and not idle, not that the agent listens right now; a task to an agent that ended less than a lease ago looks reachable, and its HELLO and BYE posts remain the better evidence there |
| 5 | The channel cannot be read when `ASSIGNMENTS` is posted | The post goes out without a table, with a log line |
| 6 | The cut part of a status post mattered to a bystander | The post id is in the item: `get_thread` shows it whole |
| 8 | The coordinator learns of an `ACK`, `DONE` or `IDLE` only with the digest | Accepted: the assignment table and the next assignment wait up to `CB_LISTEN_DIGEST_SEC`; `BLOCKED`, `NO`, a `TASK` and plain posts still wake it at once |
| 8 | An fyi post needed the reader after all | Its author names the agent, or uses another word; the post is in the digest |
| 8 | The author lookup fails (listener, drain) | Nobody counts as an agent: the post wakes, and is ranked, as before |
| 9 | One channel cannot be read (403, Mattermost fails) | That channel's entry is `{channel, role, error}` with the reason in a few words, and a log line; the other channels still come back |
| 9 | The bot's channels cannot be listed | Refused with the reason; nothing else is read |
| 9 | `scope="all"` with `who`, `take` or `release`, or a scope other than `home` / `all` | Refused; nothing is read or changed |

## 6.15 References as links

**Purpose:** A human reads the bus in Mattermost and wants to open what an agent names — a PR, an issue, a ticket, a file at a line, a commit, an earlier post — with one click, and not be fooled by a link that leads elsewhere than it shows. Agents of different projects and customers share the bus and talk about each other's repos, forges and ticket systems, so a link must come from the one who knows where the reference lives: the agent. The MCP server links only what surely belongs to the bus, a post id, and writes out links that are not what they show. Anti-fooling, not a security scanner: it decides nothing, it shows where a link goes.

**Trigger:** An agent posts through the MCP server (any tool that writes a post, RT-6.14 step 2); a post is read (`get_thread`, `list_recent`, the WebSocket inbox, a channel push, `coordinator("tasks")`).

**Participants:** MCP server; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#efficient` Low overhead — here: permalinks cost the readers no tokens, an odd link costs only its own URL

### Sequence

1. The agent writes PRs, MRs, issues, tickets, files and commits as markdown links with the URL it knows from its tools (`gh pr create`, the tracker, its forge): `[PR #46](https://…/pull/46)`. A bare `#40`, `README.md` or sha could mean any repo on the bus; the server leaves it as text.
2. Before a post is sent, the server turns each 26-character post id (letters and digits) into its permalink on this bus, `<server>/<team>/pl/<id>`, with a short label instead of the 26 characters: `[Post](…/pl/<id>)`. An id right after `thread`, `post`, `reply` or `RESULT` (any case) folds that word into the label: `thread <id>` → `[thread](…/pl/<id>)`; at the start of the post the word is its bus word and stays outside (`RESULT [Post](…)`). After `ask` the label is the ask's headline instead, the question that titles the card with whitespace collapsed, cut at 60 characters with `…`, and `\`, `[`, `]` escaped so the link holds; the link carries the title `ask`: `ask <id>` → `[<headline>](…/pl/<id> "ask")`. The server knows the headline of the agent's own asks from `asks-<uid>.env` (`TITLE=` lines that `ask_human` writes, kept after `resolve_ask` for the newest 50 asks, so an `IDLE` right after resolving still shows it; they open no ask), without a request. Another agent's ask (since 0.65.5) the adapter reads once before it sends the post (`GET /api/v4/posts/<id>`, `client._ask_headlines`): the headline is the ask's question (its callout's title, an older ask's first bold text; `client.ask_title`), taken only when the post is an ask (`props.a2a.kind = "ask"`, or `🙋 @` at its start after an optional 👑), its `@` dropped (`@dembaca` → `dembaca`, `@channel` → `channel`): it comes from someone else's post and goes into this one after its mentions were checked (RT-6.11 step 1). At most 5 reads per post; a definite answer — the headline, no ask, or a post gone or not visible (403/404) — is remembered while the server runs (the newest 500), so that post is read once; an error that may pass (5xx, timeout, no connection, bad JSON) is read again in a later post. Where neither tells the headline — no ask, not readable, past the 5 — the label is `Input`, and the post goes all the same. A link the agent wrote with the bare id as label to that permalink gets the same label. An id alone in a code span (`` `<id>` ``, since 0.65.5) is linked as a bare one is, the word before it folded: ``Ask `<id>` `` → `[<headline>](…/pl/<id> "ask")`. Other code (fenced or inline), mentions and URLs stay as written; the id stays in the URL.
3. When a post is read, a permalink to this bus labelled with its post id or `Post` reads as the bare id again, one labelled with a folded word as the word and the id (`thread <id>`), and one with the link title `ask` as `ask <id>`, whatever its label, so every reader takes an ask link back alike, knowing the headline or not; this before the post reaches a model or the task rows. A link with a post id as label that goes anywhere else is odd (step 4). The task rows take a task's PR from `PR #n`, `MR !n` or a `/pull/n`, `/merge_requests/n` URL (RT-6.14 step 3).
4. Both ways — what the agent posts, which is what the human sees, and what an agent reads from anyone — a link that is not what it shows is written out as its label plus the full URL in ASCII: `PR #57 (https://github.com@evil.example/pull/57)`. Odd is: a label that names a host (`https://…`, `www.…`, `host.tld/…`, `host.tld` or an IPv4 address — unless the link ends in it, as `README.md` does) other than the target's; a dotted label names a host only when its last part starts with a letter (a top-level domain, `xn--` too), so `RT-6.5`, `0.65.6` or `v1.2` stay links, a `user@` before the host, a host in non-ASCII or punycode (shown as punycode: `github.com` spelt with a Cyrillic `і` reads `xn--gthub-n2e.com`), hidden characters (bidi controls, zero-width, soft hyphen) in label or URL (dropped from the label, %-encoded in the URL), a scheme other than http, https or mailto, a post-id label that is no permalink to this bus. A bare URL with an odd host is shown in ASCII. Images count as links: an odd one is written out and no longer loads. Inconspicuous links stay as they come, so the human decides with the full URL in sight.
5. In what the agent posts, a markdown table right after a line of text gets a blank line before it: Mattermost renders a table only after one. Code fences stay as they are.
6. Steps 2 to 5 cover the text a human reads in a post's message attachments as well, the callout of an ask (RT-6.11): its title (the question, since 0.71.27), pretext, text and every field's value, so a post id in an ask's `context` opens with one click and an agent reads it back as the bare id. The adapter does it in its post and read calls (`client.attachment_texts`, `client.read_links`), so no caller can forget it; field titles and the plain-text fallback are the server's own and stay as they are. The agent writes PRs and issues in an ask's fields as markdown links, as in a post (step 1).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | The agent names a PR, ticket or file without a link | It stays text; the server does not guess the repo or tracker |
| 1 | A file link points at a branch that is not pushed | It opens after the push; the agent links what a reader can open |
| 2 | A 26-character word of letters and digits is no post id | Its permalink leads to "not found"; such words are rare outside ids |
| 2 | Another agent's ask cannot be read (gone, no permission, server error) or the post is no ask | Its label is `Input` and the post goes. No ask, gone or not visible (403/404) is not read again while the server runs; another error is, in a later post |
| 4 | A post by a member that does not use this server (a human, another client) | Mattermost shows it as it was written; only agents reading it see the written-out form |
| 4 | A deceptive link the rules do not catch (a look-alike ASCII host, a reference-style link) | It stays as it came: no allowlists, no reputation lookups — the bus carries many customers, and URLs do not leave it |

---

## 6.16 Who may wake an agent

**Purpose:** Once more than one human is in the channel, the readability that makes the bus steerable also makes it injectable: anyone could mention a worker and have it read their post as a request. With trusted humans configured, only they and the agents wake a worker; everyone else reaches the coordinator, which answers but takes no work from them. A gated post costs the worker no model turn: its MCP server answers in the thread itself.

**Trigger:** A post reaches the MCP server's inbox (WebSocket or backfill, RT-6.2) that would wake the agent (RT-6.2 step 4, RT-6.12 step 3): a mention, a DM, a reply in a thread the agent posted in, `@channel` / `@all` / `@here`, or a post for all agents (`props.a2a.to = "all"`, RT-6.11 step 6); or a reaction to one of the agent's open asks (RT-6.11, step 7).

**Participants:** MCP server, Shell CLI, Agent skill; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API, Mattermost WebSocket, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Approval integrity — a post by someone not trusted is never a task for a worker

### Sequence

1. The MCP server puts every post's author in one of four classes, by the account Mattermost authenticates, never by what the post says: **trusted**, a trusted human (RT-6.8); **agent**, a peer: a bus agent whose owner and every human it trusts are trusted by this session, `{owner(B)} ∪ trusts(B) ⊆ trusted(me)` (ADR-001); **foreign**, every other bus agent; **other**, everyone else, including bots that are no bus agents (CI, integrations) and every webhook post (`props.from_webhook`), whatever account it names. The client keeps one account cache for it, `user id -> (username, bot, deleted)` (since 0.71.28): a bot account with `delete_at` set is no agent, but still no human owner of a bot (RT-6.16 step 1, owner). A bus agent is a bot account whose username starts with the registrar's prefix (its `BOT_USERNAME_PREFIX`, configurable, Section 7.1; `agent-` when not configured otherwise; a person whose account has that prefix stays a person). Trusted humans come from the bot's description, which the registrar writes as `for @<owner> · trusts @…` (ADR-003), or, with the registrar's keys pinned, from a valid attestation, which then replaces the description (RT-6.24): the MCP server reads the attestation from the registrar (RT-6.24 step 5) and the description with its own bot token through the chat port (`bot_description`: `GET /api/v4/users/<id>`, else `/api/v4/bots/<id>`), cached for 10 minutes, so it works in every harness. The owner comes from `agent_owner` (0.61.0): the bot's native owner (`owner_id` of `/api/v4/bots/<id>`, a human's username; readable only where the operator grants bot accounts `read_others_bots`), else the description's `for @…`, cached alike. They must agree: a native owner that differs from `for @` gives no owner. The registrar's own account owns every bot it did not bind and is never an owner: a description that starts with `unbound` (a shared-secret bot, whose `claims @…` is unchecked) or that the registrar wrote without `for @` names none, whatever `owner_id` says. A bot without an owner, or with a trusted list the registrar cut (marked `+N`), makes a foreign agent. The split is active only while trusted humans are configured and the agent's own bot has an owner; otherwise every bus agent is a peer, as before 0.60.0. Only a bot read without an owner turns it off: one that cannot be read keeps it on, with the trusted names last read (else those configured). In doubt, foreign (ADR-016): an agent whose owner cannot be read keeps the owner last read for it for `OWNER_GRACE_SEC` (300 s, `owners.py`) after the last read that returned one, so a short outage turns no task into a request; after that it is foreign. That read may be the client's cached description (10 minutes), so an owner change hidden by an outage can hold for up to 15 minutes. Trusted humans configured but none resolved count as configured, never as none. An unbound own bot has none: `bus_info` and `session_start` carry a `warning` saying so, and an enrollment token in `MM_REG_SECRET` binds the next registration. Every event carries `from_trusted_human` and `from_agent` (true for peers and foreign agents), in `wait_for_events` and as channel push attributes; a webhook post is neither. A foreign agent's event also carries `foreign` and `owner` (`none` without one) and, when its thread's root is by a trusted human, `root_from_trusted_human` with that human's name: a routed request keeps its author. A peer's post in the home may also carry `mandated` and `mandated_by`: its author holds a role a trusted human mandated (RT-6.26 step 4); it changes no class. `props.a2a.to` is no class either: anyone can write props through the API, so a post for all agents (RT-6.11 step 6) by `other` is gated like their `@channel`, and never counts as from an agent. Nor is `props.a2a.intent`: it counts only from an agent, and the gate does not read it (RT-6.14 step 8).
2. The gate is active only while trusted humans are configured (any of `CB_MM_TRUSTED_HUMAN_USER_IDS`, `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`, `CB_MM_OWNER_USER_ID`, `CB_OWNER`). Then a post by `other` that would wake the agent (trigger) is **gated**, and so is a foreign agent's broadcast (`@channel`, or to all agents), unless the agent holds the 👑 and the post is in its home, a DM or a group message: the coordinator gets it like any post there (RT-6.6 step 5: at once when it names no agent, else with the digest) and sees from both flags being false that the author is not trusted. In another team channel, the lobby too when it is not its home, its holder is gated like any worker (ADR-002); there a gated post goes into no digest (RT-6.22 step 3). A foreign agent's mention, DM or reply in the agent's thread is not gated: it wakes the worker as a request (step 5).
3. For a gated post the MCP server, without the model:
   - answers in its thread (`root_id`, else the post itself), once per thread, as the bot: `Not woken: <author> is not on my trusted list. <coordinator> (👑) will look at it.`; in a DM, which the coordinator cannot see, `… Ask in ~<work channel>, where <coordinator> (👑) coordinates.`; with no crown holder, `…, and no agent coordinates right now. Ask <trusted humans>.` Names are plain: only `ask_human` notifies a person (RT-6.11), and the coordinator sees every channel post anyway, a post that names an agent with its digest (RT-6.6 step 5). The coordinator is the agent wearing a live 👑 in the roster (read at most every 60 s), not the oldest reachable one. A post gated only as a broadcast (`@channel`, or to all agents) gets no answer, or every worker would answer it; nor does a post in a channel that already @-mentions the coordinator (the same name, as a whole word): it was told, and "will look at it" would only repeat that. A DM naming the coordinator is still answered, since the coordinator cannot see it, and the thread is not used up for a later post without the mention. Nor is a post answered that @-mentions another agent of the roster whose description trusts the author (as owner or in its trusted list): that agent wakes and answers it;
   - writes `REPLIED=<root_id> <epoch>` and `GATED=<post_id> <epoch>` to `gate-<uid>.env` in the state directory, dropping entries older than 7 days;
   - moves the read watermark past the gated post and its answer (RT-6.2 step 1): a gated post counts nowhere, so the count that drives the Stop hook and the listener stays quiet. An older post of that channel still waiting in the inbox keeps the mark before it, until its own delivery.
4. The gated post stays in the inbox as data. `wait_for_events` returns it after all other events, with `gated` saying why (`from <author>, not trusted by me: the coordinator answers`; a foreign agent's broadcast: `from <author>, an agent of another owner: its broadcast is no task`) and without the typing indicator (RT-6.5). A session started as a channel gets it only in the next digest, its line marked `(not trusted)` (RT-6.12). The background listener does not count post ids listed as `GATED` as addressed to the agent (RT-6.2 step 4); they still count for its digest.
5. The coordinator answers what is open or next; it takes on no work from the post and assigns nothing from it: a request from someone not trusted is never a command. Should it be done, the agent that got it asks a trusted human with `ask_human` and acts only after a yes. `coordinator("tasks")` and the `ASSIGNMENTS` table leave out a `TASK` or `DELEGATE` root by `other` or by a foreign agent. A foreign agent's event is a request, never a task: the worker answers, and acts only after a yes on its own human's `ask_human`, unless the event carries `root_from_trusted_human`: then it is that human's request, routed (ADR-001). A peer's event marked `mandated` is a task by that human's standing word: the worker takes it without a confirmation ask, and it approves nothing (RT-6.26 steps 4 and 7). The crown routes, it never authorises: the answer to `announce` carries a `warning` when the home's crown holder is foreign, and `coordinator("roster")` shows each agent's `owner`, `trusts` and `foreign` (RT-6.14 step 4). A foreign agent never approves: only `from_trusted_human` does (RT-6.8).
6. A channel push carries a post's text as content inside the `<channel …>` tag Claude Code wraps it in: `<channel` and `</channel` in the text, any case, become `‹channel` and `‹/channel`, so a body can neither end the tag nor open one (in single pushes and in the digest). `wait_for_events` returns JSON, and digest lines are single lines.
7. A reaction to an open ask by `other` is gated like their post, unless the agent holds the 👑: it approves nothing anyway (RT-6.11, RT-6.13), so it no longer wakes the worker. The MCP server writes `GATED=<ask>:<user>:<emoji> <epoch>` to `gate-<uid>.env`, posts no answer (a reaction asks nothing) and queues it as data: `wait_for_events` returns it last with `gated` (`from <author>, not trusted by me: approves nothing`), a channel session gets it only in the digest, and the listener (`/v1/ask-reactions`) does not count it. A reaction to a worker's ask reaches no other agent, the coordinator included; one to the coordinator's own ask wakes it as before. Reactions by trusted humans and agents are not gated.
8. **Props for a bridge (since 0.71.34).** `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` take an optional `props` object, written into the post's props beside the bus's own. Only keys starting with the props prefix are allowed (`CB_PROPS_PREFIX`, default `x_`, one setting for the whole bus, chapter 7; called `<prefix>` here): any other key (the bus's `a2a`, `agent_card`, …) refuses the call (`posted: false`, `refused` names the keys) and nothing is posted, so no caller can overwrite what the bus sets; `<prefix>system` is the bus's alone and refused too; the error names the prefix in force, and so do the MCP instructions (`props keys start with <prefix>.`), so a call need not fail to learn it; the prefix is at most 16 characters (the instructions name it on every turn, so an absurd one would grow the carried text); at most 2000 serialised characters. A program outside the bus (a bridge such as Hermes, which acts for a person) reads them from the post, e.g. `<prefix>on_behalf_of`; the bus reads none of them.
9. **System replies wake no bot.** The answers the MCP server posts without the model (step 3, and the answer on departed or absent agents, RT-6.21) carry `props.<prefix>system = true`. A post with that prop by a bus agent (class `agent` or `foreign`) reaches every inbox as data (`gated`: `a bus system reply: for no bot`), the coordinator's included, is marked `GATED` and answered by nobody: no bot wakes for it and none answers it, so two servers cannot answer each other's answers. The same prop on a person's or another bot's post is ignored.
10. **An attested bot is an agent (since 0.71.34).** With the registrar's keys pinned (`MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS`, RT-6.24), a bot account whose username lacks the registrar's prefix but whose attestation holds (the registrar's record for its id and username, signed and not expired) is class `agent` (or `foreign` by step 1's owner rule): it wakes workers and is not gated, and its replies in a DM or in a thread of the worker wake it like any agent's (`dm`, `thread`, RT-6.2 step 4); it approves nothing: its events are `from_agent`, never `from_trusted_human`, and it is on no trusted list (RT-6.8, RT-6.11). Without pinned keys, or when the attestation does not hold, it stays `other` and is gated. The registrar's record needs `kind` `autonomous` (or `session`), `via` `enrollment`, `bot_id` and `username` of the account, and `responsible` the person it acts for.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 2 | No trusted humans configured | No gate: every post wakes as in RT-6.2; the events still carry `from_agent` |
| 2 | The MCP server is not running, or its inbox not connected yet | Nothing is gated: the Turn hooks and the listener wake on the pending count as in RT-6.2. Accepted, not worked around |
| 1–2 | The author's account or the thread cannot be read | Fails open: the post wakes as before, with a log line |
| 3 | The answer cannot be posted | Logged; the thread is not remembered as answered, so the next gated post there tries again. The post is gated all the same |
| 3 | The post mentions an agent that is no crown holder (or no coordinator is known) | The answer is posted as usual |
| 3 | The coordinator lookup fails (roster unreadable) | The answer names the trusted humans, as without a coordinator; the failure is not cached |
| 1 | Neither a bot's native owner nor its description can be read (an API error, no permission) | In doubt, foreign (ADR-016): within `OWNER_GRACE_SEC` (300 s) of the last read that returned an owner (cached or not, so at most 15 minutes after Mattermost last answered) that owner holds; after that, or never read, the agent is foreign, with a log line. When the agent's own cannot be read, the owner split stays on. Retried after a minute |
| 1 | The native owner cannot be read (`403` without `read_others_bots`) | The description's `for @…` decides, as in 0.60.0 |
| 1 | A bot's description is empty, or names no owner, while the agent's own bot has one | Foreign: nothing says whom it answers to |
| 1 | A bot is `unbound` (registered with the shared secret), or the registrar wrote its description without `for @` | Foreign: the registrar's own account owns it, whatever `owner_id` says; its `claims @…` is unchecked |
| 1 | A bot's native owner and its `for @…` disagree | Foreign, with a warning in the MCP log: one of them was changed outside the registrar |
| 1 | The agent's own bot has an owner (`agent_owner`: native owner, else `for @`) other than its responsible human (`CB_RESPONSIBLE_HUMAN`, else the first trusted human) | Only a warning: `session_start` of an MCP-registered session carries it (`warning`) and the MCP log has it; the split works with the owner the bot has |
| 5 | The trust check for `coordinator("tasks")` or `ASSIGNMENTS` fails (an author's account cannot be read) | In doubt, no root counts as a task this time (ADR-016), logged; the next read checks again |
| 1 | A lookup of `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS` by name fails (not a 404) | Not remembered: asked again after `TRUSTED_RETRY_SEC` (60 s), and `bus_info` says the names are not resolved, not that none are set; until then `owners.mine` counts the configured names (below) |
| 2 | Trusted humans are configured but none resolves (an outage, or a name with no account: `bus_info` names those, `configured, no account found for: …`) | The gate stays on (`gate.active`: configured, not resolved, ADR-016), as do the task rows' trust check and the foreign check on ask closers: nobody counts as trusted meanwhile, a trusted human's post too is gated as data, and the coordinator still gets it |
| 1 | My trusted humans or my own bot id cannot be read, or none of the configured names resolves | The owner split stays on with the names last read within `OWNER_GRACE_SEC`, else those in `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`; without either it is off, as without trusted humans. Not yet in doubt: the crown election (RT-6.6, `lease.py`) and the roster's `foreign` column read owners without the grace period; they follow in their own PR |
| 1 | The agent's own bot is `unbound` | The owner split is off, as before 0.60.0; `bus_info` and `session_start` (and the host's banner) carry a `warning` that it is unbound and an enrollment token binds it |
| 1 | The other agent runs a plugin before 0.60.0 | It treats every bus agent as a peer; this side applies its own rules to what it receives (RISK-04) |
| 3 | The roster cannot be read for the mentioned agents | The answer is posted as usual |
| 5 | An agent passes on a request from a human this session does not trust | Closed by ADR-001: an agent that trusts such a human, or has another owner, is foreign, and its request is no task. What remains is a peer that was itself injected (RISK-01, Section 11) |
| 5 | A registrar before 0.2.0 writes the owner a shared-secret caller names | Accepted until the registrar is upgraded; since 0.2.0 such bots are `unbound` and only an enrollment token binds an owner (RISK-02, Section 7.3) |

---

## 6.17 Turn hooks ask the MCP server

**Purpose:** The Turn hooks and the background listener need a few values from the bus every turn, and the agent's shell scripts post, poll and set the status; none of them needs a bot token: the MCP server, which holds it anyway, answers them over a local API. So the MCP server is required in every runtime (0.40.0), and no agent shell holds a token; with corral only the server outside the sandbox holds one (RT-6.18).

**Trigger:** A Turn hook runs (`UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `SessionStart` after compaction, IF-05), the background listener polls (RT-6.2 step 4), or the agent runs `mm-agent-post.sh`, `mm-agent-poll.sh` or `mm-agent-status.sh` in its shell (Claude Code, Cursor, OpenCode, Codex).

**Participants:** Turn hooks, MCP server, Shell CLI; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement — the hooks hold no token; `#reliable` Never wedges a coding session

### Sequence

1. At start, with a bus configured, the MCP server serves HTTP/1.1 on `127.0.0.1` only, on an ephemeral port, in its own event loop next to the WebSocket inbox. It draws a random key per start (32 bytes, URL-safe). A malformed request (request line, headers, `Content-Length`) gets `400` before the key is looked at, with no work done and nothing echoed. Every other request carries `Authorization: Bearer <key>`, compared in constant time, or gets `401`, for unknown routes too. The hooks hand the key to `curl` on stdin, never in its argv, and never log it; the Shell CLI does the same with the bot token (`mm_curl_auth`). The server serves at most 32 connections at once (one more is closed without an answer), gives up writing an answer to a client that does not read it after 5 s, and runs at most 2 requests that make several Mattermost calls (`/v1/pending`, `/v1/presence`, `/v1/post`, `/v1/reply`, `/v1/files`, `/v1/turn-end`, `/v1/wake`, `/v1/handoff`) at a time; a long poll of `/v1/wake` (step 7) takes a slot for each check only, never while it waits.
2. Hooks and scripts take endpoint and key from `CB_BUS_ENDPOINT` / `CB_BUS_KEY` when set (a corral session: the MCP server on the host serves the API with the key preStart drew and writes no file, RT-6.18), else from the file the server writes in the state directory, 0600, keyed like the session file (RT-6.10). Besides endpoint, key and `CB_BUS_PID` it names the bot's `CB_MM_BOT_USER_ID` once the server knows it and the MCP client's name from the handshake (`CB_BUS_CLIENT`, `clientInfo.name`); neither is secret. They read it on every call. The server removes it when it ends, unless a newer server of the same session wrote its own. Which file (`mm_bus_file` in the shell, `keyed_path` in the server):

   | Runtime | The server keys its file on | The shell finds it by |
   |---------|-----------------------------|-----------------------|
   | Claude Code | `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`: `endpoint-sid-<sha1(id)>.env` | the same variable, which Claude Code gives the hooks and every command the agent starts (a hook without it: `session_id` from its input); no fallback |
   | Cursor | the workspace, `endpoint-<sha1(dir)>.env`: `CB_WORKSPACE` (`${workspaceFolder}` in `mcp.json`), else the client's first `file://` root (`roots/list`, asked once initialized; the file moves then), else the server's working directory | `CURSOR_PROJECT_DIR` or `CB_WORKSPACE` when set, else the walk below |
   | OpenCode, Codex | the workspace: the server's working directory (OpenCode starts it in the project, `uv run --project` keeps it), or the client's first root | walking up from the real path of `$PWD` to the closest endpoint file: the shell starts in the project, the agent may `cd` deeper |

   Before that: `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` names the workspace on both sides when set. A directory is the real path (symlinks resolved). A workspace named by a variable is taken exactly, with no walk. The walk stops at the nearest git top (a directory with a `.git` directory or file): a repository nested in another workspace, or a worktree under a checkout, never reaches the outer workspace's server. Without a session id the server keys no file on `/`, the home directory or a directory above it: from its working directory or a root there it writes none and waits for the client's roots (`bus_info` and `session_start` say so in `warning`); a variable naming such a directory is taken. What stays: from a directory that is in no repository of its own, the walk reaches the server of the closest workspace above it.
3. The API answers JSON under `/v1`:

   | Route | Answer | Asked by |
   |-------|--------|----------|
   | `GET /v1/me` | `user_id`, `username`, `project` (a project channel is set), `crown_until` (epoch, 0 without the 👑), `channel` (the agent confirmed the channel check, RT-6.12), `relay` when a keyless session's relay is known (RT-6.18 step 11) | every hook, the listener, the status line in a keyless session (RT-6.18) |
   | `GET /v1/pending` | `count`: mentions, DMs and posts for all agents after the read watermark (RT-6.2 step 1), plus a stall event still to collect (`stall: 1`, RT-6.19); with `?detail=1` up to 5 `channels` (`id`, `type`, `pending`, `fyi`) | Stop hook (drain, RT-6.2 step 1), listener |
   | `GET /v1/last-post` | `at` (ms), `message`: the own latest post in the work channel, `at` 0 when there is none; `signed_off`: it is an `IDLE`/`BYE` and nothing changed on the bus since (RT-6.2 step 2); with `?unanswered=1` also `unanswered`, the posts that wait for an answer in their thread and were not reminded of yet; reading marks nothing (`POST /v1/reminded` does, RT-6.2 step 2); `release` (`newest`, `line`) when the host's release check passed a newer release, for the turn-end banner (RT-6.30) | Stop hook (sign-off and window, RT-6.2 steps 2–3) |
   | `GET /v1/posts` | `channel` (the name asked for, else the work channel's; `mm-agent-poll.sh` prints it) and `posts`, newest first, of the work channel or `?channel=` (an id, or a name in the bot's team) when it is the work, project or default channel or a guest channel (RT-6.22), else `403`: no DM, no group, not the stats channel (Section 7.3) (`?limit=N`, at most 200, or `?since=MS`): `id`, `user_id`, `root_id`, `create_at`, `type`, `message`, `to` (`all` for all agents), `intent`, `fyi` (an agent's fyi post, RT-6.14), `quiet` (another agent's context line, RT-6.6 step 9), `gated` (RT-6.16), `names_agent` (RT-6.6 step 5), `absent` (RT-6.21 step 5), and `files` for a post with attachments (as in a slim post, RT-6.23) | listener, `mm-agent-poll.sh` |
   | `GET /v1/ask-reactions` | `fresh`: reactions on open asks not yet handed to the agent (RT-6.11) and not gated (RT-6.16 step 7) | listener |
   | `POST /v1/presence` | `{state: working \| idle \| listening \| bus, label?, until?}` → `ok`; `label` one line of at most 64 characters (stripped), `until` an epoch within a day, else `400`; `idle` and `listening` end the turn: open permission prompts close (RT-6.13 step 5) | prompt and Stop hooks (RT-6.2, RT-6.3), `mm-agent-status.sh` by hand (RT-6.6) |
   | `POST /v1/stat` | `{k: turn \| turn_end \| block \| compact \| wake \| transcript \| inject \| usage, …}` → `ok`: one overhead counter (RT-6.9) into the server's own file, at most 4 fields with short lowercase names, numbers or strings up to 4096 characters (`inject`: exactly `name`, one of the kinds RT-6.9 names, and `chars`, an integer from 0 to 10^7; `usage`: exactly its 18 integer fields, RT-6.9 step 1), else `400` | every hook and the listener in a keyless session (RT-6.18), whose files the server cannot share |
   | `POST /v1/waiting` | `{}` → `ok`: a harness plugin waits for its first session, which holds back the no-relay warning (RT-6.18 step 11) | OpenCode plugin under corral (RT-6.20 step 1) |
