**Purpose:** The host copy under corral comes from a release tag the owner signed, not from whatever a clone holds: `mm-agent-host-install.sh --release v<x.y.z>` installs a version only when its annotated tag carries the asked name, has not moved and its tree says that version, and, when the host pins release keys (opt-in, decided 2026-11-12: `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`, kept by the host in `release-signers` beside `releases/` after the first run with it), only when its signature verifies against them (ADR-011 phase 1b); a version the host's release check saw withdrawn needs `--withdrawn-ok`. Without the keys the trust rests on the repository over https and the tag object pinned in `releases/`. Install, switch, rollback and the session records are RT-6.28's; this scenario is the source.

**Trigger:** The developer runs `mm-agent-host-install.sh --release v<x.y.z>` on the host, after the owner pushed that signed tag (CLAUDE.md, "Releasing").

**Participants:** Shell CLI, Host session hooks; Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement — with pinned keys only code the release key signed runs on the host with the registration secret and the bot token, without them it says so on every run; `#reliable` Never wedges — every refusal leaves `current` as it was

### Sequence

1. The installer takes the version only as `v<major>.<minor>.<patch>` (the `v` optional), checked before it reaches a git argument or any output, and only alone (no `--from`, `--rollback`, `--check`). It reads the pinned keys from the host only, never from the plugin or a session (ADR-011 decision (4)): `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` in its own environment when it is set — the operator's shell, which direnv can fill from a workdir's `.envrc`, so such a file is allowed only after reading it — else `release-signers` beside `releases/` and `src.git` (`CB_BUS_HOST_DIR`'s parent), which an earlier run with the variable left there (step 4), else none; stderr names the source (`release keys from CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` or `from <file>`). Not in the state directory: `XDG_STATE_HOME` comes from the same environment, and a redirected one would find no pin and downgrade to unchecked. Ed25519 public key lines (`ssh-ed25519 AAAA… [comment]`, or the FIDO2 form `sk-ssh-ed25519@openssh.com AAAA… [comment]`, one per line or separated by `;`), the owner's release key and the offline backup key. The file, whenever it exists, counts only as a regular file (no symlink) of the user's own that no one else can write, at most 16 KiB, in a directory that is the user's own, writable by no one else and by no sandbox, every line checked again as a key; anything else refuses the run, it never falls back to unchecked. The check is opt-in: with keys the signature is enforced (the variable set but holding no key is refused, also when the file exists); with neither, the installer prints a one-line WARNING on stderr (signature not checked, set the variable to enforce) and skips step 4's signature part, every other step stays. With both, the variable's keys must be the pinned ones (as a set; comments and order do not count): other keys, a rotation (old plus new) included, are refused, `pinned keys differ from CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`, unless `--replace-signers` (with `--release` only). `--forget-signers`, alone, removes `release-signers` after the same checks (a symlink or a directory a sandbox can write: refused, nothing removed): the next run without the variable is unchecked again. The repository is `CB_RELEASE_URL`, default `https://github.com/dembaca/ai-agent-bus.git`: a plain `https://` URL (no user, query or space) or an absolute local path or `file:///` URL (a mirror) out of every sandbox's reach. With keys it needs git 2.34 or newer and an `ssh-keygen` with `-Y find-principals` (OpenSSH 8.2 or newer). In every mode that changes something (not `--check` or `--help`, which only skip the release check there) the installer refuses to run from a tree a sandbox can write (the plugin cache, a corral home, a session's workdir) and names the host copy's installer instead. That check runs after it sourced `mm-agent-lib.sh` from its own tree, so it catches a mistake: the control is running the host copy's installer (`<CB_BUS_HOST_DIR>/bin/mm-agent-host-install.sh`) or the one of a clone the developer reviewed.
2. Layout and lock as RT-6.28 step 1. Then, before anything is fetched, a version that `release-withdrawn.env` beside `releases/` (the copy of `NEWEST` and the `WITHDRAWN` lines the host's release check writes with `release.env`, RT-6.30 step 4; not `release.env` itself, which `XDG_STATE_HOME` could move) lists as withdrawn, older than its `NEWEST`, is refused, naming `NEWEST`, the release whose CHANGELOG withdrew it (ADR-011 decision (5)); `--withdrawn-ok` installs it on purpose and says so on stderr. No network for this: without the file nothing is refused and stderr says `withdrawn versions unknown: no release check on this host yet; run --check`; a symlinked one refuses nothing. `--rollback` (RT-6.28 step 6) refuses a previous release whose tree says a withdrawn version the same way, and takes `--withdrawn-ok` too. git runs with no system or global config, no attributes file, fsmonitor, hook, credential helper or replace refs (`GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS`), `LC_ALL=C`, only the `https` and `file` protocols, no HTTP redirect, and checks every object it receives (`transfer.fsckObjects`).
3. It fetches `refs/tags/v<x.y.z>` (that tag only, `--no-tags`) into the bare repository `src.git` beside `releases/`, created on first use. The refspec has no `+`: git refuses to change a tag it holds already, so a tag that moved since this host fetched it is refused.
4. The tag must be an annotated tag (a tag object, not a commit), with or without keys, so the same tags serve both kinds of host. With keys it must carry an SSH signature block (`-----BEGIN SSH SIGNATURE-----`) and no PGP or X.509 one, which another program would check against another trust store. The installer writes an allowed-signers file into its lock directory from the keys in force (`CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`, else those pinned in `release-signers`), one fixed principal (`mm-release`) with `namespaces="git"`, and runs `git verify-tag` on the tag's object id with `gpg.format=ssh` and that file. An SSH signature has no GPG status line to read: it counts only when `verify-tag` exits 0 and reports `Good "git" signature for mm-release with … key SHA256:…`; that fingerprint is the signer. Keys from `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` that verified the tag become the host's: their `<type> <base64>` (no comment) go into `release-signers` beside `releases/` (a directory step 2's layout already refused when a sandbox can write it), 0600, in one rename, and the installer says `Pinned the release keys …` or, with `--replace-signers` over other keys, `Replaced the release keys …`; the same keys again change nothing.
5. The tag object's own `tag` name must be the one asked for (a ref pointed at another release installs nothing), and it must tag a commit. `git archive` of that commit, the same paths as RT-6.28 step 2, goes into `releases/.install.<random>`; the version in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `mcp/pyproject.toml` and `mcp/src/mattermost_ai_agent_bus/__init__.py` must each equal the tag's.
6. From here as RT-6.28 steps 2–7: the downgrade rule, `uv sync --locked` before the switch, the release named `<x.y.z>` (no `-dev`), the switch, `previous`, the dispatcher, the garbage collection. `SOURCE` says `SOURCE=signed`, `TAG=v<x.y.z>`, `TAG_OBJECT`, `COMMIT` and `SIGNER=<SHA256 fingerprint>`; without keys `SOURCE=release`, `VERIFIED=no`, `TAG`, `TAG_OBJECT` and `COMMIT`, no `SIGNER`. A release of that name kept in `releases/` (current, previous or only kept) whose `SOURCE` names another tag object is refused, before the downgrade rule: a release never moves, even when `src.git` was removed and the tag signed again; the operator removes `releases/<x.y.z>` on purpose to accept it. With the same tag object it is reported as current, or switched back to as it is; its `SOURCE` is never marked down (one verified before stays `signed` in a run without keys), and a run with keys marks one installed without them as `signed`. A release the garbage collection removed is not remembered. The installer prints the tag, the commit and the signer, or that the signature was not checked.
7. Which kind runs is shown: the installer's output, `--check` and the start hook say `signed release v<x.y.z>`, `release v<x.y.z> (signature not checked)` or `unsigned` (`--from`, an install from the clone, a host copy from before 0.81.0) — in the notice of a newer plugin and in the line that announces the session's bot.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | No `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` and no `release-signers` | A WARNING on stderr; the release installs with every check but the signature, marked `SOURCE=release`, `VERIFIED=no` |
| 1 | `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` set but without a key, or a line in it that is no Ed25519 public key (`ssh-ed25519` or `sk-ssh-ed25519@openssh.com`) | Refused before anything is fetched, also when `release-signers` exists |
| 1 | `release-signers` a symlink, no regular file, another user's or writable by others, in a directory that is another user's, writable by others or by a sandbox, larger than 16 KiB, without a key or with a line that is no key, with or without the variable | Refused before anything is fetched; never unchecked instead |
| 1 | `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` holds other keys than `release-signers` (a rotation included) | Refused: `--forget-signers` first, or `--replace-signers` |
| 1 | `XDG_STATE_HOME` redirected (a workdir's `.envrc`) | No effect on the keys or the withdrawn list: both lie beside `releases/` |
| 2 | `release-withdrawn.env` lists the version as withdrawn (`--release`, or `--rollback` to it, a `-dev` or `-legacy` build too) | Refused, naming the release whose CHANGELOG withdrew it; `--withdrawn-ok` goes on and says so |
| 2 | No `release-withdrawn.env` (no release check yet) | Nothing refused; one stderr line says the withdrawn versions are unknown |
| 4 | With keys from `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`: `release-signers` cannot be created, or its directory is another user's or writable by others | A WARNING; the release installs, the keys are not pinned |
| 1 | A version not of the form `v<x.y.z>` | Refused; the argument is not echoed |
| 1 | `CB_RELEASE_URL` not `https://` or an absolute path, or a path a sandbox can write | Refused |
| 1 | With keys: git older than 2.34, no `ssh-keygen`, or one without `-Y find-principals` | Refused |
| 1 | The installer runs from a tree a sandbox can write, or from a session's workdir | Refused, naming the host copy's installer |
| 3 | The fetch fails (no network, no such repository or tag) | Refused, with git's last lines; `current` unchanged |
| 3 | The tag moved since this host fetched it | Refused at the fetch, with or without keys: a release tag never moves |
| 4 | A lightweight tag, with or without keys | Refused: no tag object, no signature |
| 4 | With keys: an annotated tag without signature | Refused |
| 4 | With keys: a PGP or X.509 signature | Refused: not an SSH signature |
| 4 | With keys: a signature by a key not pinned | Refused, naming its fingerprint |
| 5 | The tag object carries another name, with or without keys | Refused |
| 5 | A version in the tree other than the tag's, with or without keys | Refused, naming the three versions |
| 6 | The same version kept in `releases/` from another tag object, with or without keys | Refused, naming both object ids; `current` unchanged |
| 6 | `uv sync` fails, an older version without `--downgrade` | As RT-6.28: `current` unchanged |

---

## 6.30 Which release is out, and what it needs

**Purpose:** Every server tells its human which release is out, whether it is a patch or a minor, `Breaking`, and what it needs first, only from a release tag that passed this host's check, so a human can decide to update; the notice installs nothing (ADR-011 phase 2, points 3, 4 and 6; the self-update between sessions that the registrar names is RT-6.32). The roster shows each agent's version, so a mixed bus and code that is no signed release are visible.

**Trigger:** The installer's `--check`; the MCP server about once a day (hourly while the newest found is older than the running host copy's version); a session's start, the end of a turn, `bus_info`, the roster.

**Participants:** Shell CLI, MCP server, Turn hooks, Host session hooks; Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement — what reaches the agents is a version, words in a fixed grammar and flags, from a checked tag only; no agent gets a way to install, and no host process runs a script a sandbox can write; `#reliable` Never wedges — a failed check changes nothing and blocks nothing

### Sequence

1. `bin/mm-agent-release-check.sh` runs on the host only: not inside corral (`CORRAL_SANDBOX`), not from a tree a sandbox can write, and it refuses a state directory, `CB_BUS_HOST_DIR`'s parent or `src.git` a sandbox can write. It reads the keys and `CB_RELEASE_URL` as `--release` does (RT-6.29 step 1, the same helpers in `mm-agent-lib.sh`): `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` when set, else `release-signers` beside `releases/` (`CB_BUS_HOST_DIR`'s parent, out of `XDG_STATE_HOME`'s reach), so the MCP server's child under corral, whose environment lacks the variable, checks with the keys the operator's `--release` run pinned (ADR-011 decision (4)); a bad file fails the check. With keys the signature is enforced, without them every other check; its last line names where the keys came from (`keys from CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` or `from <file>`) or that there were none. One check at a time (`release-check.lock` in the state directory; a lock older than ten minutes is taken over by one rename, and one that turns out fresh goes back); none while the installer holds its lock, and the installer refuses while a check's lock is fresh: both fetch into `src.git`. `--if-due <seconds>` skips a check when the last one that succeeded (`release-check.at`) is younger.
2. It lists the tags at the URL (`git ls-remote`, names only), takes the newest three of the form `v<x.y.z>` and tries them newest first: fetched into `src.git` beside `releases/` with `--depth=1` and without `+` (a tag this host fetched before, here or by the installer, never moves: the installer later installs the very tag object the notice named), in the clean git environment of RT-6.29 step 2; then the checks of RT-6.29 steps 4–5 (an annotated tag, with keys an SSH signature by a pinned key, its own name, a commit) and the version in its `plugin.json`. A tag that fails is logged once per tag object (`release-rejected.env`) and never shown; one that cannot be fetched is logged; either way the next older one is tried.
3. From the `CHANGELOG.md` of the first tag that passes (at most 4 MiB, the 60 newest entries, `## x.y.z` or `## [x.y.z]`, none newer than the tag) it reads per entry `### Breaking` and the release rules of CLAUDE.md ("Releasing"): `Needs:` lines — `MM_*` names, versions `x.y.z` and lowercase words of at most 15 letters, each with an optional `,` or `.`, one space between, at most 120 characters (`NEEDS_PATTERN` in `release.py`, the same regex in `mm-agent-lib.sh`) — and `Withdrawn: v<x.y.z>[, …]` lines naming older releases. A line outside its grammar is dropped and counted on stderr; `scripts/doc-check.py` refuses one in the repository already.
4. It writes `release.env` in the host state directory (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/mm-agent-bus`), 0600, in one rename: `FORMAT=1`, `CHECKED_AT=<epoch>`, `NEWEST`, `TAG_OBJECT`, `COMMIT`, `VERIFIED=yes|no`, `SIGNER` with keys, `ENTRY=<version> breaking|-`, `NEEDS=<version> <text>`, `WITHDRAWN=<version>`; then `release-withdrawn.env` beside `releases/` (0600, one rename: `NEWEST` and the `WITHDRAWN` lines, which the installer reads, RT-6.29 step 2, and `TAG=<x.y.z>` for each tag of step 2's list, newest first, whether it passed or not, from which the installer takes the target when the registrar names `latest`, RT-6.32 step 4) and `release-check.at`, the time of this successful check. A check that fails (no network, no tag that passes, an unreadable CHANGELOG) leaves both as they were.
5. The MCP server looks a minute after it starts and then hourly whether a check is due: the last success a day old (an hour old when the newest found, `release.env`'s `NEWEST`, is older than this server's version and it runs from a host copy (signed, release or unsigned; a plugin tree or checkout ahead of the tags is normal and keeps the day), so that a version installed just after a check catches up instead of showing step 6's stale notice for a day; `--if-due` gets the same period), and fewer than three failed tries in the last day (`release-check.failed`, which the server writes on a failure or a stop). Which script: its own tree's when no sandbox can write that tree (a host copy's release, a reviewed checkout); for a tree a sandbox can write (the plugin cache under `~/.claude`, a session's workdir) never that one, but the host copy's (`CB_BUS_HOST_DIR`, default `~/.local/share/mm-agent-bus/current`) when it is out of every sandbox's reach, else none, said once in the log; such a server still reads the notice the host's check wrote. The child: `/bin/bash`, from `/`, stdin closed, its own process group, the environment `PATH`, `HOME`, `LC_ALL=C`, `CB_RELEASE_URL`, `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`, `CB_BUS_HOST_DIR` (the host copy whose script runs; for a host copy's release without it, the layout it lies in), `CORRAL_WORKDIR`, and what reading the registrar's release record takes (RT-6.32 step 2): `MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS`, `MM_REGISTER_URL`, `CB_MM_CHAT_URL`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `https_proxy`, `NO_PROXY`, `no_proxy`; never a token or the registration secret; after 120 s its group gets `TERM` (git removes its lock files on it), 2 s later `KILL`. Not inside a corral sandbox. Its output goes to the server's log. The server parses nothing from the network: it reads `release.env`, checks every field again (`release.py`, the same grammar; a `WITHDRAWN` not older than `NEWEST` is dropped) and skips what does not hold.
6. The notice, for this server's version: `release v<newest> is out (<major|minor|patch>[, Breaking][; needs: …]; signature checked|not checked)` when the newest is newer, with Breaking and the `Needs:` lines of every entry after this version up to it; `this host's <version> is withdrawn` when an entry withdrew it; `the release check of <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MMZ> found v<x.y.z> as newest, older than <version>: not run since, or the repository is frozen or rewound` when the newest is older (the time from `CHECKED_AT`, in UTC; without a readable one `the release check found …`): never a step back, so no line names the installer for it, neither the turn-end banner (RT-6.2 step 8), corral's start hook nor the installer's `--check`. `bus_info` carries it as `release`: `this` (version and source: `signed release v<x.y.z>`, `release v<x.y.z> (signature not checked)`, `unsigned` for a host copy from `--from` or a clone, `plugin` for a tree without a `SOURCE` file: the plugin, a checkout), `newest`, `step`, `breaking`, `needs`, `signature`, `checked_at`, `withdrawn`, `older`, `notice`, always `auto` (the self-update's state, RT-6.32 step 7), `check` while no check has passed yet, and the note that a human installs it, never the agent; `{}` when the server does not know its own version. The Stop hook reads `release` (`{newest, line}`) from the server's answer to `GET /v1/last-post`, which it asks every turn end anyway, and names it once per version in the banner (RT-6.2 step 8); corral's start hook and the installer's `--check` print it from `release.env` with `mm_release_notice` (`mm-agent-lib.sh`), the same text.
7. The roster (`coordinator(action="roster")`): every server puts its version as the last segment of its status text, `v<x.y.z>`, `v<x.y.z> unchecked`, `v<x.y.z> unsigned` or `v<x.y.z> plugin`; when the text is too long it goes first, before the label (RT-6.6 step 1). A row shows `version` and, when not a signed release, `source`; an agent whose status names none shows its HELLO card's `version`. An older reader skips the segment: its status word, numbers and home stay where they were.
8. The check installs nothing: the host copy changes when a human runs `mm-agent-host-install.sh --release v<x.y.z>` (RT-6.29), or, under corral, between sessions to the version the registrar names, or the newest tag of step 2's list that is not withdrawn when it names `latest` (RT-6.32, which also reads the registrar's record in this check, for the notice only); the plugin follows Claude Code's own update. The installer reads the withdrawn versions from `release-withdrawn.env`: `--release` and `--rollback` refuse a withdrawn version unless `--withdrawn-ok` (RT-6.29 step 2).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | Run inside corral, from a tree or with a state directory a sandbox can write | Refused; nothing written |
| 1 | Another check runs, or the installer | Exits without a check; the installer, while a check's lock is fresh, refuses and says to rerun |
| 1 | A killed check left its lock | Taken over after ten minutes by one rename |
| 1 | `release-signers` refused as in RT-6.29 step 1 (a symlink, another's, writable by others, in such a directory, too large, a line that is no key, other keys in `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`) | The check fails; `release.env` as it was |
| 2 | No network, no such repository, no tag | Fails; `release.env` and `release-check.at` as they were |
| 2 | The newest tag fails the check (unsigned with keys, another key, a lightweight tag, moved, another version in its tree) or cannot be fetched | Logged (a failed check once per tag object), never shown; the next older tag is tried |
| 2 | None of the three newest tags passes | Fails; `release.env` as it was |
| 3 | A `Needs:` or `Withdrawn:` line outside its grammar | Dropped, counted on stderr |
| 5 | The child does not end within 120 s, or fails | Its process group is stopped; a failed try, repeated at the next hourly look, at most three a day |
| 5 | The server's own tree can be written from a sandbox (the plugin cache) and the host copy has no script out of every sandbox's reach | No child; said once in the log |
| 5 | `release.env` missing, a symlink, or without its fields | No notice; `bus_info` says no check yet |
| 5 | Under corral, `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS` only in the operator's shell, not in corral's hook environment, and no keys pinned in `release-signers` | The host process checks without keys and says `signature not checked`; one `--release` run with the variable pins them for it |

---

## 6.31 MCP debug

**Purpose:** While testing trust, mandates and work on someone's behalf, a trusted human sees in the chat what each agent's own MCP server decided about an event (why it woke, which class it gave the author, which signed records held, why an ask's answer counted, what it changed in a tool call, the props as they came and went), without the API, and only for as long as asked (ADR-010 phase 2). The agent gets the same lines with its events.

**Trigger:** A trusted human types `/agent-debug` in a home (registrar 0.8.0, ADR-010 phase 1); the server delivers an event of its home or a DM; the agent posts in its home or a DM; the heartbeat (RT-6.19) finds the record due to be read again; `bus_info`.

**Participants:** MCP server, Agent skill; Agent runtime, Registrar, Mattermost REST API, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#usable` Reachability — the answer to "why did it do that" stands under the post that raised it; `#secure` Secret confinement — never a key, a signature, a token or an argument's value; debug grants nothing and changes no decision

### Sequence

1. **Switching, in the registrar.** A trusted human types `/agent-debug on <category…>` (adds), `off <category…>` (removes), `off` (all off), `full` (all on), `set <category…>` (replaces) or `status` in the home, each with `for <duration>` (at most 24 h, default an hour), which sets one end for every category. The registrar keeps one record per home, built from the previous one and the command, and publishes it signed: `GET <registrar>/register/v1/debug/<channel id>` answers `{"record": "<JWS>"}`, `404 {"error":"no debug set"}` when none is on, `400 {"error":"invalid channel id"}`. Header `{"alg":"EdDSA","typ":"mm-agent-debug+jwt","kid":…}`, the attestation key; claims `channel_id`, `categories` (non-empty, in the order `wake, identity, sig, asks, calls, props`, never `full`), `set_by`, `set_by_id`, `set_at_ms`, `until_ms` (the setting's end), `iat`, `exp` (an hour after `iat`, the signature's freshness). No post syntax and no environment variable switches debug.
2. **Reading the record** (`debug.py`). Only with the registrar's keys pinned (`MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS`, RT-6.24 step 4): each server reads its home's record (found as RT-6.25 step 8 finds the mode, `/debug/<id>` in place of `/agents`; no Authorization header, 5 s, at most 8 KiB) at its first look (an event, a post, `bus_info`) and then on the heartbeat once 120 s have passed (`stall.watch`), and verifies it (`attest.verify_debug`): a pinned kid, the debug type, the expected home, both ids 26 characters, `exp` with 60 s of skew, `until_ms` later than now with the same skew, and the categories checked here: a non-empty list of distinct names out of the six. It counts only when `set_by_id` is one of this session's trusted humans (by id, as RT-6.25 step 8): two sessions in one home may differ. A read holds only until the next one, nothing on disk and no last good record: the exact 404 body means off at once, any other 404 (a registrar before 0.8.0, a proxy) is a failed read (`registrar has no debug endpoint`, logged once), and a failed read, a record that does not verify and one set by someone else are off too, each with why. The end (`until_ms`) and `exp` are checked again at every use between reads.
3. **The lines of an event** (`debug.remember`). While a category is on, every event the server delivers of its home or of a DM (not a guest channel, not the lobby, not its own stall or advice events) is remembered in memory (the newest 200; its reads may take 2 s, `DEBUG_EVENT_SEC`) with one line per category: `wake`, the rule that woke it, sent it to the digest or gated it (a mention, a DM, a reaction on its ask, departed or not here, to all, a broadcast, its own thread, a plain post naming no agent, which wakes only the 👑, another agent's, the digest, gated with why; RT-6.2, RT-6.6 step 5, RT-6.16, RT-6.21) and how it came (pushed, held for the digest, returned by `wait_for_events`); `identity`, the author's class (trusted human, peer or foreign agent with its owner and whether attestation or description gave it, other; ADR-001, ADR-003), `mandated`, `root_from_trusted_human`; `sig`, the records the server relied on (each its own row in the card, step 5): the debug record (kid, exp), the author's attestation (kid, exp, or none: its description decides), the ask mode and its source, the mandate or why none; `asks`, the answer's ask with its age and why a reaction counts or not (trusted, this ask, within 24 h; RT-6.11), and how many asks are open and queued; `calls`, what the server refused or changed in the agent's tool calls since the last event; `props`, the event's props as they came.
4. **What it never shows.** No key, signature or token, whole or in part (a record shows its `kid` and `exp`). A call shows its arguments by name and length only, never their values (`reply_in_thread(root_id 26, message 52, props 2): kind RESULT put first`), a refused call only its tool. Props are shown by `a2a.props_view`: `attachments` as one line with their count and titles (`[1 card, title: …]`), no text or fallback; a value under a key naming a secret (`token`, `key`, `secret`, `passw`, `auth`, `bearer`, `credential`, `jwt`, `private`, `signature`; any case, at any depth) as its length (`<23 chars>`), and so is any text of 40 characters or more that looks like a compact JWS (three base64url parts joined by dots), whatever its key; every other text over 500 characters cut. Only `props out` leaves the server's own `debug` attachment out; in `props in` a card titled `debug` counts like any other, so a post cannot hide a card from the view.
5. **The attachment** (`server._with_debug`). When the agent posts in its home (`post_message`, `reply_in_thread`, `ask_human`, the shell's `POST /v1/post` and `/v1/reply`) while a category is on, the server adds a grey attachment titled `debug` after any card the post has (an ask's keeps its own first). It opens with the event it is about: the same thread's last delivered event, else the channel's last delivered, marked `a guess: none in this thread`, or none. Then a two-column markdown table (`what`, `value (times in UTC)`; Mattermost renders a table in an attachment's text only with its header row), one row per fact with the label in bold and a value as literal text (backslash, `` ` ``, `*`, `_`, `[`, `]`, `<`, `>`, `!` and `|` escaped, so an owner named as a link or image stays text): `event`, then per active category `wake`, `identity`, `sig` as one row per record relied on (`debug record`, `attestation` of a peer or foreign author, `ask mode`, `mandate`, each fact once, the mandate's reason only in its row), `asks`, `calls` (this call); `props` as two `json` code blocks below the table, `props in` (the event's) and `props out` (this post's), keys sorted and indented. Times read short and in UTC: `19:11:30Z` when it is today, else `2026-11-12 21:15Z`. The categories and who sees them are as before: the layout is presentation only. The whole is cut at 4,000 characters, the props blocks first, so the other lines stay. An agent that posts nothing adds nothing. Posts that answer no event carry no card: `announce` (HELLO, BYE), an ask's link post in the thread it came from, the 👀/⏳ notes, the permission-relay DMs (which may go to a human this session does not trust) and the server's stall and system replies; the server's own events carry no block either. A failure, or lines that take more than 2 s, leave the post without it.
6. **DMs and the agent's own view.** A DM may get the attachment when it holds no more than the other side could see already (the owner's decision of 2026-11-12): the setting is the sender's home's; with a peer agent (the same owner, ADR-001) or a trusted human all active categories, with a foreign agent or any other person only `props` and `calls`, which describe the post itself (`wake`, `identity`, `sig` and `asks` show this session's trust decisions). Its lines come from that DM's events only, never the home's. The agent gets the lines of its home's events, only while a category is on: as a `debug` field in `wait_for_events` and as a block after the post in its channel tag's body (RT-6.12, escaped like the post), `props in` cut at 1,000 characters, `calls` what was refused or changed since its last view.
7. **Never** in a guest channel, the lobby or a group message (RT-6.22): nothing is remembered there, and a post there carries no attachment.
8. **`bus_info`** shows `debug`: `categories`, `until` and `set_by`; or empty `categories` and `why` (no keys pinned, no debug endpoint, none set, set by someone not trusted, not read, refused, ended).
9. **What it never grants.** The lines report decisions; none is taken on them. Props are no secret (every member reads them through the API) and no proof (whoever may post may set them): a prop that claims an assignment proves none (RT-6.16 step 8). The view is the own server's: a line about another agent's server would be made up.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 2 | No keys pinned | Nothing is read; debug off, `bus_info` says why |
| 2 | The registrar answers 404 with exactly `{"error":"no debug set"}` | Off from this read |
| 2 | Any other 404 (a registrar before 0.8.0, a proxy), a 400, a timeout, a 5xx | A failed read: off until a read goes through, logged (no endpoint: once); never kept from before |
| 2 | The record does not verify (wrong key, type, home, expired, ended, bad categories) | Off, logged once per reason |
| 2 | The record was set by someone this session does not trust | Off, logged once |
| 2 | `until_ms` passes between two reads | Off at once |
| 3 | A thread, an owner or a record cannot be read for a line, or the lines take more than 2 s | That event goes out without lines, at once, logged at info |
| 5 | No event delivered in that channel while debug is on | The attachment says so; `calls` and `props out` still show |
| 5 | The attachment cannot be made | The post goes out without it, logged at info |
| 6 | A DM partner cannot be told (a group message, a DM with oneself) | No attachment |

---

## 6.32 Self-update between sessions

**Purpose:** corral's host copy follows the release the registrar's operator names, by itself, between sessions, while the code's signature stays the owner's: the registrar distributes the release keys once and names a version, or `latest`, the newest release tag the host's release check knows that is not withdrawn; a host installs only a newer, not withdrawn release tag signed by a key it pinned, only when no session of the host copy runs, and only while not held (ADR-011 phase 3, the owner's decisions of 2026-11-13). Every other host update stays RT-6.29's.

**Trigger:** corral's `postEnd` (the dispatcher, then the recorded release's stop hook, RT-6.28 step 9); the host's release check (RT-6.30), for the notice; `mm-agent-host-install.sh --hold` or `--unhold`; a session's start, the end of a turn, `bus_info`, `--check`.

**Participants:** Host session hooks, Shell CLI, MCP server; corral, Registrar, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement — what runs on the host is still only a tag a pinned release key signed; the registrar's key decides which version and offers keys once, never replaces them, and nothing a sandbox can write takes part; `#reliable` Never wedges — the update runs after the bot is gone, never fails the end hook, and every refusal leaves `current` as it was

### Sequence

1. **The stop switch.** `mm-agent-host-install.sh --hold` writes `release-hold` beside `releases/` (`CB_BUS_HOST_DIR`'s parent, 0600, one rename; out of `XDG_STATE_HOME`'s reach like `release-signers`), `--unhold` removes it; both alone, logged. Anything of that name holds, a symlink too (fail safe), and only a person on the host lifts it. `--release` still installs by hand while held.
2. **The record.** The registrar (0.9.0; `latest` from 0.10.0) publishes one record for its installation, nothing per team and no chat command: `GET <registrar>/register/v1/release` (`MM_REGISTER_URL` `/agents` → `/release`, else `CB_MM_CHAT_URL` + `/register/v1/release`) answers `{"record": "<compact JWS>"}`, or 404 with exactly `{"error":"no release set"}`: no target and no keys offered. Header `{"alg":"EdDSA","typ":"mm-agent-release+jwt","kid":…}`, the attestation key; claims `signers` (1 to 16 distinct `ssh-ed25519` or `sk-ssh-ed25519@openssh.com` keys, `<type> <base64>`, canonical), `version` (`v<x.y.z>`, or `latest`, that lowercase word exactly, which the operator sets with `RELEASE_VERSION=latest`; optional: keys only means no target), `set_at_ms` (the registrar's start time: it orders and keys nothing), `iat`, `exp` (an hour later). It counts only verified (`attest.verify_release`) with the pinned registrar keys (`MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS`, RT-6.24): a pinned kid, the release type, `exp` and `iat` with 60 s of skew, the claims' shapes, each key decoded as an SSH key blob of its type. Without pinned registrar keys nothing is read. The MCP server never reads it: the host copy's own Python does (`mattermost_ai_agent_bus.selfupdate`, `-I`, from `/`, an environment of `PATH`, `HOME`, `LC_ALL`, the registrar's keys and URLs and TLS and proxy settings only, no Authorization header, 5 s, at most 8 KiB, no redirect, stopped after 30 s), and prints fixed lines (`STATUS`, `WHY`, `KID`, `VERSION` as `x.y.z` or `latest`, `SIGNER`) that `mm_registrar_release` (`mm-agent-lib.sh`) checks again, every key with `mm_release_parse_keys`. Any other 404, an error, a redirect or a record that does not verify is a failed read and changes nothing.
3. **Keys pinned once.** A host takes the offered keys only when it has none: no `release-signers` and no `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`. They go into `release-signers` as a keyed `--release` run writes it (RT-6.29 step 4: 0600, one rename, a directory of the user's own), and the log names the registrar key that signed the record. From then on a record whose key set differs from the pinned one (compared as sets of keys, by type and decoded key: order, comments and spelling do not count) is refused as a whole: no update, logged, shown in the notice and in `bus_info`. Changing keys stays a person's step on the host (`--release v<x.y.z> --replace-signers` with `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`, `--forget-signers`). A `release-signers` refused as in RT-6.29 step 1 refuses the record too.
4. **Only forward, only signed.** With a version named, keys pinned and their set the record's, the installer goes on only when the version is newer than `current`'s (never down: `--rollback` stays manual; a `-dev` host copy whose `plugin.json` already says the named version stays as it is and does not move to the signed release), then runs the host's release check (RT-6.30) so the withdrawn versions are current, and goes on only when `release-withdrawn.env` exists after it (no check ever passed on this host: no self-update, logged) and does not list the version (RT-6.29 step 2; no `--withdrawn-ok`); then it installs it exactly as `--release v<x.y.z>` (RT-6.29 steps 2–6: the fetch without `+`, the annotated tag, its SSH signature by a pinned key, its own name, the version in its tree, the tag object pinned in `releases/`), never with `--downgrade`, `--withdrawn-ok` or `--replace-signers`. A host without release keys (unchecked mode) never updates by itself. **`latest`** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-13, ADR-011) names no version but a rule: the newest tag in the release check's list (`TAG` in `release-withdrawn.env`, RT-6.30 steps 2 and 4: the newest three tags at `CB_RELEASE_URL`) that its `WITHDRAWN` lines do not list (`mm_release_latest` in `mm-agent-lib.sh`). When the list the last check left says that tag is not newer, nothing more is done (no network; the daily check, RT-6.30 step 5, brings a newer tag into the list); otherwise, or with no list yet, the check runs as above and the newest such tag of its new list is the target, from there exactly the path of a named version: newer than `current`'s, not withdrawn, installed as `--release v<x.y.z>`. That tag need not have passed the check: a newest tag that fails the signature check (unsigned, another key) or any other `--release` check is refused at install time like a named version, and the host does not step back to an older tag that would pass; it stays as it is until a newer tag passes.
5. **Between sessions.** The stop hook (`90-mm-session-stop.sh` of the release the session recorded, so 0.86.0 or newer: a session that started on an older release ends without it) first stops the host process and removes or keeps the bot as before (RT-6.1, RT-6.18); then, on every way out of the hook (its `EXIT` trap), it removes the session's release record and runs that release's installer with `--auto`, only for a release of the host copy's layout, from `/`, stdin closed, with an environment of the host's settings only, each passed when it is set: `PATH` (the hook's, without the entries a sandbox can write), `CB_BUS_HOST_DIR` (default the `current` beside the release's `releases/`), `UV_HTTP_TIMEOUT=60`, `HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`, `XDG_STATE_HOME`, `CORRAL_SANDBOX`, `CORRAL_SESSION_ID`, `CORRAL_WORKDIR`, `CB_RELEASE_URL`, `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`, `MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS`, `MM_REGISTER_URL`, `CB_MM_CHAT_URL`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `https_proxy`, `NO_PROXY`, `no_proxy`; no token, no registration secret. corral runs hooks without a timeout, so the hook bounds it: after 600 s the installer and its children get `TERM` (`mm_bounded`; its `EXIT` trap removes its lock and the half-made release, `current` stays), and git gives up a transfer slower than 1 byte/s for 60 s (`http.lowSpeedLimit`, `http.lowSpeedTime`). Its output goes to `release-auto.out` in the state directory; its exit status never reaches corral. `--auto` takes no other option and keeps every guard of the installer: not inside corral, not from a tree a sandbox can write, one installer at a time (`.install.lock`, which holds the installer's pid: a lock whose installer is gone is taken over and logged, RT-6.28 step 1), none while a release check runs. It writes corral's hook files (`current.hooks/*`) only as every install does, when the dispatcher's own text changed, which it is meant never to do; corral would then ask for approval at the next launch (RT-6.28 step 4). After the lock it updates only when no other session of the host copy runs: no release record other than this session's and no live host-managed session file (RT-6.28 steps 1 and 4); a state directory that cannot tell counts as busy, and the last session to end updates. A running session keeps its version (RT-6.28 step 8).
6. **One line per decision.** Each run appends one line to `release-auto.log` in the host state directory (the newest 200 kept): held, no registrar keys, no release set, a failed read and why, keys pinned, keys refused, keys only, not newer, withdrawn, other sessions run, updated (with the signer), or `not updated:` and the installer's reason. The terminal gets one line, `mm-bus: self-update: …`, only when something happened or waits (updated, refused, failed, waiting, held), none for nothing to do (no registrar keys, no release set, keys only, not newer). `release-auto.env` beside it holds the words the notice uses: `REGISTRAR` (`ok`, `none`, `failed`, `nokeys`), `TARGET` (`x.y.z`; for `latest`, `latest (v<x.y.z>)` with the tag it stands for as of the last check, or `latest` while none is known; the decision lines name it the same way), `KEYS` (`none`, `match`, `mismatch`, `bad`), `AT`, `RESULT`, `RESULT_AT`; a failed read keeps the last `TARGET` and `KEYS`.
7. **The notice.** The release check (RT-6.30, daily, and `--check`) reads the record the same way for the notice only: it writes `release-auto.env`, pins nothing and installs nothing. The start hook, `--check` (`self-update:`), the turn-end banner and `bus_info` say what will happen, in plain words, for a server that runs from a release of the host copy: `self-update held on this host (mm-agent-host-install.sh --unhold lifts it)`, `the registrar names a release key this host doesn't trust: no self-update`, `the release keys on this host can't be used (see release-auto.log): no self-update`, or `updates itself to v<x.y.z> when this session ends` (`updates itself to latest (v<x.y.z>) when this session ends` for `latest`; nothing while its tag is not known) (`mm_release_auto_notice` in `mm-agent-lib.sh`, `release.auto_line` in the server: the same text). The banner leaves out "a human installs it" then. `bus_info`'s `release.auto`: `applies` (false for the plugin or a checkout, which never update themselves), `held`, `registrar`, `target`, `keys`, `read_at`, `last`, `will`. The agent never runs the installer.
8. **Once per host, by a person:** install 0.86.0 or newer by hand (`--release`), with `MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS` and the registrar's URL in the environment corral runs its hooks with; keys come with the first record, or are pinned before with `CB_RELEASE_SIGNERS`. A host before 0.87.0 refuses a record that names `latest` as a whole (`bad version`, a failed read: no keys pinned, no update): the operator names a fixed version of 0.87.0 or newer until every host runs one, then `latest`. Harnesses without corral have no end hook and update as before (RT-6.29); nothing of the bus's function or security depends on this scenario.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | `release-hold` is a directory | `--unhold` refuses; it holds until removed by hand |
| 2 | No `MM_REGISTRAR_KEYS` | Nothing read, nothing pinned, no update; logged |
| 2 | 404 `{"error":"no release set"}` | No target, no keys offered; `TARGET` cleared; logged |
| 2 | Any other 404, a 5xx, a timeout, a redirect, an answer over 8 KiB, a record that does not verify (another key, another type, expired, bad signers or version) | A failed read: nothing changes, the last `TARGET` and `KEYS` stay for the notice; logged with why |
| 2 | The host copy's release has no Python environment | A failed read; logged |
| 3 | The record's keys differ from the pinned ones | Refused: no update, `KEYS=mismatch`, the notice says the registrar names a key this host doesn't trust |
| 3 | `release-signers` cannot be written | No update; logged |
| 4 | The version is not newer, or withdrawn | Nothing installed; logged |
| 4 | `latest`, and the release check knows no tag that is not withdrawn | No self-update; logged (`failed`); `TARGET` says `latest` |
| 4 | `latest`, and its newest tag fails a `--release` check (unsigned, a key not pinned, moved, another version in its tree) | `not updated:` and the installer's reason; `current` unchanged, no older tag taken; tried again at the next session end |
| 2 | A host before 0.87.0 reads a record naming `latest` | A failed read (`bad version`): nothing pinned, nothing installed |
| 4 | The tag is unsigned, signed by a key not pinned, moved, named otherwise, or its tree has another version; the fetch or `uv sync` fails | `not updated:` and the installer's reason; `current` unchanged; tried again at the next session end |
| 5 | Another session of the host copy runs, or the state directory cannot tell | Nothing installed; the last session to end updates |
| 5 | Another installer or a release check runs | `not updated`; tried again at the next session end |
| 5 | The installer is killed, or stopped after 600 s | `current` unchanged (RT-6.28); a lock it left is taken over by the next installer, the release check does not wait for it; logged |
| 4 | No release check ever passed on this host (no `release-withdrawn.env` after the check `--auto` runs) | No self-update; logged |
| 5 | The update fails in any way | The bot was removed or kept before; the hook exits as it would have |
| 7 | `release-auto.env` missing or with fields out of their words | No self-update notice; `bus_info` shows what holds |

---

## 6.33 An agent attaches a file

**Purpose:** A human asks an agent for a bar chart; until 0.90.0 it could only post text bars. Now an agent attaches files to its posts, images above all, although its MCP server may run on the host, outside its sandbox (RT-6.18): the server never opens a path the agent names, the bytes come from inside the sandbox (ADR-012).

**Trigger:** The agent runs `mm-agent-post.sh --file PATH … MESSAGE`, or calls `attach(name, text)` and then `post_message` / `reply_in_thread` with `files`.

**Participants:** Shell CLI, MCP server, Agent skill; Agent runtime, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement — no host file reaches the chat, a link in the workspace neither; `#usable` Reachability — a chart, a PDF or a CSV arrives as a file, shown inline; `#efficient` Low overhead — the bytes never pass through the model's context

### Sequence

1. **The bytes.** `mm-agent-post.sh` reads each `--file` (at most 10) itself, in the sandbox with the agent's own rights, and sends it as the body of `POST /v1/files?name=<base name>` (RT-6.17: the session's key, `curl --data-binary @PATH`), with `root=<id>` for a reply, `to=<agent>` for a DM, `channel=<id or name>` for `--channel`, else the home. The `attach(name, text, channel, to)` tool carries a small text file in its argument instead (at most 100 KB, `attach.TEXT_MAX`; a base64 image would cost about one token per three bytes). The server opens no path, follows no link and reads nothing of the workspace.
2. **The channel** (`hookapi.upload_channel`). A file goes where the agent's posts go: the home, a guest channel, the lobby (the channels the bus watches, RT-6.22) or a DM of the bot; a group, any other channel, or an id that is none of the bot's is refused (`403`). `to` (also of `attach`) names an agent for the DM with it and refuses a person, as `post_message` does; a DM channel id the bot is in is allowed like for a post, a person's too.
3. **The checks** (`attach.check`), before anything goes to Mattermost. The type comes from the bytes, never from the name: PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP and PDF by their magic bytes; `txt`, `md`, `csv`, `tsv`, `json`, `log`, `yaml`/`yml` only as valid UTF-8 without NUL. The name's extension must name the detected type: a key renamed `chart.png` is refused. The name is cut to its base name, control and format characters dropped, at most 100 characters (`attach.NAME_MAX`) with the extension kept. SVG is refused: it can carry script, and Mattermost shows it only with `EnableSVGs`; render it as PNG. An empty file is refused. The hook API reads at most 10 MiB (`attach.FILE_MAX`) for this route and refuses a longer `Content-Length` before it reads a byte (`413`); every other route keeps 64 KiB. A text file's links are not rewritten as a post's are (RT-6.15): Mattermost shows a text file's preview as plain text, so no label can hide its target, and rewriting would change the data of a CSV or JSON file.
4. **The upload.** The server uploads the file into that channel as the bot (`POST /api/v4/files`, multipart, through `ChatPort.upload_file`) and answers `{file_id, name, type, size, channel}`. Mattermost's own limits apply on top: over its `MaxFileSize`, or with file attachments off, its refusal is the answer (`422`, the tool's `refused`). The server remembers the upload, in memory: id, name, channel, time.
5. **The post.** `post_message` and `reply_in_thread` take `files` (the shell's `POST /v1/post` and `/v1/reply` too): at most 10 ids (`attach.POST_MAX`, Mattermost's limit), each one this server uploaded within the hour (`attach.PENDING_SEC`) and no post has taken yet, each in the post's channel (the one given, else the thread's, else the home: Mattermost attaches only a file of the post's own channel). Anything else is refused and nothing is posted: a human's file, another agent's, one used or expired, one in another channel (with the channel to upload it to). The ids are taken when the post goes out and given back when Mattermost refuses it. With files the message may be empty. The post's result names its files (`files`, as a slim post does, RT-6.23); the debug card's call line counts them (`files 2`), never their names or content (RT-6.31 step 4).
6. **Making a chart.** The agent writes the file into its workspace, e.g. `uv run --with matplotlib python chart.py` writing `chart.png`, then `mm-agent-post.sh --file chart.png --root <id> "…"`. OpenCode and pi use the same shell route; their own code needs nothing (RT-6.20, RT-6.27).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | `--file` names no readable file, or more than 10 | Exit 2, nothing sent |
| 1 | A `--file` over 10 MiB (`wc -c`) | Exit 1 `over 10 MiB` before any upload |
| 1 | No server of this session answers | Exit 3 and what to do (RT-6.17), nothing posted |
| 1 | `attach` text over 100 KB | `refused`, with the shell route as the way for a larger file |
| 2 | A group, another channel the bot is in, a channel id that does not exist or is not the bot's, a channel of the team it is not in, `to` naming a person | `403` or `400`, nothing uploaded; a DM channel id the bot is in is allowed like for a post |
| 3 | The bytes are no allowed type, or not the type the extension names; SVG; an empty file; a name without extension | `400` with the reason (the tool's `refused`); that file is not uploaded, earlier files of the same command stay uploaded, pending for an hour; the script exits 1 |
| 3 | `Content-Length` over 10 MiB | `413` before the body is read; the script exits 1 |
| 4 | Mattermost refuses the upload (over its `MaxFileSize`, attachments off, no permission) | `422` with its reason; nothing remembered; the script exits 1 |
| 5 | An id not uploaded by this server, used, older than an hour, pushed out by more than 50 newer uploads (`attach.PENDING_MAX`), or more than 10 | `posted: false` with the reason, nothing posted; the good ids stay usable |
| 5 | A file uploaded to another channel than the post's | `posted: false`, the post's channel named to upload it again; nothing posted |
| 5 | Mattermost refuses the post | Its reason as for any post (RT-6.14); the ids are given back |
| 4 | The server restarts between upload and post | The ids are forgotten: upload again |

---

## 6.34 A host logs in to Keycloak

**Purpose:** The NATS adapter (ADR-014, the NATS home of RT-6.36) needs two things before it may connect: a valid Keycloak access token of the session's responsible human, and the host's own NATS user key. This building block delivers both in the MCP server the plugin starts over stdio, with and without corral (the owner's decision 10, #154 step 2; the amendment of 2026-11-26 to the NATS decision, ADR-014, points 6 and 7); in a keyless corral session that is the relay in the sandbox, which hands the host process access tokens and the nkey seed over the login channel (RT-6.18 step 12). The NATS home (RT-6.36) uses it; unconfigured, nothing changes.

**Against a stolen token** (#154 step 3, built): the login service watches each Keycloak session (`sid`) and each host key (RT-6.31). A single network change is reported; two returns to an earlier network within 30 minutes refuse that session for 30 minutes, a retry included, and a host key doing so is reported for the owner to rotate. Each move and copy is published for the owner on `<presence>.notice.<owner anchor>.<verdict>.v1` (one subject per verdict, so a move never erases a waiting copy; one record per id and verdict in 30 minutes, so a refused session's retries tell once), a subject only the login service may publish, and the owner's live servers turn a copied one into one DM: the agent the record names tells it if it is live, else the owner's live agent with the smallest name, from presence alone — only the owner's own agents count. A server remembers the newest record it told in its state, so a restart does not repeat it. Nobody tells when no live server of the owner is left, when the responsible human is unknown, or when the DM fails; the record stays in the stream within its 24 hours and the console and the log keep it. The notice trusts the owner's own servers: the one that tells may be the thief's if a copied host key let them run an agent of that owner, and a compromised server can stay silent. The service holds no Keycloak admin right, so the token stays valid at Keycloak until it runs out or the owner ends the session (the owner's decision). The limits are RISK-26's and 05's: a thief on the same prefix, a shared NAT or proxy, a use one after another, a rotation among three networks, and a thief keeping the owner out by alternating. **Decided, not built yet** (#154 step 4): the offline token gets a maximum lifetime (today none: 30 idle days, renewed by use), and DPoP binds the token to a key (against an agent that reads the token only with the key in hardware).

**Trigger:** `CB_LOGIN_ISSUER` and `CB_LOGIN_CLIENT_ID` are set, and the plugin's MCP server starts over stdio: outside corral, or in a corral sandbox without the host hooks, the server itself; in a keyless corral session the relay, once the host's handshake answer offers the login (RT-6.18 step 12).

**Participants:** MCP server; Keycloak, Mattermost REST API, Responsible human

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Secret confinement — tokens and the nkey seed stay in the MCP server's store and memory, under corral in the sandbox's home where the agent can read them (RISK-26), and in a keyless session the access tokens and the seed also in the host process; in no log, error, environment or argument vector; the approval reaches the human at this machine and the responsible human, never a channel, never the device code; `#usable` Reachability — one approval, then the login resumes from the stored refresh token

### Sequence

1. **Configuration** (`login.settings_from_env`). `CB_LOGIN_ISSUER` (https; http only on a loopback host such as localhost, for a test realm) and `CB_LOGIN_CLIENT_ID` (a public client, no secret). Both unset: the login is off, silently, and nothing here runs. One without the other, or a wrong value: a clear error in the log and the login stays off. `CB_LOGIN_NKEY=ephemeral|host` (default `ephemeral`) picks where the nkey lives (step 7). Decision 10 has the server keep the host key, but two sessions of one owner on one host would then share one key and so one NATS agent name (it comes from the owner's anchor and the host's key, ADR-016); the default stays `ephemeral` until #163 settles it, and both work without a code change.
2. **Start, in the background** (`login.start`). The MCP server starts it in `server._lifespan` and stops it there; in a keyless corral session the relay starts the same class (RT-6.18 step 12), and the host process starts in `hosted.services()` only its view of it (`login.start_relayed`, no login and no store of its own); one per process, and a second `start` changes nothing. The session never waits for it. The running object is `login.current()`, for the adapter.
3. **Resume or approve.** With a refresh token in the store (step 8) the first call needs no approval. Otherwise the OAuth 2.0 device authorization grant (RFC 8628) starts against the endpoints in `<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`, with `client_id` and `scope=offline_access` (no ID token, ADR-014), and polls the token endpoint at the `interval` it was given: `authorization_pending` waits, `slow_down` adds 5 s, `access_denied` and `expired_token` end it (`ApprovalDenied`, `ApprovalExpired`). A network error or a 5xx does not end it: the poll waits longer each time (doubling up to 60 s) until the device code's expiry. The discovery document must name the configured issuer and every endpoint it gives must have the issuer's scheme, host and port (http only when the issuer itself is on loopback); the verification link is checked the same way before it is sent. An empty responsible human is refused before any refresh or approval. Callers that wait for a token share the result of the one running approval; after any approval that failed (declined, expired, refused, or any other error such as a token without the role) no new approval starts for 60 s (`COOLDOWN_SEC`), so a broken realm does not send a DM on every call. During it a caller is told why: the fixed reason, and for any other error (Keycloak not reachable, no way to tell the human) that error's own text, on one line and cut at 120 characters, as long as the process that saw it is the one asking. That mark is kept beside the token (`keystore.cooldown_entry`), so a process that restarts obeys it too, and it records why: the deadline is a wall-clock one, since a monotonic one means nothing in another process, while the process that set it keeps its own monotonic deadline and skips its own mark. It is read before every new approval, not once per object, so a cooldown another session set in the meantime counts, and a login that succeeds deletes it. A mark without a finite deadline is none, and its reason is reported only when it is one this module writes, else as `failed`; the module writes none beyond those 60 s, so a later deadline can only be a wall clock that moved backwards, and it is capped when it is taken in, once, so the lock never outlasts the cooldown. Deadlines and token expiry use the monotonic clock, that stored mark excepted. A token's lifetime is the shorter of the response's `expires_in` and its own `exp` minus `iat`, both durations, so a host clock that runs ahead of Keycloak's does not make every token stale; only a token without `iat` is measured by its `exp` against the host's clock, and no token is held longer than one day (`MAX_LIFETIME_SEC`). A token is handed out only while that lifetime, counted from when its request went out (a slow answer only shortens it), leaves at least 60 s on both the monotonic and the wall clock, since the monotonic clock stands still while the machine sleeps. An `exp` or `iat` that is no positive number below 2^34 (a NaN, an infinity, an integer too large for a float), or an `exp` not after the `iat`, makes the token unusable before anything is stored.
4. **Tell the human** (`login_notice`), never in a channel and never the device code: whoever opens the link and logs in becomes the owner. `Combined` tries every way and the approval goes on when one of them reached the human. At this machine (`Shown`): the server opens `verification_uri_complete` in the desktop's browser, only an https link with the issuer's scheme, host and port, by an argument vector without a shell (`/usr/bin/open` on macOS; `xdg-open` only with `DISPLAY` or `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, never a console browser, which would take the stdio; at most 10 s), and writes the address and the user code to its stderr, saying whether a browser opened; the harness keeps that in its MCP log (Claude Code shows it with `--debug`). By DM (`DMNotifier`): `verification_uri_complete` and the `user_code` go as a direct message to the responsible human (`owners.responsible_human`); in a keyless corral session the host process sends it, with a link it builds itself (RT-6.18 step 12). In the home channel the server posts an ask that says only that a login approval for this agent waits in the direct messages; it is registered like an `ask_human` ask (`asks.add`), so the open asks and the reminders (RT-6.19) track it. When the approval ends, approved or not, the ask is settled (`asks.resolve`, ✅); a new approval settles an ask still open first, and an ask left over from a session that ended mid-approval is settled when the login starts. If the post in the home fails after the DM went out, that is logged and the approval goes on. A reaction to this ask approves nothing for the agent (SKILL.md, the MCP instructions). Stderr counts as delivery, so a session without a chat, or whose human the chat does not know, still polls: the person check of step 5 is the barrier, not the delivery. Only when no way reached the human is it `NotifyError`, and the approval ends.
5. **The token and its person.** After every approval and every refresh the access token's payload is read, without a signature check (the login service checks it, services/auth/README.md), and `preferred_username` must be the responsible human, compared ignoring case (the NATS side compares exactly, RT-6.37 step 2). If not, or with no responsible human: the person is checked before anything is stored: the refresh token is revoked in Keycloak (`revocation_endpoint` of the discovery document, `token_type_hint=refresh_token`, `client_id` in the body, best effort), the tokens are dropped from memory and the store, the log says that another person approved, the human gets a DM, and `WrongHuman` is raised, none of them with any token content. The token's `iss` must be the issuer and its `azp` the client id; the response must carry a refresh token and the access token the realm role `offline_access` (`realm_access.roles`), else `LoginError`, and the refresh token of that response is revoked in Keycloak like a wrong person's, since it is never stored and its session would stay open. The Mattermost username equals the Keycloak username is a documented prerequisite, not enforced. The approval link need not be hidden from the agent itself: the person check is the barrier, not secrecy of the link.
6. **The refresh token is used once.** `access_token()` returns a token with at least 60 s left and refreshes otherwise, with the refresh token grant. The refresh token of every response is stored as soon as the person, issuer, client and role have been checked (step 5), before it is used or the access token is handed out; the host never sends one a second time (Keycloak's reuse detection is off by decision, ADR-014 point 7). `invalid_grant` drops the stored token and starts a new approval (step 3). The refresh token always persists in the store the server was given (step 8), one entry per (issuer, client, human): the file name carries a hash of the three, the file records them and a mismatch counts as missing, so a token never goes to another realm or person. Load, refresh and store happen under `flock` on a lock file per entry (taken without blocking the event loop; every store call runs in a thread, since a keychain answers through a subprocess) and the token is loaded again after the lock is taken: sessions of one OS user take turns, and a token another process stored meanwhile is used, never the old one; only one of them starts an approval. Only a lock another holder has makes a session wait; a file system that offers no locks at all (`ENOLCK`, as on NFS or SMB) logs a warning and the login goes on without the lock: at worst one session gets `invalid_grant` and asks for one more approval, no token is disclosed.
7. **The nkey** (`nkey()`). The host's NATS user key, made with the `nkeys` package: the public key and `sign(nonce)`, which the auth callout checks (services/auth/README.md, "Auth callout"). The seed is kept, not only the public key, so later messages can be signed. `ephemeral` keeps it in memory, a new key per process; `host` keeps it in the same store as the refresh token and reuses it. It is made or loaded once, under a lock, whichever thread asks first.
8. **The stores** (`keystore.py`). `login.default_store()` is `keystore.default()`, chosen on first use in a thread and the same for the whole process. Under corral (`CORRAL_SANDBOX`) it is always the 0600 file in `$HOME/.local/state/mm-agent-bus/login`, the sandbox's own home, never a keychain. Else it is what the host's first start recorded in the marker entry `store_backend` beside the file store in `login/` of the host's state directory (`session_file.host_state_dir`), and it sticks, so later starts never split the entries between two stores; at that first start, under a lock, the system keychain if it answers a probe within 3 s, else the 0600 file. The probe reads first (an existing nkey seed item, attributes only); with nothing to read it writes a worthless value under a name of the process's own, reads it back and removes it. `MacKeychain` keeps generic passwords of the service `mm-agent-bus-login`, the account the entry name, through `/usr/bin/security -i`: the command line, the value base64-encoded, goes on its stdin, never in an argument vector, and a write is read back, since `security -i` reports no failure in its exit status; a value too long for one such line goes to a 0600 file in `overflow/` of the store directory instead, with a warning naming the entry, and a read looks there first. `SecretTool` keeps them in libsecret (GNOME Keyring, KWallet's Secret Service) through `secret-tool` from a fixed path (`/usr/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, never `PATH`), the value on its stdin. A keychain call that hangs is killed after 10 s and counts as an error. Every store locks with `flock` on lock files in the same 0700 directory, a keychain having no locks of its own, so all stores of a host take turns. `Ephemeral` is memory only. `HostFile` is one file per value, mode 0600, in a 0700 directory. A write is a temporary file renamed over the old one, then the directory is synced; a read refuses a symlink, a file of another owner and one readable by group or world. The cooldown mark of step 3 is an entry of the same store, beside the token's and not inside it, since the token's file is rewritten on every refresh. **Moving an earlier store** (`migrate_from`): outside corral the server moves, once per process and before its key is made, the refresh token, its cooldown mark and the nkey seed that an earlier version kept in `login/` of the host's state directory into the chosen store, each under the new store's lock: an entry is copied only where the new store lacks it, and removed from the old place only once the new store holds it; a store at the same place, also behind the wrapper that chooses it late, is left alone. Under corral that directory is out of the sandbox's reach; the host process of a keyless session moves it (RT-6.18 step 12). Without either, the first start after the upgrade asks for one new approval.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | One variable without the other, an issuer that is neither https nor loopback http, an unknown `CB_LOGIN_NKEY` | Error in the log naming the variable; the login stays off, nothing else changes |
| 3 | The human declines, or the device code expires | The ask is settled, `ApprovalDenied` or `ApprovalExpired`, nothing stored; the next `access_token()` starts a new approval after the 60 s cool-down, which a restart does not reset |
| 3 | Network error or 5xx while polling | The poll waits longer and goes on until the code expires |
| 3 | The discovery document or the verification link is not the issuer's | `LoginError`, no approval, no link sent |
| 3 | Keycloak or its discovery document is not reachable | `LoginError` naming the exception type; stored tokens stay; the background start logs it and ends |
| 4 | No chat, or the human is unknown to the chat | No DM, logged by its type; the approval goes on, shown in the browser and on stderr |
| 4 | No browser, stderr closed and no DM: no way reached the human | `NotifyError`, nothing is polled and the link goes nowhere else; the device code Keycloak already gave is left to expire unused. The 60 s cool-down applies (step 3) |
| 5 | Another person approved or refreshed | Tokens dropped, log line and DM without token content, `WrongHuman` |
| 5 | The token lacks the issuer, client or role | `LoginError`, the refresh token revoked, nothing stored, 60 s cool-down |
| 5 | The token's `exp` or `iat` is no usable time, or it expires before it was issued | `LoginError`, the refresh token revoked, nothing stored, 60 s cool-down after an approval |
| 6 | `invalid_grant` on a refresh | Stored refresh token dropped, new approval |
| 6 | The file system offers no locks | Warning in the log, the login goes on without the lock |
| 8 | A stored file is a symlink, not the user's, or open to group or world | `KeyStoreError` naming the entry; the value is treated as missing, a new approval or a new nkey follows |
| 8 | The keychain does not answer the probe, or its tool is missing | The 0600 file, recorded in the marker |
| 8 | A keychain recorded earlier is gone (the marker sticks) | The 0600 file, with a warning; what the keychain holds is not found, so one new approval |
| 8 | The marker cannot be written | The 0600 file of that directory, unrecorded, with a warning |
| 8 | A keychain call fails or hangs past 10 s | `KeyStoreError`, the value treated as missing as above |


## 6.35 The login service manages an agent's consumers

**Purpose:** An agent on NATS reads only through push consumers the login service makes for it (ADR-014 point 16): it has no right on the JetStream API, and its only subscribe right is `deliver.<name>.>`. This scenario keeps those consumers in step with the credential, so that what an agent reads follows what it may read, and removes them when the agent signs off or its owner is stopped. Built in the login service (`services/auth/internal/consumers`); agents connect to it through the NATS home (RT-6.36).

**Trigger:** A host connects to NATS, renews its credential, says bye, or the emergency brake changes. The login service always runs with its own user in the tenant account (`CB_AUTH_JS_SEED_FILE`), the only holder of JetStream API rights; without it, it does not start: credentials carry `deliver.<name>.>`, and without someone keeping the consumers in step, read rights would no longer follow the credential.

**Participants:** Login service; NATS server, Host process

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Least privilege — an agent reads only the channels in its current credential, and nothing an agent sends can remove another agent's consumers; `#reliable` Recovery — history from a sequence after a gap, and nothing left behind by a host that vanished

### Sequence

1. **Start** (`Manager.EnsureStreams`). The login service makes the streams `BUS` (`<bus>.>`, 14 days, 1 GiB) and `PRESENCE` (`<presence>.>`, one message per subject, 24 h, 16 MiB) when they are missing, with `no_ack`, every `allow_*` switch and `mirror_direct` off, `deny_delete`, `deny_purge`, `discard: old` and size limits. It reads back what the server kept and refuses to start on a stream that differs in any of these, or whose subjects are not exactly `<prefix>.>`, which has a subject transform, a retention other than limits, a `max_age` above 14 days (`BUS`) or 24 h (`PRESENCE`), or a `duplicate_window` above 100 ms, which would widen the side channel over message ids (`ErrUnsafeStream`): the proof (`native_sender_test.go`) holds only for these settings, and it uses the same configuration.
2. **Every issue of a credential** (`Manager.Reconcile`, called by the auth callout after the user JWT is built and before the answer). Wanted: one consumer per granted channel (`<name>_ch_<channel>`, filter `<bus>.ch.<channel>.*`, deliver subject `deliver.<name>.ch.<channel>`), one per granted channel for people (`<name>_hu_<channel>`, filter `<bus>.hu.<channel>.*`, deliver subject `deliver.<name>.hu.<channel>`, ack none, new messages only, RT-6.42), one for direct messages (`<name>_dm`, `<bus>.dm.<name>.*`, `deliver.<name>.dm`) and one for presence (`<name>_presence`, filters `<presence>.*.*` (every agent's presence, `<owner>.<name>`, RT-6.37), `<presence>.attest.*.v1` (everyone's attestation, RT-6.41) and `<presence>.account.mattermost.*.v1` (the account records, RT-6.42), and `<presence>.crown.<channel>.*`, `<presence>.member.<channel>.*` and `<presence>.bridge.<channel>.v1` (the bridge's signal) for each granted channel only, last message per subject, `deliver.<name>.presence`). All with ack policy none, no flow control, an inactivity threshold of one hour, the agent and owner in their metadata; an agent's consumers are found by that metadata and by their known names, never by a name prefix, and the presence consumer records the agent's channels so that a bye or a reconcile needs no listing of all consumers. The record is written first with the old and the new channels together, then the channel consumers are made and removed, and only then is it cut to the new channels: a reconcile that fails in between leaves no channel consumer outside the record. A consumer that exists with these settings is kept with its position (a renewal every 15 min moves nothing); one of a channel no longer granted is removed, so a human removed from a channel stops their agent reading it at the next renewal; the presence consumer's filters follow the channels. A new channel consumer starts with new messages. A new host session reads the presence stream afresh: its hello carries the since key `@presence` until the bus has read the stored presences once, and the login service then deletes and recreates the presence consumer (last message per subject, the login stamp kept), so a restart within the inactivity hour does not keep the old position and see an empty stream; a renewal of a running session leaves the key out and keeps the position.
3. **History** (`since` in the hello, the host's CONNECT `auth_token` JSON, per channel the last stream sequence the agent has seen, and under the key `@dm`, which no channel name can be, the last one of its direct messages). Taken only for channels in the credential; others are ignored and logged. That channel's consumer starts again at the next sequence, so nothing comes twice, and never before the stream's first message. No message is read for it, only the stream's info: `BUS` keeps at most 14 days (checked at start), and only when its first message is older than that by more than a minute (the server expires old messages every few hundred ms, and clocks differ a little) is the start by time at now minus 14 days, which replays more than asked; a sequence past the last starts with new messages.
4. **Sign-off** (`Manager.WatchByes`). The service follows `<presence>.*.*` itself; a message `{"status": "bye"}` on `<presence>.<owner>.<name>` removes that agent's consumers made before the bye (the server's time from the message's metadata; a message without it is ignored). Every login stamps the server's time on the agent's presence consumer (`login` in its metadata); a bye older than the last login is ignored, so a bye handled after the same agent logged in again, or renewed, leaves its consumers. The sender is the subject's last token, which only that agent may publish on: a bye cannot be forged.
5. **Emergency brake** (`Manager.WatchBrake`, every 10 s). The consumers of every owner the brake holds are removed, all of them when it holds everyone or cannot be read; the brake also refuses the owner's next credential (services/auth/README.md).
6. **The service's own JetStream user** needs only these rights in the tenant account (a test runs the manager with exactly these): publish `$JS.API.STREAM.CREATE.<S>` and `$JS.API.STREAM.INFO.<S>`, `$JS.API.CONSUMER.*.<S>` and `$JS.API.CONSUMER.*.<S>.>` (create, info, delete, list, names) for `<S>` = `BUS` and `PRESENCE`; subscribe `_INBOX.>` for the API's answers and its own bye watcher. For the channel directory (RT-6.39) also publish `$JS.API.INFO`, `$JS.API.STREAM.CREATE.KV_BUS_DIRECTORY`, `$JS.API.STREAM.INFO.KV_BUS_DIRECTORY`, `$JS.API.DIRECT.GET.KV_BUS_DIRECTORY.>` and `$KV.BUS_DIRECTORY.>`, and for its key listings (the channels, an operator's trust index, the sweep of expired trust records) `$JS.API.CONSUMER.CREATE.KV_BUS_DIRECTORY.>` and `$JS.API.CONSUMER.DELETE.KV_BUS_DIRECTORY.*`; for the attestation (RT-6.41) `$JS.API.STREAM.MSG.GET.PRESENCE` and `<presence>.attest.*.v1`; for the account records (RT-6.42) `<presence>.account.mattermost.*.v1` (`services/auth/nats-server.example.conf`, pinned by its test). An agent's credential holds no right on `<bus>.hu`, the account records or the bridge's signal (`TestNoAgentMayPublishPeopleAccountRecordsOrTheBridgesSignal`). No other stream, no `$JS.API.STREAM.DELETE` or `PURGE`, no `MSG.GET` on `BUS`, no `MSG.DELETE`. The service's other user, the one that answers the callout in its account `AUTH`, subscribes to `$SYS.REQ.USER.AUTH` only and publishes nothing but one answer per request on the reply subject the server set (`allow_responses`); every user of the example has a publish and a subscribe rule, since one without may do that on any subject of its account (#172).
7. **A host that vanished.** Its consumers have no listener and JetStream removes them after the inactivity threshold (one hour).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | A stream exists with an unsafe setting, or JetStream cannot be reached | The login service does not start; the reason is in the log |
