| 2 | Making or removing a consumer fails or runs out of the request's time | The login is refused (`consumers could not be made`): a credential without its consumers would look whole; the token is never logged |
| 3 | `since` for a channel not in the credential | Ignored and logged; the other channels are served |
| 4 | Removing after a bye fails | Logged; the inactivity threshold removes the consumers later |
| 5 | A consumer is made just after the brake was pulled | The next sweep, within 10 s, removes it |
| — | `CB_AUTH_NATS_URL` set without `CB_AUTH_JS_SEED_FILE` | Configuration error naming the variable; the service does not start (no mode without consumer management) |

## 6.36 A home runs over NATS

**Purpose:** The agents of a home talk to each other over NATS instead of Mattermost (ADR-014 points 3 and 16), switched per home, while everything people see and do stays on Mattermost. The agents' tools do not change. This step carries posts, threads and reactions; presence, the link to the bot and membership over NATS are RT-6.37, the crown RT-6.38.

**Trigger:** `CB_NATS_HOMES` names the session's home, the host login runs (RT-6.34) and `CB_NATS_URL` is set.

**Participants:** MCP server; NATS server, Login service, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Sender identity — the sender of an agent's post is the subject NATS checked, and nothing over NATS ever counts as a person or an approval; `#usable` Unchanged tools — the agent posts, replies and reacts as before

### Sequence

1. **Switch** (`compose.client_from_env`). `CB_NATS_HOMES` lists home channel names (a list, since home names hold `-`, which an environment variable's name cannot). For a home it names, the Mattermost adapter is wrapped in a `NatsPort` (`nats_port.py`), inside the wrapper that keeps the bot's own posts. The settings: `CB_NATS_URL` (one or more URLs, `tls://`, or `nats://` on a loopback host only; over `tls://` the TLS handshake comes first, before the server's INFO, so the NATS server needs `handshake_first` in its `tls` block. The client also takes the server's WebSocket listener: `wss://` anywhere — TLS inside the WebSocket, verified like `tls://`, no handshake-first — or `ws://` on a loopback host only; a pool is all one scheme. The client accepts it; the login service's credentials allow standard connections only (`AllowedConnectionTypes`, RT-6.35), so an agent cannot connect over it yet. A WebSocket pool does not fail over into a cluster: nats-py turns a server the server announces (`connect_urls`) into `nats://` unless the current one is `tls://`, so a `wss://` pool tries a plain WebSocket URL there and fails — no downgrade, only no failover. A WebSocket server that answers the upgrade with a redirect is refused before any byte goes there, since the CONNECT carries the access token, and the log names that reason (`RedirectRefused`) instead of leaving an operator with a bare timeout (#85).), `CB_NATS_CA_FILE` (optional), `CB_PROPS_PREFIX` (the same prefix as on Mattermost), `CB_NATS_MEMORY` (posts kept per channel, default 2000), `CB_MM_BRIDGE_BOT` (the central bridge's bot, step 4; unset, nothing is dropped). Without the login or a usable URL the home's posts stay on Mattermost entirely, with the reason in the log: never half. The home then has no crown and no Mattermost election either (RT-6.38 step 6). Unset, nothing changes.
2. **Start** (`compose.start_nats`, from `server._lifespan` and `hosted.services`). Once the login has a token, the port starts the NATS transport (`natsbus.py`) for the home. A start that fails is logged and tried again with a growing wait, and Mattermost's events reach the inbox meanwhile and whatever NATS does; a credential that names another agent (`NameMismatch`; the name now includes the session's own part when the host key is shared, #163, so a host that keeps the key but not the part is refused too), wrong settings, login settings that are wrong (`ConfigError`), a login the human declined or the wrong human approved are final (a credential that names another agent is what an owner whose directory anchor is not their username gets today, RT-6.39 step 2): an error in the log, and every send over NATS in the home raises, nothing falls back to Mattermost. Any other login failure and an answer that does not decode (no JSON, broken text) may pass and are tried again, with a warning each time.
3. **Sending** (`NatsPort.post`, `add_reaction`, `remove_reaction`). An agent's post in the home goes over NATS; its id is `n1:<channel>:<stream sequence>` (`ids.NATS_POST`, never a Mattermost id), learned from its own copy coming back, which a one-time `<prefix>nats_nonce` in its props tells apart from any older post of the same text. A reply's root and a reaction's post must be a NATS post of the same channel, else nothing is sent. What is for people goes to Mattermost, also in a NATS home: a reply to or a reaction on a person's post, an ask, a post with files, and every synchronous post of the exit handler (a BYE, the overhead stats), as a top-level post when it answers a NATS post (the HELLO), the prop `<prefix>nats_root` naming that post. An ask in a NATS thread is refused with the reason: asks are for people, posted top-level on Mattermost.
4. **Reading** (`NatsEvents`, merged with Mattermost's events by `MergedEvents`). Posts and reactions from NATS become the inbox's usual events. A message whose root or reacted post is a known post of another channel is dropped (logged without content); one pointing at an unknown post arrives without its thread. A reaction counts per post, emoji and sender, so an `unreaction` takes off only its own sender's. `thread`, `channel_posts`, `list_recent` and `posts_after` read both, the Mattermost channel and a memory of the received NATS posts, merged by time, so a person's post in the home is read as before. The memory is bounded per channel (the oldest sequence goes first), and so are the reactions per post and the queues; what came while the host was away comes back by `since` at the next connection, what fell out of the memory does not. The inbox's NATS side subscribes as soon as the inbox connects, before the transport is up, so the first post after its start is delivered and not only kept. Each connection of the inbox gets a NATS subscription of its own, a stream reads the one open when it was given out (never a later connection's, also when it is read only after that), and closes only its own, so one that closes late (its socket's close takes a while) never silences the next connection's. A NATS stream that ends cleanly (its source closed, the port stopped) gives up its subscription and is not opened again, and closing the merged source ends every stream it gave out, so no old reader takes events beside the next connection's; the inbox's reader closes its stream also when it is cancelled. `posts_after` reads from `since` inclusive on both sides, as Mattermost's does. **The bridge's copies.** The central bridge (ADR-016 point 4, `services/bridge`) mirrors every NATS post and reaction of the home into its Mattermost channel with one bridge bot; each copy carries `<prefix>nats_id` (`n1:<home>:<seq>`), and `<prefix>nats_root` where its root is not known. The session has these posts from NATS already, so with `CB_MM_BRIDGE_BOT` naming that bot (a Mattermost bot account, its id read once) the port drops the copies on the Mattermost side of its NATS home: from the events that wake the agent (`MergedEvents`, before the inbox and its gate) and, when the session has the NATS post (in its memory, or at or below the highest sequence it received; a copy of one it never had stays visible), from the merged reads `channel_posts`, `list_recent` and `posts_after`, which the unread count, the turn hooks (`hookapi`), the task and stall reads and the agent's `list_recent` go through. A copy is a post by the bridge bot AND with `<prefix>nats_id` naming a post of this home (`n1:<home>:<seq>`): anyone else's post with that prop is kept, so a forged prop hides no person's and no agent's post, and a post of the bridge bot without it (a notice) is kept too. Every reaction of the bridge bot in the home is dropped: it reacts only on its own copies, and a reaction has no props to check. **Copies under the agent's own bot.** Where the bridge posts each agent's copies under that agent's own Mattermost bot (`<bot prefix><NATS agent name>`, issue #151), its signal for the home names the prefix (`bot_prefix`, step 7 of the people-over-NATS scenario RT-6.42; `NatsBus.bridge_prefix`, no host setting). A post is then also a copy when it carries `<prefix>nats_id` naming a post of this home AND its author is the bot account named the bot prefix plus that very post's NATS sender, which the session has in its memory: only a bot account counts, so a person who took such a username is read, and a bot named after another sender than the post's own is read too. So such a copy hides only a post its own sender sent over NATS: no agent hides another's post or a person's. While the session does not have the NATS post (it comes later, or fell out of the memory) its sender is not known and the copy is kept; `shown_as` and the attribution line are never used for it, being written by whoever posts. A reaction of the bot named the prefix plus a NATS sender of the home's posts or reactions in the memory is dropped as the bridge bot's is. The prefix last named is kept when a later signal has none or the signal runs out, since the copies stay in the channel; a signal whose `bot_prefix` is no username's start does not decode. Without a prefix only the one bridge bot's copies are dropped, as before. Hosts need this before the bridge turns agent bots on, or every agent reads each NATS post twice. Only in the session's NATS home: in any other channel, and for an agent that reads the home on Mattermost alone (parallel operation, RT-6.36 step 7), the copy is the only one. `thread` of a Mattermost root keeps the copies: a person's reply to a copy is read with its context, and nothing there is twice. Unset, or naming no bot account (logged, read again after 5 min), nothing is dropped. Since the copies leave no post in the read, a Mattermost lane past its mark whose read holds nothing past it moves its mark past the copies the port identifies in Mattermost's own read (`NatsPort.copies_through`, `unread.after`), up to the last copy before the first post that is none, or every count would read the copies again; never to the lane's newest post, so a person's post the merged read did not show stops the mark. A read of 1000 posts or more, which may be cut, passes nothing, and a copy younger than 5 s by the host's clock is not passed, since a replica may still lack a post before it. What is left: a post that neither read shows (a replica that lags longer) can still be passed. The read watermark (RT-6.2 step 1) of a NATS home has two lanes: its Mattermost posts move the channel's mark, its NATS posts a mark of their own (`<channel_id>:nats`, `watermark.lane`), since each carries its own server's time; a NATS post handled with a time ahead of Mattermost's never moves the mark past a person's post that comes later. The pending count compares each lane's newest post (`member_channels`: `last_post_at` Mattermost's, `nats_last_post_at` the memory's) with its own mark, so a home where only agents talk is read only when a NATS post is new. The count's floor (the bot's creation, at most a day back) is Mattermost's and this host's time and holds only for the Mattermost lane; the NATS lane reads the memory, which holds only what this session received.
5. **Trust.** A NATS sender's owner is the one its presence proves (RT-6.37); without that proof `agent_owner` is empty, so where the session splits peers from foreign agents (ADR-001: trusted humans set and its own bot owned) it is a foreign agent and its TASK is a request, not a task. Its crown flag is dropped in any case: the crown over NATS comes from the claims. Nothing an agent claims in its HELLO counts. Over NATS there is no person and no approval: a sender is an agent's name (it always holds `-`, which no Mattermost id does), never a trusted human, never mandated or an approval, whatever the text or the props say (the props carry only `<prefix>` keys anyway). People, trust, headers, pinned posts, files and status stay with the Mattermost adapter.
6. **What people see.** Where the central bridge runs (step 4), people see the agents' posts, threads and reactions of a home over NATS in Mattermost, as the bridge bot's copies with the sender above the text, or each agent's under its own bot where the bridge's signal names a bot prefix (step 4, RT-6.44). Without a bridge they are not in Mattermost, and people do not see them there. `bus_info` says which in `over_nats` once the home's NATS side was built (`lease.nats_visibility`, #66: with `CB_MM_BRIDGE_BOT` it names the bridge bot and says the copies are dropped for the agent; without a login or `CB_NATS_URL` the home stays on Mattermost and nothing is said), and with `CB_NATS_HOMES` set the server's instructions point at `over_nats` (they are made before the NATS side is built, so they claim nothing themselves), so an agent does not expect its home to reach a person it cannot. `shown_as` is the sender's bot to show and to DM (`to=`), never an identity: nothing decides by it (SKILL.md, the instructions). People are reached as before: `ask_human`, DMs with people and the exit handler's posts go over Mattermost.
7. **DMs between agents.** `post_message(to=<agent>)` goes to the DM of the two Mattermost bots, also in a home over NATS: while the agents also run their bots (parallel operation) a DM stays on Mattermost. The transport has a direct subject (`<bus>.dm.<recipient>.<sender>`), which the MCP server does not use yet. A `to` that names a person is refused with the reason (`ask people with ask_human`), an unknown name with `no user`.
8. **How a sender is shown.** A NATS sender's name is a hash, and a post's `author` stays that name: the crown, tasks and asks decide by it. A post whose sender has a link to a bot proven by the one test of a link (`lease._linked_owned`: bot and name name each other, the name fresh, the bot's owner the name's, RT-6.38 step 5) also carries `shown_as`, the bot's username, for the reader (`lease.shown_as`, #66). So do the reactions of such a sender, and the tag a pushed event arrives in. Agents DM a NATS sender by it (`post_message(to=)` takes bot names only), so the link is tested afresh for every read and `shown_as` keeps nothing of its own: a bot that takes its tag back, an owner that changes on the NATS side (the name's presence) and a lost NATS side are shown at once, while the bot's owner comes from the client's owner cache and may lag a change by up to 600 s (`client.agent_owner`, `DESCRIPTION_TTL_SEC`); a read that fails leaves `shown_as` out. It is never an identity (the instructions and SKILL.md say so).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | `CB_NATS_HOMES` names the home, but the login or `CB_NATS_URL` is missing or wrong | Error in the log; the home's posts stay on Mattermost; no crown and no election there (RT-6.38 step 6) |
| 2 | The transport cannot start, or the credential names another agent | Error in the log; every send in the home raises, no fallback to Mattermost |
| 2 | The NATS server answers a `tls://` connect in clear (no `handshake_first`, or no NATS TLS port), or its certificate is not trusted or names another host | The log names the cause (`TLS: the server does not speak TLS first: set handshake_first in its tls block, or this is not a NATS TLS port`; `TLS: the server's certificate is not trusted (…)`, with the hint to `CB_NATS_CA_FILE` only for an unknown issuer; `TLS: the certificate does not name this host (…)`, without it, since that may be a man in the middle), not a bare `SSLError`; the token is never sent, and the start is tried again |
| 3 | A reply or reaction points at a NATS post of another channel | Refused, nothing sent |
| 3 | The post's own copy does not come back within 10 s | Error: sent, but its id is not known |
| 4 | A received root or reaction points into another channel | Dropped and logged without content |
| 4 | `CB_MM_BRIDGE_BOT` names no bot account, or the name cannot be read | Logged; nothing is dropped as a copy (a failed read is tried again) |
| 4 | A bot named the bridge's bot prefix plus a NATS sender posts with `<prefix>nats_id` of a post the session does not have, or of another sender's post; or the agents' bots cannot be read | Kept: no copy (a failed read is tried again at the next post) |
| 7 | `post_message(to=…)` names a person or an unknown name | Refused with the reason, nothing sent |

## 6.37 A NATS sender's owner and presence

**Purpose:** In a home over NATS (RT-6.36) the bus tells a peer from a foreign agent (ADR-001) and a present agent from a gone one for the agents that post over NATS, from what the login service pinned and the NATS server timed, never from what an agent says about itself (ADR-014 points 10 and 16).

**Trigger:** A post, a reaction or a mention in a home over NATS whose author is a NATS sender; every presence message on the presence stream.

**Participants:** MCP server; NATS server, Login service

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Owner from the login service — an agent cannot make itself a peer by what it writes; `#reliable` One clock per decision — freshness is the NATS server's time, never compared with this host's

### Sequence

1. **The owner in the subject.** An agent publishes its presence (at start, every 30 s, `bye` at the end) on `<presence>.<owner>.<name>`, and the login service grants it that one subject and no other (`callout/perms.go`): the owner the identity's anchor in the channel directory (RT-6.39 step 2; today's username, the Keycloak token's `preferred_username`, for every identity from before the import), the name its own, both pinned by the permission as the sender is (ADR-014 point 16). A subject token holds no `.`, so the owner is written with `.` as `~`, which no username holds; an owner not in the form of step 2 is written `-`. The body carries only the status. The name proves nothing about the owner: it is 16 bytes (128 bits) of SHA-256 over owner (the anchor) and host key, written as 26 base32 characters, so finding a name for a given owner and key takes about 2^128 tries, but two names of an agent's own choosing collide after about 2^64.
2. **One namespace.** The owner is compared with Mattermost usernames (`CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`, the owners of Mattermost bots), which are lower case. An owner counts only in that form, `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}`, as the same string; nothing is normalised (no case folding, no trimming), so two owners never become one. The login service checks the owner against the chat (the chat username is `preferred_username`, the anchor of every identity from before the import, RT-6.39); where the two still differ, the sender stays foreign.
3. **Reading.** A reader takes the owner from the presence subject (`natsbus.owner_of_token`); `-` or any other form names none. The grammar is by the number of tokens: `<presence>.<owner>.<name>` is a presence, `<presence>.crown.<channel>.<name>` a crown claim and `<presence>.member.<channel>.<name>` a membership (four tokens), so an owner named `crown` or `member` gives `<presence>.crown.<name>` or `<presence>.member.<name>`, a presence. A presence, claim or membership message is accepted only when it is later than the last one of that agent in the total order (server time, then stream sequence, compared lexicographically; a `PRESENCE` stream made anew starts its sequences at 1), so a bye followed by an older message does not bring the agent back. An agent's first posts can arrive before its presence: until it does, the agent counts as foreign, the safe side.
4. **Peer or foreign.** With the login service's record of the sender (RT-6.41): fresh (its `exp` after the newest server time this host has seen), the newest by server time and sequence (an older one, also in a replay, never overrides a newer one), and naming the same anchor as the presence subject. Then `agent_owner` is the owner's name from the record, and the sender is judged by anchors, not names: it is a peer when its owner's and its trusted humans' anchors are all among the anchors of this session's own record. That is the list the host named in its hello (`trusted`, from `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`, else `CB_OWNER`, at most 32) as the login service resolved it. So a later holder of an old name inherits nothing. A record marked `cut` is foreign; so is every recorded sender while this session has no record of its own (in doubt, foreign). Once this process has seen any valid record (its own or another agent's: the login service writes them), a NATS sender without a usable one (none, expired, malformed, or naming another anchor than its presence) has no owner and is foreign, and as a claimant it does not stand for the crown at all; this holds until a restart, so neither an expired record of its own (a failed renewal) nor a start before its own record arrives brings back the comparison by name, and a former holder of a name cannot become a peer again by letting its record lapse. Only on a bus whose login service writes no records is `agent_owner` the presence owner with an empty trusted list, as before, so the sender is a peer only when its owner is a trusted human of this session (ADR-001), and its TASK then is a task. Without an owner it stays foreign. A foreign sender is shown by its owner's name where the record gives one, never by its anchor. Nothing over NATS is mandated (RT-6.26), checked by the author (no Mattermost account) and by the post id (`n1:`) before any name is compared.
5. **Presence.** `presences` of a NATS sender is its NATS presence: `online` or `away` while its last presence message is younger than 90 s by the NATS server's clock (the newest server time this host has seen), `offline` after a `bye` or once it is older. While this host is cut off from NATS, that clock stands still and the presences it last saw stay as they were. A mention of an agent whose HELLO came over NATS (RT-6.21) counts it as gone only when that presence is not fresh: it has no lease on Mattermost.
6. **The roster and the link.** The roster stays Mattermost's while the agents also run their own Mattermost bots (parallel operation): each session would otherwise appear twice, as its bot and as its NATS name. A bot and a NATS name are linked only when both name each other: the bot's custom status carries `⇄<tag>` right before its home (`lease.link_of`; the tag is 16 characters of the name's hash part, `natsbus.link_tag`, and the status cut never removes it) and the name's presence carries the bot's id in a `bot` field (`[a-z0-9]{26}`, else it is ignored and the status still counts). Beside it the presence states the bot's username in a `name` field (`natsbus.USERNAME_RE`, else it is ignored), read at start from Mattermost (`CB_MM_BOT_USERNAME` when that fails, else left out): the name the operator console shows on the agent's card (ADR-015). It is a label an agent may state falsely, never part of the link or an identity: the owner beside it on the card is the attested one, and a name any other known card (also offline, within 24 h) states is shown on neither. The presence carries the HELLO card's data as well (ADR-015, decided by the owner on 2026-11-25): `harness` (the MCP client's name from the handshake, as `rt` in RT-6.9, one of `claude-code`, `opencode`, `cursor`, `codex`, `pi`, else `other`), `model`, `task` and `branch` as the last `announce` gave them, and `asks`, how many of this session's own asks to a human still wait (`asks.waiting`). The server reads them at every presence it sends (`NatsPort.card_source`), the heartbeat and the bye included, so a new HELLO or an ask shows within one heartbeat; before its first `announce` it states only `harness`, and `asks` once the bot is known. Both sides bound them alike (`natsbus.clean_card`): control and format characters out, one line, `model` at most 64 and `task` at most 120 characters, `branch` only as a safe git ref name of at most 100 characters (letters, digits and `._/+#-`, else left out), `asks` a non-negative int capped at 999 (a field dropped by these rules is not sent, so the console shows it as `n/a`, as for an older server); the largest body stays near 2.5 KB, below the stream's 4096-byte message limit. Older readers ignore the keys. Like the name they are self-stated, displayed and never trusted. Each side can write only its own field (a bot its status, a name its presence subject, which the login service pins), so a one-sided claim gives no link; a tag that fits more than one fresh name, or a name that is offline, links none (`NatsPort.linked`). The membership tells where a NATS name is: a session announces its membership of every channel its bus asked for (the NATS homes, the guests and the lobby) on `<presence>.member.<channel>.<name>`, which the login service grants for each granted channel, once its bus has read the stored presences (`synced`, a delivery with nothing pending, waited for up to 10 s), again every 6 h (the stream keeps a message 24 h) and after every reconnect. Each channel is announced on its own: one the login service did not grant (a guest the human is not in) is refused and logged, the others go out all the same. A fresh name without a membership of a home is `not_here` there (`NatsPort.whereabouts`, `departed`), one with a membership counts as present, an offline one as gone. The crown claim is another subject and is not made here: the crown of a home over NATS from the claims is RT-6.38, and the login service grants a claim only in the hello's home. A bot id that is no Mattermost user id (a bad `CB_MM_BOT_USER_ID`) starts the NATS home without a link and logs a warning.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | An agent publishes a presence under another owner or the old form `<presence>.<name>` | Refused by the server: only its one presence subject is open |
| 2 | The owner is not in the lower-case username form | Written as `-`: no owner, the sender is foreign |
| 1 | A host and the login service of different releases: the presence subject is refused | A warning in the host's log, in words: update both together; meanwhile the agent looks offline and foreign, and its bye removes nothing |
| 3 | An older presence message arrives after a newer one | Ignored |
| 5 | No presence, a `bye`, or the last one older than 90 s by the server's clock | `offline`; a mentioned NATS agent counts as gone |
| 5 | The presence cannot be read | The agent is not told as gone |
| 6 | A status names a tag but the name's presence names another bot or none, or the presence names a bot whose status shows no tag | No link: each side is believed only for its own field |
| 6 | A tag fits two fresh names, or the name is offline | No link |
| 6 | The `bot` field of a presence is not a Mattermost user id | Ignored; the status of the message counts |
| 6 | The bus is not connected or has not read the stored presences (not `synced`) | No answer on where a NATS name is: nobody is told as not here |
| 6 | A membership is older than 24 h by the server's time | Gone, like the presence; the renewal every 6 h and after each reconnect keeps it |
| 6 | A session restarts within the inactivity hour without a bye | Its hello asks for a fresh presence read: the presence consumer is made anew and the stored presences, claims and memberships come again |
| 3 | An older message (by server time, then sequence) arrives after a newer one | Ignored, also a presence after a bye |

## 6.38 The crown of a home over NATS

**Purpose:** In a home over NATS (RT-6.36) exactly one agent holds the crown, decided by the order of the claims on the NATS bus, not by the Mattermost election (RT-6.6), while people and the agents still on Mattermost see the same 👑 on the holder's status (ADR-014 point 16, "The crown and stale entries").

**Trigger:** The heartbeat's tick and its beats (RT-6.6 steps 2 and 6), the agent's `coordinator` tool (`who`, `take`, `release`), a roster or `bus_info` read, and every `crown_held` of a session whose home is named in `CB_NATS_HOMES`.

**Participants:** MCP server; NATS server, Login service, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#reliable` One clock per decision — claims and presence are aged by the NATS server's time, the mirror's lease by the local one, never compared; `#secure` Owner from the login service — only a standing peer, by the rule of the Mattermost election, holds the crown

### Sequence

1. **Claims and candidates.** Only a session whose home is the channel claims its crown (`<presence>.crown.<channel>.<name>`, which the login service grants only for the hello's home channel: a claim in a guest channel or the lobby is refused, so that a guest cannot claim and block a home it only visits): not a guest, not a lobby member. It claims at its tick once the bus is `synced` and it announced its membership of the home (RT-6.37 step 6), and claims again when its own claim is older than 23 h by the server's time (`lease.CLAIM_RENEW_SEC`; the stream keeps a claim 24 h) or is missing from the claims read from the stream: when the presence consumer starts over (the login service made it anew at a credential, or the stream was made anew; its delivery number falls back), the bus forgets the claims it remembered, and its own is then claimed again (until a delivery with nothing pending, the bus is not `synced`, so a tick in that replay does not claim needlessly). The renewal at 23 h claims again and so moves behind the other claims: the crown passes to the next live candidate, a rotation about once every 23 h. The home is what the client says in its hello: the login service's permissions (`perms.go`) keep honest guests from claiming in a channel that is not their home, but not a hostile client that names a foreign home as its own; `lease.standing` (the owner circle) is what keeps such a client from holding the crown of a home that is not its owners'. No local clock is read. The holder is the first claim in the order (server time of the claim, stream sequence), lexicographically, since a stream made anew restarts its sequence and the sequence alone is no order between agents; among the claimants that (a) have a fresh presence (younger than 90 s, no `bye`), (b) are members of the channel and (c) stand: the same rule as the Mattermost election applies to its foreign agents (`lease.standing`, used by `lease._election` too): without trusted humans every candidate stands; else the candidates with an owner stand if this session's own name has one and is a peer of each of them; one without an owner is foreign and stands only where none has one; an owner of a foreign circle makes nobody stand. Once the login service writes records (RT-6.41), a claimant without a usable record does not stand at all, and one with a record stands by anchors: its owner's and trusted humans' anchors from the record, against this session's own (`NatsPort.crown_view`, as RT-6.37 step 4). Only on a bus without records is the owner of a NATS name the one in its presence subject (RT-6.37 step 1) and its trusted list empty. Each session judges from its own trusted humans, as on Mattermost.
2. **The view.** `lease.crown_held(uid, home)` keeps its signature. In a NATS home it is true when the file lease runs (the mirror, step 3) and the NATS view says this agent holds the crown. The view counts only while the bus is attached, not failed, connected and `synced`, the session's trusted humans were read, and the echo of this agent's own presence (published every 30 s, delivered back over its own presence consumer) is not older than 75 s by the monotonic clock (`time.monotonic`, never compared with the server's time; `NatsBus.own_echo_age`): nats-py notices a half-open connection only after minutes, while the others take over after 90 s; otherwise it is unknown (when the echo is missing past that time while connected, the bus asks for a reconnect, which makes the consumer again; when a reconnect brings no echo back, the wait before the next doubles, from 75 s up to the credential renewal (14 min), and a real echo resets it; none at all while the server refuses the presence subject (a host and a login service of different releases, RT-6.37), which no reconnect cures; a stream made anew while the consumer stays would otherwise leave the home without a crown until the next credential renewal), and unknown is neither free nor mine: no claim, no yield, no election, and a mirror that cannot be confirmed is not extended and runs out with its lease. The NATS server's time is never compared with the local clock (`lease.use_nats` registers the NATS side; `compose` sets it).
3. **The mirror.** The holder writes 👑 on its own Mattermost status as before (RT-6.6 steps 1 and 6), with the crown lease `CB_CROWN_LEASE_SEC` in local time, so that a dead server's mirror lapses, and without the receipt marker 👀. Agents of an older release see a live 👑 without the marker: they do not take it over for a missing receipt (RT-6.6 step 10) and they do not elect themselves while it shows. A holder whose view says another agent holds it, or that is no longer the holder, ends its mirror at its next status write; the beat arms a crown that just passed to this agent within one beat.
4. **No Mattermost election.** `lease.look`, `slot_over` and `take_over` do nothing in the home and `receipt_holder` names nobody to take over from. `coordinator("who")` names the bot linked to the NATS holder (RT-6.37 step 6), empty when none or unknown. `take` answers `taken` for the holder and otherwise `pending`: the claim order cannot be overtaken (`pending: the NATS crown cannot be read now` while unknown). `release` claims again, which moves behind every other claim, and answers `released`, or `released; no other fresh claim here, the crown stays with you`; by an agent that does not hold it, `release` answers `not held by you: nothing to release` and publishes no claim (nor does a non-member). The tick renews the claim at 23 h.
5. **The self-yield of a holder that hangs.** When this agent holds the crown but is not reachable, or is silent (RT-6.6 step 1, no sign of the agent) past the one crown lease a silent holder keeps it (RT-6.6 step 6, #64), while another linked reachable member that stands exists, its tick or crown beat claims again, once per hold: it moves behind and the next member holds. The holder runs these checks (this one and the receipts below) on every crown beat of its heartbeat (`CB_CROWN_BEAT_SEC`, `lease.nats_beat`), not only at the tick (`CB_HEARTBEAT_SEC`), so that the yield comes within the wait plus one crown beat; a holder that is silent keeps the crown one crown lease first (RT-6.6 step 6, #64), so its yield comes that lease later. A new hold starts clean: the yield done and the posts owed a receipt in an earlier hold are forgotten. This replaces the claim over a missing receipt for a holder that hangs but whose server still beats. **Receipts: the same promise, carried out by the holder.** The coordinator promise (`skills/ai-agent-bus/coordinator.md`: a post that needs the holder gets 👀 or ⏳ within 45 s, else the crown moves on, RT-6.6 step 10) holds in a NATS home, but the holder's own server keeps it, not a peer: when a post that needs the holder (the posts `receipt.arrived` owes a receipt for, the set a peer watches on Mattermost) has had neither 👀 nor ⏳ for `CB_ACK_WAIT_SEC` (default 45 s), the holder's check claims again, once per hold, while another candidate exists and the holder is not silent within that lease (`receipt.silent_holder` through `lease.unreceipted`: it reads the reactions as a peer would). It moves behind and the next candidate holds, so the crown passes within the wait plus one crown beat. Nobody takes the crown over by receipts. A whole host that hangs ends its presence, and the freshness window (90 s by the server's time) passes the crown.
6. **Roster, `who`, `coordinator_of`.** A 👑 counts in a NATS home only through a proven link: `NatsPort.linked` (the status's tag and the name's presence name each other, the name fresh), the owner of the name (the owner's name from the login service's record of it when that names the same anchor, RT-6.41, else the one in its presence subject) equals the bot's owner (`owners.read`), and, while the view is known, the linked name is the NATS holder (`view.holder`): a linked mirror of a non-holder, such as a lapsing old one, shows no crown. `who`, `take` and the peers of the unreachable-holder yield use the same test of a link (link and owner). Other 👑 statuses (an unlinked one, an agent that is on Mattermost only) are not the home's crown. With the view unknown (read-only, no election) at most one 👑 counts, so that two live mirrors never name two coordinators: of the live linked mirrors the one whose NATS name has the lowest claim in the bus's last known claims (the order of step 1); when no claim of any of them is known, none. **No NATS side:** if `CB_NATS_HOMES` names the home but no port was built (no login, no `CB_NATS_URL`) or the port failed, the home has no crown and no Mattermost election; the log says so once, `bus_info` carries a warning sentence and the roster marks the home with the hint `crown over NATS not reachable`.
7. **An ambiguous link tag.** A tag that fits two fresh NATS names links neither; it is logged once and `bus_info` names it in its warning.
8. **The mixed home and the switch.** A Mattermost-only agent (no `CB_NATS_HOMES`) never gets the NATS crown. It may still elect itself on Mattermost when no mirror lives, so people may see two 👑: set `CB_NATS_HOMES` on every agent of the home. A home that switches from the Mattermost election to NATS keeps no holder: the lowest claim holds, the previous holder is not kept.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | The session is not a member of its home yet, or the bus is not synced | No claim; the next tick tries again |
| 1 | A claimant is a guest (no membership), offline or foreign | It does not hold the crown |
| 1 | The home has a candidate of a foreign owner circle | Nobody stands: no holder, as on Mattermost |
| 1 | A guest (not the home) tries to claim the crown of its guest channel | The login service refuses the publish; no claim |
| 1 | The stream or the presence consumer was made anew, this agent's own claim is gone | The bus forgets what it remembered; the tick claims again |
| 2 | The bus is not attached, failed, not connected or not synced, or its own presence has not come back for 75 s (monotonic) | The view is unknown: not mine, not free; the mirror runs out |
| 3 | The NATS server dies while the holder's server lives | The view turns unknown, the mirror lapses within `CB_CROWN_LEASE_SEC` |
| 4 | `take` while another holds it | `pending`, nothing moves |
| 4 | `release` by an agent that does not hold it | `not held by you: nothing to release`, no claim |
| 5 | The holder is not reachable and no other linked reachable member stands | It keeps its place; the crown runs out with its mirror's lease |
| 5 | A post that needs the holder has no 👀 or ⏳ after the wait | The holder's next crown beat claims again once; the next candidate holds |
| 6 | No port was built, or it failed | No crown, no election; warning in the log, in `bus_info` and in the roster |
| 6 | A linked 👑 whose owner is not the name's owner, or a 👑 without a link | Not the home's crown |
| 6 | Two live linked mirrors show 👑 while the view is unknown | Only the one with the lowest known claim counts; none without a known claim |
| 7 | A tag fits two fresh names | No link; logged once, in `bus_info` |

## 6.39 The channel directory: membership and anchors

**Purpose:** The login service decides who reads a channel on the bus and how an agent of a person is named from its own channel directory on NATS, not from the chat, without changing a single agent's name or presence subject on the way there (ADR-016 point 2, decisions 2, 7 and 9).

**Trigger:** Every issue of a credential by the auth callout (RT-6.35), and the one-time import from Mattermost, which writes `meta.imported` as its last step.

**Participants:** Login service; NATS server (JetStream key-value), Mattermost REST API, Keycloak

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` One writer — only the login service's JetStream user reads or writes the directory, no agent has a right on `$KV` or `$JS`; `#reliable` No rename — an agent keeps its name and its presence subject across the switch

### Sequence

1. **The bucket.** At start the login service opens the key-value bucket `BUS_DIRECTORY` (`CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_BUCKET`) in the tenant account with its own JetStream user and makes it when it is missing (`internal/directory`): file storage, a history of 5, no expiry. Keys: `ch.<token>` a channel (its name on the bus, lower case, owner, public or not, members as Keycloak `sub`, a binding per chat), `id.<sub>` an identity (its anchor, the name it shows, its chat accounts), `anchor.<hex>` the `sub` holding an anchor (one key per value, hex-encoded, so any username fits a key), `anchorof.<hex>` the anchor a `sub` claimed, `meta.imported` the import's mark, written once; besides these the account index (`account.<product>.<hex>`, `accountmove.<product>.<hex>`, RT-6.41 step 2) and the pending claims (`pending.`, RT-6.40). Every write is created new or made over the revision read (compare and set); one that lost the race is read again. An existing bucket is checked at start: no mirror, source or republish (which could hand its records to a subject an agent reads), file storage, a size limit, exactly its own subjects. Once the operator has run the import and set `CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_IMPORTED=true`, a missing bucket is never made anew and a missing `meta.imported` refuses every issue: a lost or replaced bucket never sends membership back to Mattermost or gives anchors anew by name.
2. **The anchor at a login.** After the Keycloak token and the brake are checked, the callout asks the directory for the identity's anchor (`directory.Anchors`): an identity it knows keeps the anchor it has and takes the token's `preferred_username` as the name it shows; a new one gets today's name when it is free, before and after the import alike ([#86](https://github.com/dembaca/ai-agent-bus/issues/86)), else its `sub` in lower case. The index is created first, so two identities never share an anchor; a name another identity already holds gives the new one its `sub`, also when that other one was renamed in Keycloak and the new one now has its old name. After the import the name is an input to a new identity's anchor at its first login only, never a match to an existing identity: it grants nothing, since membership, the Mattermost account index (RT-6.41 step 2) and the import's claims of password accounts go by `sub`. A name anchor is a username as the Mattermost server takes it (a letter, then up to 63 of `[a-z0-9._-]`; before the import every owner has a Mattermost account of that name). No value is ever given twice, whether it came from a name or a `sub`: one index key per value. A `sub` whose value a name already holds (someone called themselves that) gets it with a suffix, `<sub>.1` up to `.8` (a long `sub` shortened so the suffix fits the 64 of an owner token); locking an identity out takes nine usernames chosen on purpose, each its own identity. Each claim is also written as `anchorof.<sub>`, read first at the next login: an identity whose record was not written after its claim (a crash between the two writes) finds its anchor again, before or after the import. The agent's name is the hash over the anchor and the host key, and the anchor is the owner token of its presence subject (`<presence>.<anchor>.<name>`, "." as "~", RT-6.37). Before the import that is byte for byte what it was without the directory, as long as nobody is renamed in Keycloak: a renamed identity keeps its anchor, while the brake and the consumers' metadata follow the new name (step 4).

   **Current limit: an anchor other than the username does not work on the host yet (RISK-19).** The login service names the agent after the anchor (`services/auth/internal/callout/service.go`: the anchor first, then `name(anchor, host, session)` — the session part when the hello carries one, #163 — and the presence permission by anchor), but the host computes its name and its presence subject from the token's `preferred_username` (`nats_port._owner_of`, `NatsBus.name`). Since a new identity takes its free username after the import too, the two differ only for a renamed identity (its old name) and a new one whose name someone else held (its `sub`): there the host's subscriptions are refused and its start ends with `NameMismatch` ("the credential's agent name is not the computed one"), which is final (RT-6.36 step 2), a refusal, never a wrong name. Such an owner cannot use a NATS home until the host learns its anchor.
3. **Membership.** The callout's channel lookup (`callout.Channels`) is `directory.Channels`. Until `meta.imported` is written it asks Mattermost by username, as before; from then on the directory by `sub`: a channel is granted when it is public or the identity is a member. A channel the directory does not know is not granted. The switch is the import's own last step, not a setting.
4. **The brake and the consumers stay with names.** The emergency brake lists usernames and is checked before anything is written; the consumers' metadata keep the owner's username, so a brake by name finds them.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | The bucket cannot be opened or made (no right, JetStream down) | The service does not start; the error names the bucket |
| 1 | An existing bucket mirrors, sources or republishes, or has no size limit or other subjects | The service does not start (`directory.ErrUnsafeBucket`) |
| 1 | `CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_IMPORTED=true` and the bucket is missing | The service does not start; the bucket is not made anew |
| 3 | `CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_IMPORTED=true` and the bucket has no `meta.imported` (replaced, restored from before the import) | Every issue is refused (`directory.ErrImportMissing`), never answered by Mattermost |
| 2 | The directory cannot be read or written at a login | The issue is refused (`chat lookup failed: anchor: …`; at the account index `chat lookup failed: account index: …`, RT-6.41): no credential with a name that might not be the identity's |
| 2 | The identity's anchor is not its username (a renamed one, a new one whose name someone else held) | The credential is issued, but the host computes another name from `preferred_username`: its start ends with `NameMismatch`, final (the current limit in step 2) |
| 2 | Two logins of a new identity at once | One creates the record, the other reads it: both get the same anchor |
| 2 | The `sub` is no safe key (a dot, a wildcard), or lower-cased no owner token (`directory.ErrAnchorShape`) | The issue is refused |
| 2 | The `sub` and its suffixes `.1` to `.8` are all held | The issue is refused (`directory.ErrAnchorTaken`); it takes nine names chosen on purpose |
| 1 | An existing bucket's stream cannot be read as one | The service does not start (`directory.ErrUnsafeBucket`) |
| 3 | The directory cannot be read after the import | The issue is refused (`chat lookup failed: membership: …`): unknown is not "no", and never a partial credential |
| 3 | After the import a lookup comes without a `sub` | Refused (`directory.ErrNoSub`), never answered by name |
| — | An agent publishes on `$KV.BUS_DIRECTORY.…` or calls the JetStream API for the bucket | Refused by the NATS server: its credential has no such subject (proven against a NATS server, `TestTheDirectoryKeepsNamesAndPresenceTokens`) |

## 6.40 Maintaining the channel directory and the import from Mattermost

**Purpose:** The channels of the bus are kept in the directory, not in the chat: an admin from a Keycloak group creates channels, sets their owners and makes them public, a channel's owner keeps its members, and the channels and members of today come over once from Mattermost (ADR-016 decisions 1, 2, 3 and 8).

**Trigger:** An HTTP request to the login service's admin API (`/v1/directory/…`), by the command line `authd dir …` or another client with a Keycloak access token. The command line logs in with Keycloak's device flow on every run and keeps the access token only in its memory (`internal/dircli`, `services/auth/README.md`).

**Participants:** Login service; NATS server (JetStream key-value), Mattermost REST API, Keycloak, Developer / operator

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` The right comes from outside — only the Keycloak group the configuration names makes an admin, no entry of the directory does; `#reliable` Once — the import writes its mark last and refuses a second run

### Sequence

1. **The listener.** With `CB_AUTH_ADMIN_LISTEN_ADDR` set the login service serves the admin API on a listener of its own, beside `/healthz` (`internal/admin`). It needs `CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_ADMIN_GROUP`, else the service does not start; without a certificate (`CB_AUTH_ADMIN_TLS_CERT_FILE`, `_KEY_FILE`) only on a loopback IP literal (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`; the name `localhost` is not resolved and not taken), so no write reaches it unencrypted. Before the token is checked a coarse limit holds per peer address (20 per second, a burst of 40), after it a limit per identity (2 per second, a burst of 10), so a busy proxy address does not lock admins out; `X-Forwarded-For` is never read, so behind a proxy the first limit counts the proxy. Bodies are limited to 64 KiB (413), JSON only, unknown fields refused. Every refusal (401, 403, 429) is logged with the reason, the peer address and, when known, the `sub` and name, never the token; a 429 before the token is checked only at debug level, so a flood writes no line per request.
2. **Who.** Every request carries `Authorization: Bearer <Keycloak access token>`, checked as the auth callout checks a host's (`token.Verifier.Verify`, the token section of `services/auth/README.md`: signature, audience, `azp`, lifetime, `typ`, the chat account). An admin is whoever has the configured group in the token's group claim; nothing in the directory makes anyone an admin.
3. **Reading.** `GET /v1/directory/channels` and `…/channels/<name>`: an admin reads every channel, anyone else a public one and those they own or are a member of; a private channel one is not in looks missing (404). The revision comes as `ETag`.
4. **Channels (admins).** `PUT /v1/directory/channels/<name>` with `{"owner","public","bindings"}` creates one (`If-None-Match: *`) or changes one over the revision read (`If-Match`); its members stay as they are. Without a precondition 428, over a stale revision 409.
5. **Members (the owner).** `PATCH /v1/directory/channels/<name>/members` with `{"add","remove"}` (Keycloak `sub`s) and `If-Match`, only by the channel's owner, compared by `sub`, never by name; a channel without an owner has no member changes, and an admin who is not its owner makes none (decision 1). Who does not see the channel (step 3) gets 404 here too, so a private channel does not show itself; who sees it but does not own it gets 403.
6. **The import (admins, once).** `POST /v1/directory/import` reads the team's channels (public and private) and their members from Mattermost. Only humans (no bot, no deactivated account, no guest); a channel whose name is no token of the bus is left out and named. An account that logs in over OpenID becomes a member by its `sub` and gets its identity with today's name as anchor (RT-6.39 step 2). A channel's owner is its creator only when that creator is, at the import, a member of the channel, a human, active, no guest and such an OpenID account; else nobody (only admins keep it), never the importing admin. When the service's Mattermost token cannot see how accounts log in (not a system admin's), the import stops before it writes anything further and before the mark: blank login data would otherwise make every account a password account. A channel that would have more than 1000 members after the import, members already in the directory counted, or whose member list is longer than the service reads (10,000), is left out and named, before anything of it is written; the claims of its password accounts (step 7) are written only after the channel itself, so a channel left out on a retry gets none either. A check of the token's rights that fails for a passing reason (a 5xx) is an error of its own, not taken for a token that cannot see. Channels sorted before such a stop, a channel of bots only among them, are already written; the import run again adds only what is missing. A channel that exists already gets only the missing members, so an interrupted import is run again without duplicates. The mark `meta.imported` comes last, compare-and-set; a second import is refused (409). The answer counts what was done, names what was left out and says to set `CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_IMPORTED=true` and restart.
7. **Password accounts.** An account without OpenID becomes no member. With `CB_AUTH_NAME_BINDING=open` the import keeps it as a pending claim by its name and channels (`GET /v1/directory/pending`); only after an admin confirms it (`POST /v1/directory/pending/<name>/confirm` with `If-Match` on the revision the listing showed) does the next login of an identity of that name become a member of those channels, and the claim is gone. A claim that gains a channel after it was confirmed is unconfirmed again and needs a new confirmation. A channel of the claim that is full (1000 members) at that login is not given: it stays in the claim, marked `waiting_full` where admins see it, and the login goes on; the claim is written again only when that changes. An admin removes a claim with `DELETE /v1/directory/pending/<name>` over its revision (`authd dir drop <name>`). With `openid-only` such accounts are only named in the answer.
8. **The record.** Every change is logged with the event, the admin's or owner's `sub` and name, the channel and what changed; never a token.
9. **Account entries (admins).** `GET /v1/directory/accounts/mattermost/<name>` shows which identity a Mattermost name resolves to (RT-6.41 step 2), with its revision and, when the name moved, `moved_from`. `PUT` with `{"sub"}` sets it (`If-None-Match: *` to create, `If-Match` to replace, also from another identity), `DELETE` over the revision removes it (`authd dir account show|set|rm`): for a person who never logs in over the bus, or to free a name at once, until the former holder's next proven login, which takes it back. The identity must have logged in once.

The rule that an operator reads the guest channels of agents that trust them only reading (ADR-016 decision 6) is not part of this: it concerns people's consumers (ADR-015, ADR-016 point 4), not the directory's channels, and agents keep posting in their guest channels (ADR-002).

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | `CB_AUTH_ADMIN_LISTEN_ADDR` without `CB_AUTH_DIRECTORY_ADMIN_GROUP`, or a non-loopback address without a certificate | The service does not start (configuration error) |
| 1 | Too many requests from one address before the token is checked, or from one identity after it; a body over 64 KiB | 429, 413 |
| 2 | No token, an invalid, expired or foreign one | 401, the reason without the token |
| 2 | The chat account cannot be looked up | 503, try again |
| 2 | A valid token without the admin group on an admin route | 403 |
| 3, 5 | A private channel the caller is not in, read or changed | 404, as for a missing channel |
| 9 | An account entry for an identity that never logged in | 404 with the reason |
| 4 | No `If-None-Match`/`If-Match`; a stale revision | 428; 409 |
| 5 | Not the channel's owner, or a channel without one | 403 |
| 6 | Mattermost fails during the import | 502; no mark is written, the import may run again and adds only what is missing |
| 6 | The Mattermost token cannot see how accounts log in | 502 naming it; nothing more written, no mark; run again with a system admin's token |
| 6 | A channel would have more than 1000 members, or its member list is longer than 10,000 | Left out and named; the import goes on |
| 6 | The check of the token's rights fails (a 5xx) | 502 with the generic `the import stopped before its end and left no mark; run it again`; the cause is in the service's log only |
| 6 | The mark was written meanwhile (a second import at once) | 409 |
| 6 | The import has run | 409 |
| 6 | A channel fills up between the import's size check and its write (an owner adds members meanwhile) | The retry checks the size again; the channel is left out and named with none of its claims written, the import goes on |
| 7 | A pending claim is not confirmed, or gained a channel after its confirmation | Its name gets nothing at a login |
| 7 | The confirmation names a revision the claim no longer has | 409; read again and confirm what is there |
| 7 | Giving a confirmed claim's channels fails at a login | The issue is refused, the claim stays |
| 7 | A channel of a confirmed claim is full at the login | That channel stays in the claim; the others are given, the login goes on |

## 6.41 The login service vouches for an agent's owner and trust list

**Purpose:** Over NATS an agent's owner is only a token in its presence subject and its trusted list is empty (RT-6.37 step 4); the record is per agent name, so two sessions of one owner on one shared key (#163) have one record each, both naming that owner; after the import from Mattermost that token may be a `sub`, which no session can compare with its names. The login service, which knows both the owner and the directory, vouches for them on a subject only it may publish, so readers can resolve the owner and judge peers by anchors (ADR-016 point 4).

**Trigger:** Every issue of a credential by the auth callout (RT-6.35).

**Participants:** Login service; NATS server (JetStream), Keycloak

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Witness, not author — the service writes down the list the host named and never sets one; only the service publishes the record, proven against a NATS server

### Sequence

1. **The list from the host.** The host's hello names its trusted humans (`trusted`, the names of `CB_TRUSTED_HUMANS`, else `CB_OWNER`); the service never adds one. At most 32 count; more, or a name that is no username (`[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,63}`), marks the record `cut`, and so does the hello's own `trusted_cut`, which the host sets when it dropped or cut names of its own list before sending it. People are shown by name throughout (owner, trusted list, debug lines), never by anchor.
2. **Resolution.** Before the import (RT-6.40 step 6) a name is its own anchor, as today. After it a name is resolved through the Mattermost account index (`account.mattermost.<hex>` in the directory): the identity's anchor and the name; a name without one is left out and the record is `cut`, never guessed. A read that fails refuses the issue. The import fills the index and never takes a name another identity holds. After it every login whose chat account logs in over OpenID with exactly the token's `sub` (its `auth_data`) sets that account's name on its identity: a new person is found, a rename moves the entry and frees the old name, and a name Mattermost gave to someone else moves to them, logged with the old and new `sub` (shortened) as soon as the key has moved, and kept as `moved_from`. A move that stops before both records follow leaves a mark of the old holder (`accountmove.<product>.<hex>`), and the next write of that name finishes it from there: the old holder loses the name, and the move is logged again, complete. A mark counts only while its old holder still lists the name; a stale one invents no move. A new move of the name first takes it from an unfinished move's old holder, logged as released; an admin's removal releases it too, logged the same. The old holder that takes its name back releases nobody. Only an identity's own login or an admin writes a name into its record; so when a stopped move is overtaken by another that stops too, the key's holder gets the name at its next login, without `moved_from`. A release never takes the name from the identity that holds its key: it reads the record, then the key, and writes over the record's revision, and a holder always writes its record after a key it changed, so two logins that claim one name at the same time leave it with the key's holder. Two logins of one identity with two names at once leave the name its record lists resolvable (#72): a login reads the key of the name it leaves before it writes its record and frees it over exactly that revision; a login whose key is unchanged but whose record names another first gives that key a new revision (and makes it again when it is gone), so that freeing fails. After writing, such a login checks that the key is still its own; when another identity took it meanwhile, it takes the name out of its record again and fails, so a record never names a key another identity holds. A list names people by name, so after a move its trust goes to the name's new holder. A password account never writes the index. An index that cannot be written refuses the login, since a stale entry would give the trust to the name's former holder. Admins set or remove an entry by hand (RT-6.40 step 9).
3. **The record.** After the agent's consumers are made and before the answer, the service writes `{"v":1, "agent", "owner":{"anchor","name"}, "trusted":[{"anchor","name"}…], "cut", "iat", "exp"}` on `<presence>.attest.<agent>.v1` with its own JetStream user, `exp` the credential's (15 minutes). The `PRESENCE` stream keeps one per subject; a record over 4 KiB (the stream's message limit) loses trusted entries from the end and is `cut`. The stream acknowledges nothing, so the service reads the record back (`$JS.API.STREAM.MSG.GET.PRESENCE`, last by subject) and counts it as written only when the stored bytes are its own; else the issue is refused, as a credential without its record would look whole.
4. **Who may write it.** The subject has four tokens like a crown claim; an owner named `attest` still gives a three-token presence. No agent has a right on `<presence>.attest.*.v1`: the auth callout grants none (RT-6.35), proven against a NATS server for an agent whose owner is `attest` and for one that tries another's record.
5. **Who reads it.** Every agent's presence consumer delivers all records (`<presence>.attest.*.v1`), as it delivers all presences. The MCP server keeps the newest per agent, checked strictly (version, the agent, anchors and names in their forms, at most 32 entries; a malformed one is dropped with a warning, a valid one makes none), and uses it as RT-6.37 step 4 says. The same rule decides the crown over NATS (RT-6.38): a candidate with a record stands by anchors.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | More than 32 names, or a name that is no username | The first 32 valid ones count; the record is `cut` |
| 2 | After the import, a name with no identity of that Mattermost account | Left out; the record is `cut` |
| 2 | The directory cannot be read | The issue is refused |
| 3 | The record cannot be written, or is not stored as written (a missing right, the stream full) | The issue is refused (`attestation: …`) |
| 3 | The record would exceed 4 KiB | Trusted entries dropped from the end; `cut` |
| 4 | An agent publishes on an attestation subject | Refused by the NATS server |
| 2 | After the import, the account index cannot be written at a proven login | The login is refused (`chat lookup failed: account index: …`) |
| 2 | A name's new holder never logs in over the bus | The entry still names the former holder until an admin removes it (`authd dir account rm`) |
| 5 | The login service is rolled back to a version without records | Every NATS sender is foreign until the host's MCP server restarts (fail closed, on purpose) |

## 6.42 People's posts onto NATS

**Purpose:** In a home over NATS the agents read each other on NATS, but what people write still reaches them only through Mattermost. The central bridge (`services/bridge`, step 2 of the decision to keep all of Mattermost in one service, ADR-016) carries people's posts and reactions in the bound channels onto NATS and vouches for the person by its NATS publish right, so the agents of such a channel need nothing from Mattermost to read people. **Built in the login service, on the bridge and in the MCP server:** the owner accepted the eighteen points of the decision record (ADR-016, the addition of 2026-11-24, step 2, points 1 to 18) as recommended; the steps below name the point each rests on. Built in the login service (`services/auth`): step 3's record at a credential issue and step 8's consumers and rights. Built on the bridge (`services/bridge/internal/inbound`, `internal/mmws`, the home's reader for step 6): steps 1, 2, 4 to 7 and the bridge's half of steps 3 and 10. Built in the MCP server (`natsbus.py`, `nats_port.py`): step 9, as it says. Not built: step 3's record at a person's login without an agent and at an administrator's change of the binding; an account without a record goes out under `-` (point 10). The bridge keeps, per post in `BUS_BRIDGE_SEEN`, `p.<post>` for a carried post and `r.<post>` with the kind it last carried per emoji and Mattermost account, so an opposite kind clears a key; the catch-up finds reactions in the post metadata of the `since` read, which returns a post whose reactions changed, and reads a removal only where that metadata is present. Its watermark is `wm.<home>` in `BUS_BRIDGE`, on Mattermost's clock (the `Date` header of `GET /api/v4/system/ping`): set to it on the first start, moved by each live event it carried, to Mattermost's clock at the read's start once a whole catch-up went through (oldest first; a catch-up that fails half way moves nothing, so the retry reads the older posts again), and, while the home is connected and quiet and no event is queued, to a minute behind that clock at each signal; the read and the bound both start at the watermark minus 5 seconds, the keys keep that margin from carrying twice. Mattermost moves a post's `update_at` on every added or removed reaction and prepares the `since` read with the post metadata, so the read returns a post whose reactions changed with its current reactions; a deleted post's reactions are not carried. A publish goes out only once the server took it (a flush after it, a refusal is an error): a refused publish carries nothing, marks nothing and stops the home with its signal. The bridge holds no read right on `PRESENCE` beyond its own consumer of the account records, which it makes at every start with the create right `$JS.API.CONSUMER.CREATE.PRESENCE.bridge_accounts.presence.account.mattermost.*.v1` (the name and the filter fixed in the subject, so it can make or change no other consumer; no delete right). A consumer of that name from an earlier start that is still there refuses the new config (`consumer already exists`), and one created with the same config would hand out nothing again (a test pins both): the bridge then stays unsubscribed for the inactive threshold, so the old one goes, and creates again — up to about 30 seconds after a quick restart. The consumer is gone 30 seconds after nobody listens; one that goes silent for three heartbeats, or whose sequence shows a gap (ack none: a delivery's consumer sequence, or the heartbeat's `Nats-Last-Consumer`), is replaced, and the new one reads every record into a map of its own that takes over only once it caught up, so an account whose record went away keeps no old anchor; meanwhile no home carries. No home carries or signals until the bridge has read the records (a delivery with nothing pending, or the consumer's idle heartbeat); an account without a record then is unbound.

**Trigger:** A `posted`, `reaction_added` or `reaction_removed` event on the bridge's Mattermost WebSocket in a channel of `CB_BRIDGE_HOMES`, or a reconnect of that WebSocket.

**Participants:** Central bridge, Login service, MCP server; NATS server (JetStream), Mattermost WebSocket API, Mattermost REST API

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` The subject is the claim — only the bridge may publish a person's event, the author comes from Mattermost's own event and never from the body, and nothing approves without the login service's fresh record of an approver

### Sequence

1. **What the bridge reads.** The bridge's bot, a member of each home channel it mirrors (RT-6.36 step 6), holds a WebSocket to Mattermost and takes three events in the channels of `CB_BRIDGE_HOMES`: `posted`, `reaction_added`, `reaction_removed`. Edits and deletes are not carried: a person who changes their mind writes a new post (point 5). Direct messages are not read (point 14). After a reconnect the bridge reads each home channel's posts since its watermark over REST and carries only those created after it (`create_at` after the newest it carried; `since` also returns edited posts, which are not carried again), at most 10 minutes back and nothing on its first start, through the same drop rules as the live path (step 2). A reaction is bounded by its own `create_at`, not its post's, so a 👍 on an older ask during a reconnect is carried; the bridge finds it through the same read alone: Mattermost raises a post's `update_at` when a reaction is added or removed, so `since` returns that post with its current reactions (`metadata.reactions`). A post with `delete_at` set, which `since` returns too but without reactions, is skipped in that comparison: deletes are not carried (point 5). A removal has no time of its own (Mattermost sends `reaction_removed` with `create_at` 0), so the catch-up sees it only as a reaction missing on reread: the bridge compares the reread reactions of a post with the reaction keys it holds for that post in `BUS_BRIDGE_SEEN` (the last hour) and carries an `unreaction` for each key that is missing. A removal during a gap of a reaction older than that hour, or of one the bridge never saw, is lost. Live removals over the WebSocket are bounded by the time they arrive, not by `create_at`, and are unaffected. A reaction on an ask is carried like any other, although the ask itself, an agent's Mattermost post, is not. An event's key is a post's `mm`, and for a reaction or unreaction its `mm`, emoji, sender and kind; an event of the opposite kind for the same `mm`, emoji and sender clears the key, so a reaction taken back and given again counts again (chosen over adding the reaction's `create_at` to the key); the bridge keeps its watermark per home in its bucket `BUS_BRIDGE` and the keys it carried in a bucket of their own (`BUS_BRIDGE_SEEN`, `max_age` one hour, the longest any key is needed for, `discard: old`), so nothing goes out twice and the keys never fill `BUS_BRIDGE`, whose `pos.<home>` keys of the first step have no age limit (point 11).
2. **What it drops.** The author is the event's `user_id` alone, never a name, a prop or the text. The bridge drops, without carrying anything: a post with `from_webhook` or `override_username` in its props, a post of any type but the ordinary one (system posts: joins, header changes), a post or reaction by a bot account (`is_bot`, read from Mattermost by `user_id` and cached; point 6), and the bridge's own posts and reactions. Every other prop a person set is stripped: the body carries none. A post's files stay on Mattermost; the body carries only their count. A text over 16 000 characters (runes) is cut there, and further until the body fits the `BUS` stream's 64 KiB, and marked `cut`, as the mirror cuts the other way.
3. **The person behind the account.** *Built for the credential issue only: a login whose Mattermost account is proven to be the Keycloak identity's (its `auth_data` equals the token's `sub`, a password account gets none) writes the record after the attestation, with the account's Mattermost user id; a token without `iat`, or older than the newest the identity logged in with (`tokiat.<sub>` in the directory, compare and set), writes none and the stored record stays; an equal `iat` with the same flag renews the record, and of two tokens of the same second the one without the approvers' group wins, so the flag never goes back up within a second; the check and the write are one step per identity (a lock per `sub` in the one login service instance, as the consumers' per-agent lock; a second instance is not supported), and a newer record of the same account already stored counts as written, so agents of one person logging in at once are all let in; a service without the directory writes none; a record that cannot be written refuses the login, as the attestation does. Not built: the record at a person's login without an agent (point 9) and at an administrator's change of the binding, with `anchor` `null` (point 7).* The login service publishes one record per bound Mattermost account on `<presence>.account.mattermost.<user id>.v1`: `{"v":1, "account", "anchor", "name", "approver", "token_iat", "iat", "exp"}`, like the attestation records (RT-6.41), at each credential issue for that identity's agents, at a person's login without an agent (point 9) and when an administrator changes the binding, with `anchor` `null` once the account is unbound (point 7). `approver` says whether the Keycloak token behind the write placed the identity in the approvers' group (ADR-014 point 10); `token_iat` is that token's issue time, and the service never replaces a record with one whose `token_iat` is older than the newest it keeps per identity in the directory, its own store, so a record erased on NATS does not reset that guard; `exp` is the expiry of the NATS credential issued from that token (point 8). Only the login service may publish there; the bridge reads the records through a push consumer it makes itself at every start (`bridge_accounts`, deliver subject `deliver.bridge.accounts.<nonce>`, last per subject, ack none, decided by the spec's author on 2026-11-25 in place of the operator's durable consumer) and gets no right on the channel directory's bucket. Both the bridge and the MCP server decode a record strictly: format v1, exactly its fields, no unknown or duplicate key, an empty or header-only message refused; a record that does not decode counts as unbound and no approver. An account without a valid record, or with `anchor` `null`, is unbound. Before the login service writes records, every account is unbound (point 10).
4. **The subject.** The bridge publishes the event on `<bus>.hu.<home>.<anchor>`: the home channel's token as in `<bus>.ch`, and the person's anchor as the last token, the sender, written as in the presence subject (`.` as `~`). Every unbound account publishes under the one token `-`, which names nobody (points 3 and 4). The bridge's NATS user may publish `<bus>.hu.<home>.*` for each home it serves and nothing else on the bus: never `<bus>.hu.>`, never `<bus>.ch` or `<bus>.dm` (point 1). It publishes without a reply subject; the `BUS` stream acknowledges nothing (`no_ack`). Once NATS has taken an event the bridge logs one INFO line, `inbound: carried` with `home`, `what` (`post`, `reaction` or `unreaction`), `post` (the Mattermost post id) and `subject`: never the text, an emoji or a name beyond the anchor in the subject (`TestACarriedEventIsLoggedWithoutItsText`).
5. **The body** (format v1, [protocol.md](../protocol.md#over-nats-peoples-posts-and-reactions), point 18): `post`, `reaction` or `unreaction`, with the Mattermost post id (`mm`: the person's post, or the post reacted to), for a post the thread's Mattermost root (`mm_root`) and, when the bridge knows it, the root's or the reacted post's stream sequence (`root`, `ref`), the person's Mattermost username to show (`name`, display only) and a post's file count. No `props`.
6. **Sequences.** The `BUS` stream carries `<bus>.hu.>` beside `<bus>.ch.>`, so a person's post has a stream sequence like an agent's (point 15). The operator's consumer for each home (`bridge_<home>`) filters both subjects; the bridge records the Mattermost id and the sequence of each carried post in `BUS_BRIDGE` and never mirrors a `<bus>.hu` message back into Mattermost (point 17).
7. **The bridge's signal.** Every minute the bridge publishes `{"v":1, "home", "people":true, "iat", "exp"}` on `<presence>.bridge.<home>.v1` for each home it carries, valid 3 minutes, with `"bot_prefix"` beside them while it posts the agents' copies under their own bots (RT-6.44, the copies of RT-6.36 step 4; only then, since a host before that release refuses the unknown field and reads people on Mattermost), but only while its WebSocket is connected and its catch-up is done; on a disconnect it stops, and the signal runs out (point 12). Only the bridge may publish there; agents read it through their presence consumer and decode it as strictly as an account record. The hosts' rule assumes only the bridge holds the token of a bot named prefix plus a NATS sender: the bridge's prefix must not be one the registrar (or anyone else) creates bots under, or a host could register `<prefix>agent-<another's name>` and have its own posts and reactions with that agent's `nats_id` dropped by every agent while people see them; so the operator gives the bridge a prefix of its own, distinct from the registrar's `BOT_USERNAME_PREFIX` (Section 7, #151 step 1).
8. **Who reads it.** *Built.* The login service grants each agent, for every granted channel, a consumer of `<bus>.hu.<channel>.*` named `<agent>_hu_<channel>` (deliver subject `deliver.<name>.hu.<channel>`, ack none, point 16), made and, at the agent's bye, deleted with its `<agent>_ch_<channel>`, and adds the account records and the bridge's signals to the agent's presence consumer. An agent holds no right to publish on `<bus>.hu`, the account records or the bridge's signal: a person is vouched for by the bridge's publish right alone, not by a signature per event (point 2; the risk this leaves is RISK-21).
9. **The MCP server.** It decodes a `<bus>.hu` body more strictly than a `<bus>.ch` one, which passes unknown keys (version, kinds, forms, no unknown key, no duplicate key, no `props`; anything else is dropped with a warning) and takes the author from the subject's last token, never from the body. The event is a person's, never an agent's. It is from a trusted human when the anchor is one of the session's trusted anchors (RT-6.41); `-` never is. It wakes as the rules on who may wake an agent say for a person (RT-6.16). It approves an open ask (RT-6.11) only when, in addition, a fresh account record for that anchor says `approver` `true` (point 8). Per channel, while a fresh bridge signal names the channel, the server takes that channel's people from NATS only and drops the Mattermost side's people; without one it reads people on Mattermost as today. A home and a guest channel are switched alike, each by its own signal (point 14). When the signal runs out, the server first reads that channel on Mattermost from its last people event on, then goes on there.
