At a switch both sides may deliver the same event once: the server drops an event whose key it holds (a post's `mm`; a reaction's `mm`, emoji, sender and kind, cleared by an event of the opposite kind, step 1). Other bots' posts and direct messages it still reads on Mattermost (points 6 and 14). Replies to a person and reactions on a person's post still go to Mattermost by the `mm` id, and an ask stays a Mattermost post whose replies and reactions arrive over NATS by `mm_root` and `mm` (RT-6.36 step 3). How the agent sees it: a person's post keeps its Mattermost id (`mm`) and its thread root is the NATS post `root` names when the session holds it, else `mm_root`; its author is `hu:<anchor>` (`hu:-` for every unbound account), shown under the body's `name`, never a Mattermost account or an agent. The session's trusted humans gain `hu:<anchor>` for each anchor of its own attestation, under the attested name; an event of a trusted person without a fresh approver record is marked `not_approver` (also as a channel tag) and approves nothing: no ask, no relayed tool permission (RT-6.13). The reads mark it alike: `get_thread`, `list_recent` and an ask's card show `not_approver` on such a post, a reaction and the card's answer, judged as the event came and remembered 24 hours; such an answer does not stop an ask's reminders (RT-6.19), so the human learns the ask still waits. A person's files are read from Mattermost by the post's `mm`, so `get_file` fetches them. The server watches the signals every 10 seconds and at each people event; a signal counts only while the connection is up and its own presence comes back (else the server's clock stands still), and never longer than 3 minutes from its message, a record's `approver` never longer than 15. A person's event over NATS is delivered also while no signal is fresh, beside Mattermost's, and the second of the two is dropped by its key. The server holds each key for an hour; after a signal ran out, and after a gap on the person's consumer (which has no `since`), it reads Mattermost with `since` (which returns older posts whose reactions changed too) from a minute before the channel's last people event over NATS, but never from further back than the keys reach (by the later of this host's and the NATS server's clock; a skew of more than a minute beyond both could reopen it), after a gap from the last people event before it, and a read that fails is tried again at the next look. On Mattermost's side it takes the sender of a reaction as the anchor the account's record names, or last named before a message that did not decode erased it (`-` without one). Bots are told apart by their account; a webhook or system post is never a person's and stays on Mattermost; an edit is not carried, and in a switched channel Mattermost's edits of people's posts are dropped too.
10. **Reply subjects.** NATS checks the subject of a publish but never its reply subject, and JetStream answers onto it (ADR-014 point 16). Agents hold no JetStream right, but the bridge's and the login service's users do, so a JetStream answer (a key-value or stream read, an ack) can land on a person's subject, an account record or a bridge signal. No strict decoder takes such an answer for a v1 body or record (step 3, step 9). Since `PRESENCE` keeps one message per subject, a stray answer can erase an account record until its next write; the account then counts as unbound, which fails closed. Direct get stays off on every stream (`allow_direct`), so no stored message can be replayed onto these subjects.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | The WebSocket drops | The bridge reconnects and catches up over REST; what it carried before is not sent again. It waits 1 s after the first drop and after a socket that held 30 s, otherwise twice the last wait up to 30 s. An authentication challenge that takes longer than 20 s in all (the dial and upgrade before it have their own 45 s), or a peer that sends a frame over 1 MiB or more than 128 events before the authentication reply, counts as a failed connection |
| 1 | A gap longer than 10 minutes, or a first start | What is older is not carried; the bridge logs the gap |
| 1 | During a gap, a person removes a reaction older than an hour, or one the bridge never saw | Lost: no key to miss on reread, no `unreaction` is carried |
| 1 | `BUS_BRIDGE` or `BUS_BRIDGE_SEEN` refuses a write (full, or a missing right) | The bridge logs the refusal with the bucket's name, stops carrying that home and stops its signal, so sessions read people on Mattermost again (step 9); it retries, and carries on from its watermark once the operator has made room |
| 2 | A webhook, `override_username`, system or bot post, or the bridge's own | Dropped, logged without content |
| 2 | A person's text over 16 000 characters, or a body over 64 KiB | Cut and marked `cut`; the full post stays on Mattermost |
| 3 | No account record, a record that does not decode, or `anchor` `null` | Carried under `-`: a person no session trusts |
| 3 | The record is stale (`exp` passed) or says `approver` `false` | The post wakes as a trusted human's may, and approves nothing |
| 3 | A write with a token older than the stored record's | Refused by the login service; the stored record stays |
| 4 | The bridge publishes outside `<bus>.hu.<home>.*` of its homes | Refused by the NATS server |
| 8 | An agent publishes on `<bus>.hu`, an account record or a bridge signal | Refused by the NATS server |
| 9 | A body that is not format v1, carries `props`, an unknown key or a malformed id | Dropped with a warning |
| 9 | No fresh bridge signal for a channel (a bridge without step 2, stopped, or its WebSocket down) | The server reads that channel's people on Mattermost from its last people event on, as today |
| 9 | The catch-up read on Mattermost fails | Logged and tried again at the next look (10 s); the live events on Mattermost go on |
| 9 | NATS is disconnected or the own presence does not come back | No signal counts: the channel's people are read on Mattermost |
| 10 | A JetStream answer lands on an account record | Not decoded: the account counts as unbound until the login service writes it again |

## 6.43 The console on the bus as its operator

**Purpose:** The operator console (ADR-015) reads the bus on NATS as the logged-in person, with a credential the login service issues for that person alone, and shows the open asks and the two stage-1 reports from records the agents publish under their own names. **Partly built:** the fifteen points of the console decision's addition of 2026-11-25 (ADR-015) are for the owner. Built is the login service's part of steps 2 to 5 and 7: each agent's publish right on its one ask record (`<presence>.ask.<home>.<name>`, its own name, its hello's home only) and on its `STATS` subject (`<stats>.<name>`, `CB_AUTH_STATS_PREFIX`), and the `STATS` stream the service makes and checks at its start (`internal/callout/perms.go`, `internal/consumers/streams.go`); and the person's path in the auth callout and each login's two consumers (`internal/callout/person.go`, `internal/consumers/person.go`, `internal/directory/persons.go`), off until `CB_AUTH_CONSOLE_CLIENT_ID` is set; the channels a person may see are the public ones, those the directory lists the person as a member of, and the home and guest channels of the person's own agents and of agents that trust the person directly, from the login service's trust index (`internal/directory/trustindex.go`). Built too is the MCP server's and the console's part of steps 5 to 8: the ask record and the `STATS` lines published, read with the strict decoders, the asks list and the two reports. Built too is the console's part of steps 1 and 4 and point 7 (`console/server/src/console_server/nats_read.py`, `app.py`): each login connects as its operator, renews, and ends; and step 9, the logout that ends the Keycloak session too; the ask records and the `STATS` lines reach a session only through that login's own two consumers, and "for me" and "I may answer" compare the operator's anchor from the login's own record, never anything derived from the login name (point 4). The static NATS account of the pilot is gone. The card's fields in presence are specified with that work, not here. Beside the home a card carries its guest channels, and the graph marks the operator's own person node: both from what the bus itself publishes — the ask record's channel (`<presence>.ask.<home>.<name>`) and a crown claim, each only beside a membership, and the memberships for the home and the guests, and the login's own record's anchor for the `you` mark, never a value the browser sends; people are nodes by `person:<anchor token>` (a channel's and an agent's id their name), the name only the label, so two anchors of one name stay apart (#147).

**Trigger:** An operator logs in to the console or logs out, or their console session renews or ends; an agent's MCP server posts an ask, sees its first answer, resolves it or ends; a server cuts a part of its `STATS` line (RT-6.9 step 2).

**Participants:** Operator console, Login service, MCP server; NATS server (JetStream), Keycloak

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` The person, not the console — the bus decides what an operator reads, the operator's credential publishes nothing, and every ask and every line comes under the name of the agent that made it

### Sequence

1. **Login.** The operator logs in with the console's own Keycloak client (authorization code with PKCE, ADR-015 point 4). The console's server makes a key pair for this login, kept in memory, and connects to NATS with the operator's access token as the connect token (point 1 of the addition). It never derives the person's name: it subscribes to `deliver.person.<login>.presence` and `.stats` by their literal names and takes the name and anchor from the login's own record (step 2), which arrives on the presence subject with the rest; the record's `exp` is that credential's end. Until the record arrives the reading says `waiting: …`, and after 30 s without it it says so. The state of its reading (`reading`, `waiting: …`, `refused: …`, `ended: …`) and the name and anchor go to the browser in `/v1/me` as `bus`. A browser that logs in again ends its previous session, and the console keeps at most four sessions per person (by `sub`), closing the oldest, below the login service's cap of logins. Each session shows the fleet its own consumers deliver; before a login the console reads nothing, and its readiness probe asks only whether the NATS server answers.
2. **The person's credential.** The auth callout takes the person's path only for a token whose `azp` is `CB_AUTH_CONSOLE_CLIENT_ID` and whose `aud` names it, the agent's path only for the bus's client; the console's client has the standard flow alone (device authorization grant, direct access grants, implicit flow and service accounts off). It checks the token against the realm, requires the operators' group (`CB_AUTH_OPERATOR_GROUP`) in `groups`, and reads the person's anchor from the channel directory's name index by `sub` (RT-6.39, RT-6.41 step 2). The name is `<CB_AUTH_PERSON_PREFIX><anchor>` (points 2 to 4). The user JWT's subject is this login's public key, so it is of no use without the login's seed; it allows no publish, subscribe only on `deliver.person.<login>.>` (`<login>`: that whole key in lower case, so no operator can grind a key whose token is another's; the subject names no person), and lives 15 minutes (points 5 and 6). At each issue the service writes the login's own record on `<presence>.person.<login>.v1`, `{"v":1,"name":…,"anchor":…,"iat":…,"exp":…}` with the anchor in its subject form: the console takes the person's name and anchor from it and derives neither. Only that login's presence consumer filters on that subject; no agent's and no other login's does, and only the login service may publish it. The connect token is the bare access token, no JSON hello; the connect's nkey is the login's key and signs the server's nonce ([protocol.md](../protocol.md#over-nats-an-operators-connection)). A refusal names its reason in the login service's log only; the client sees an authorization violation. The access token is a bearer token, not bound to the login's key: whoever holds it can get a credential for a key of their own, until it expires, as far as the console's server holding it is concerned (RISK-12). A NATS reconnect runs the callout again, so the console's server must bring a fresh access token on every reconnect, not only every 10 minutes. One person holds consumers for at most `CB_AUTH_PERSON_MAX_LOGINS` logins at once (default 8): a new login above it is refused, the renewal of a held one passes. The consumers are kept in memory: a server restart drops them with the connections, and the reconnect's callout makes them again.
3. **What the person reads.** At a login's first issue the login service makes `<name>_<login>_presence` on `PRESENCE` (names on the service's side only; both consumers deliver to `deliver.person.<login>.presence` and `.stats`), delivering the last message per subject and then new ones: the login's own record, every agent's presence and attestation, and for each channel the person may see (the public channels, those the directory lists the person as a member of, and the channels of the person's own agents and of agents that trust the person directly, never over several steps: each such agent's home and guest channels, the guest channels for reading only, as the guest channels decision (ADR-016 point 4) asks. These come from the trust index the login service keeps in the directory's bucket: at each agent's credential it writes, from that issue's own checks, the owner's anchor, the trusted anchors as the agent's attestation names them (RT-6.41) and the channels it granted, never the lobby; nothing an agent publishes about itself goes in, and a channel its owner is no member of was not granted. A record counts until that credential's expiry, the agent's next credential replaces it, and every 5 minutes the login service purges the expired ones in the background, never inside an agent's login, so an ended agent's name leaves one purge marker in the bucket, not its records) its crown claims, memberships and ask records (`<presence>.ask.<channel>.*`); and `<name>_<login>_stats` on `STATS`, from 7 days back. At a renewal both are kept with their position while the visible channels are the same; when they changed, the presence consumer is made anew with the new filters. So a trust the agent's host drops, or an agent that ends, leaves the person's view at the person's next renewal after the agent's next credential or its record's expiry: within the agent's 15 minutes and the person's 15. Two logins of one person have consumers of their own. No channel consumers, no direct messages.
4. **Renewal and end.** Every 10 minutes the server refreshes the operator's access token with the refresh token of the ordinary Keycloak session and asks for a new user JWT. At logout, at the end of the console's session or when a refresh or an issue fails, it closes that connection and drops key and tokens. A person publishes no bye: the login's consumers end by their inactivity threshold, 15 minutes without a subscriber (point 6). The new connection subscribes while the old one still stands, then the old one closes; a delivery that arrives on both (the same consumer and stream sequence) is dropped the second time, so no repeat reads as a consumer made anew. A lost connection is taken again the same way, with a fresh token and the same key. A refresh Keycloak refuses (400 or 401; 408, 429 and 5xx pass), or a refreshed token without the operators' group or of another `sub`, ends the console session; a passing failure is retried while the running connection lasts. NATS answers a refused connect and a login service that is down or slow alike (an authorization violation), so the server retries with fresh tokens and backoff until the current credential's `exp` (from the login's record; 15 minutes after the login without one); only then does that session's reading end, its key and tokens wiped, with the reason in `bus`.
5. **The asks on NATS.** The asking agent's MCP server, when it has a NATS link, publishes one record of its open asks on `<presence>.ask.<home>.<name>` ([protocol.md](../protocol.md#over-nats-the-consoles-asks-and-stats-lines)): at most 20 asks, a title each of at most 200 characters, the record within 4 KiB (the encoder stops at 3840 bytes: the stream's limit counts headers too, and it sends none), the rest counted in `more`; the asked human goes by the anchor the agent's own attestation (RT-6.41) pairs with that name, `-` without one; `iat` is the server's clock as the host last saw it, carried forward by its monotonic clock; a new server's first check publishes even an empty list, so a crashed predecessor's record is replaced at once. It publishes at each change (an ask, RT-6.11; its first answer by a trusted human; `resolve_ask`) and every 10 minutes while an ask is open, each valid 30 minutes; at its bye an empty list once. The login service grants each agent exactly that subject, for its own name and its hello's home (points 9 to 11).
6. **The asks in the console.** The ask records reach the console with the presence lines, through the login's own presence consumer, for the channels the person may see. The console shows a record while its `exp`, and at most 30 minutes after the server stored it (the agent's clock is not trusted), is ahead of the console's floored clock. It marks an ask whose `human` is the anchor of the operator's own credential as "for me" (only when the asking agent's attestation also names that anchor), one by an agent whose attestation names the operator's anchor as its owner or among its trusted anchors (RT-6.41) as "I may answer"; one whose `asked` is older than 24 h as expired, a hint only; the asks of an agent without fresh presence as "agent gone". "For me" and "I may answer" are n/a until the login's own record has arrived, which gives the anchor; none is derived from the login name. When the presence consumer is made anew, the console drops the asks it held and shows only what the new consumer delivers, so the asks of a channel the person lost do not linger. `title` is shown as plain text, control and direction characters stripped. It offers no answer: it says the ask is answered in its thread in the chat (point 8).
7. **`STATS` on NATS.** Each MCP server with a NATS link publishes each part of its `STATS` line on `<stats>.<name>`, the same JSON object it posts in the stats channel, beside that post, with keys outside the allow list of `stats.py` left out and `rt` written as `other` for a client outside the known harnesses, at most 8000 bytes; the last part goes out before the bus stops, in a hosted session too (best effort: an exit by signal or by the parent's death leaves without it, the chat line still goes); a failed publish waits for the next part (point 13). The login service makes the `STATS` stream at its start (30 days, 64 MiB, 500 per subject) and grants each agent `<stats>.<name>` for its own name only (point 12).
8. **The reports.** The lines reach the console through the login's own stats consumer, from 7 days back; each session keeps its own reports. The console decodes each line strictly (point 14: strings only in `v`, `sid`, `rt` and `kind`, each by its pattern, every listed key of the type the list gives it, each number an integer 0 to 10^12, a share shown within 0 to 100; keys from the allow list, others ignored and never shown) and counts each line once by its stream sequence (a consumer made anew replays the 7 days), and computes the bus overhead share of all tokens per version (the overhead segment of the token split in input-token equivalents, marked estimated; before it the bus and coordination share, which counted a request the bus caused whole), the asks over 24 hours and 7 days, the bus traffic by category over 7 days (the overhead measurement, RT-6.9 step 3: bus overhead and coordination in items and characters, the overhead's share of the characters, polls and the empty ones' share, and from how many lines), and the tokens by category over 7 days (the token split of the overhead measurement, RT-6.9 step 2: one horizontal stacked bar per row of task work, bus overhead and coordination with the percent on each segment, rows for the fleet, per version, per role and per harness, input-token equivalents by default and raw tokens on a switch; each bar marked "estimated", the report with a one-line note on the method and the coverage: from how many lines and which harnesses) with `stats.py`, the numbers `stats_report` gives on the same lines; a missing field shows "n/a"; the view says the age of its oldest line. It shows no agent's name and no report by home or owner (point 15).
9. **Logout.** The operator logs out with the button in the console's top bar, a form that POSTs `/logout` (a GET answers 405, so no link or image of another site can log anyone out; a POST from another origin, by `Origin` or Fetch Metadata, gets 403, and so does `Origin: null`; the console's pages send `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, under which the browser names the console's own origin on that POST, where `no-referrer` would make it send `null`, while no other site gets a referrer). The server drops the session as at its end (step 4) and clears the cookie, then ends the realm's session too, an RP-initiated logout (OpenID Connect): it redirects the browser to Keycloak's `end_session_endpoint` from the discovery document with `client_id`, `post_logout_redirect_uri` (`<CB_CONSOLE_PUBLIC_URL>/`, which must be registered as a valid post logout redirect URI on the console's client) and the login's ID token as `id_token_hint`, kept in the server's memory with the session and sent expired or not: OpenID Connect allows an expired hint, and Keycloak checks its signature, not its expiry. Without a stored ID token (the session already gone, as after a restart) it sends `client_id` alone with the redirect URI, and Keycloak asks the person to confirm the logout. The hint travels in that redirect's URL, so it lands in the browser's history and in the access logs of Keycloak and of the proxies in front of it; the redirect carries `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, so no cache keeps it and no next page gets it as its Referer. When the server cannot send the browser to the realm's logout (no end-session endpoint, or the issuer's endpoints not discovered yet), it answers with a page that starts no login and says that the Keycloak session is still open, since `/login` would come straight back in through it. Keycloak then sends the browser back to the console, which starts a fresh login: the single sign-on is gone, so the realm asks for the password, and on a shared machine the next person is not logged in as the previous operator.

10. **The metrics page** (#158, ADR-015). `GET /metrics` answers in the
    Prometheus text exposition format (0.0.4), behind the operator's login
    like every `/v1` route — who may scrape is the operator that is logged
    in, the same right the Reports/Stats tab needs: no anonymous scrape, no
    token of its own, and no NATS credential beyond each login's own. Two
    kinds of numbers on one page: the console's own observations,
    aggregated over all sessions (requests by bucketed route, method and
    status — one bounded set of buckets, a variable thread tail is its one
    bucket; the open fleet streams; the sessions held; the readers'
    connection states and connects; the credentials' renewals and their
    failures, step 4; and the records the strict decoders refused, steps 6
    and 8) — every one of them counted **process-wide at the event**, so
    no session's end resets a series — and the agent numbers of the scrape
    session's own reading — the fleet's agents by state and harness, the
    open asks it sees (step 6) and the tokens by category over 7 days per
    version and harness (step 8, RT-6.9 step 2), the same numbers
    `stats.split_summary` gives — served **only while that reading is
    live**: a waiting or ended reader shows no series at all. A value that
    is n/a leaves its series out, never a 0. No session id, agent name,
    owner or home is ever a label (the reading's own stance, steps 6 and
    8); the harness, the readers' own state words and the state words all
    come from bounded sets with anything else counted as `other`, and only
    the newest versions keep their own label, the rest folded into one
    `other` per harness. Every family carries the `choirbus_` prefix. The
    **session cookie is never a scrape credential** (it is that
    operator's whole console): an unattended scrape comes from the
    approved follow-up, a loopback listener with a scrape token and a
    read-only service account on the STATS stream. The console's own
    build is one series, `choirbus_console_build_info{version,revision} 1`
    (#182): the release version and source commit written at image build,
    `unknown` where a run has none; `/v1/me` carries the same two in its
    `build` field, and the console's footer shows them.

    **The unattended page** (the owner's go, #158): with `CB_CONSOLE_METRICS_NKEY_SEED_FILE` and `CB_CONSOLE_METRICS_TOKEN_FILE` (both or neither, the start refuses a pair torn in half) the console runs a service reading of its own — one fleet and one stats book, no person, no anchor, no login — fed by two push consumers the **login service makes** at its start (`CB_AUTH_CONSOLE_METRICS` there, default off: `console-metrics_presence`, filtering `presence.*.*` and `presence.ask.*.*`, delivered to `deliver.metrics.presence`, and `console-metrics_stats`, 7 days back, on `deliver.metrics.stats`). The account that consumes them is a static nkey of the NATS configuration, a fourth in `auth_users`, **subscribe-only** on the two deliver subjects: no publish, no JetStream right, no directory, no person's record — the narrowing of the owner's go. A Bearer scrape token (the token file, compared constant-time) gates **a listener of the unattended page's own**, `CB_CONSOLE_METRICS_LISTEN_ADDR` (host:port, default 127.0.0.1:8748 — the owner's ask said loopback): the token's page serves the service reading there alone, every other path on it 404, and without or with a wrong token 401, plain, no operator session to fall back on. The main listener's /metrics stays the operator's session page; the 
### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 2 | A token of the bus's client on the person's path, of the console's on the agent's, or one naming both or neither | Refused by the callout: no credential |
| 2 | The console's token in an agent's JSON hello, or `CB_AUTH_CONSOLE_CLIENT_ID` unset | The agents' path takes it and refuses it: its `azp` is not the bus's client |
| 2 | A new login of a person who already holds `CB_AUTH_PERSON_MAX_LOGINS` logins | Refused until one of them has been unlistened for 15 minutes |
| 2 | The operators' group is missing from `groups` | No credential; a person taken out of the group loses the console at the next renewal, within 15 minutes |
| 2 | No anchor in the directory for the token's `sub` | No credential; the console says the person is not known to the bus |
| 2 | The person's credential publishes on any subject | Refused by the NATS server |
| 2 | An agent starts a device flow or a password grant with the console's client id | Refused by Keycloak: the client has the standard flow alone |
| 3 | The same operator logs in twice | Each login has its own key, credential and consumers; ending one leaves the other |
| 3 | An agent's host drops a trusted person, or the agent ends | Its record in the trust index is replaced at its next credential, or counts for nothing after its expiry; the person's presence consumer follows at the person's next renewal |
| 3 | An agent names a person as trusted in its own presence, or wishes a home its owner is no member of | Opens nothing: only the record the login service writes counts, with the channels it granted |
| 3 | The trust index cannot be written at an agent's issue, or its bucket cannot be listed or read at a person's | That credential is refused: a record left from before could still name a dropped trust, and a partial list would look whole |
| 3 | One trust record cannot be read (no record's format) | It opens nothing, and the person's credential is issued with the other channels; the login service logs its key, never its content, and its next sweep purges it over the revision it read |
| 4 | The Keycloak refresh fails or the realm session ended | The server closes that operator's connection and drops key and tokens; the browser is sent to log in again |
| 4 | The login service or Keycloak is down at a renewal | The connection runs until the user JWT's 15 minutes end while the server retries (up to a minute apart); after that the operator's fleet receives nothing and ages out (agents shown offline), and the console's log says why; the view does not say it yet |
| 1, 4 | The login service refuses the connect (no operator, no anchor, past `CB_AUTH_PERSON_MAX_LOGINS`), or is down or slow | NATS says the same for both: retried with fresh tokens and backoff until the current credential's `exp`, `/v1/me` saying `waiting: …`; then that session reads nothing more, key and tokens are wiped and `/v1/me` says `refused: …`; the session stays, so the browser shows why instead of going round the login again |
| 9 | The realm's discovery document names no `end_session_endpoint` | Warned once at discovery; at a logout the console's session ends and the browser gets a page that starts no login, saying the Keycloak session is still open; the log says it too |
| 9 | A logout before the issuer's endpoints are discovered (Keycloak down since the console's start) | The console's session ends; the same page, and the log names that case |
| 9 | `post_logout_redirect_uri` not registered on the console's client | Keycloak refuses the redirect and shows its error page; the console's own session has already ended |
| 9 | No ID token stored (the session already gone, as after a console restart), and the person dismisses Keycloak's confirmation | The console's session is gone, the realm's stays: the next `/login` comes back in without a password |
| 1 | A leftover `CB_CONSOLE_NATS_SEED_FILE` | The console refuses to start without reading the file: the static account and its secret go |
| 5 | An agent publishes an ask record under another agent's name, in a home that is not its hello's, or on any further subject | Refused by the NATS server: one subject per agent, so it cannot fill `PRESENCE` |
| 5 | More than 20 open asks, or titles past 4 KiB | The newest are left out and counted in `more` |
| 5 | A server crashes with an ask open | Its record runs out within 30 minutes and leaves the console; no `ended` is needed |
| 5 | An agent without a NATS link asks | Not on NATS: the console does not show it (RISK-13) |
| 6 | A record that is not format v1, over 4 KiB or 20 asks, with an unknown or duplicate key, `props`, a `title` over 200 characters, a `human` that is neither `-` nor an anchor, or a malformed or repeated `mm` | Dropped whole, with a warning that carries none of it |
| 6 | The asked human answers in the chat | `answered` with the next record; the ask stays listed until the agent ends it |
| 7 | An agent publishes a `STATS` line under another agent's name | Refused by the NATS server |
| 7 | An agent floods its own subject | The stream keeps its newest 500 lines; other agents' lines stay |
| 7 | Many names flood together | The stream keeps 64 MiB and drops the oldest lines of all; the report says the age of its oldest line (RISK-22) |
| 8 | A line with a string outside `v`, `sid`, `rt` and `kind` or off its pattern, a duplicate key, or over 8 KiB | The whole line is dropped with a warning that carries none of it |
| 8 | A key outside the allow list | Ignored, never shown |
| 8 | No lines yet, or none in the window | "n/a", never 0 |
| 10 | A STATS line or a presence record breaks the rules of its format | Dropped whole, counted on the page (`choirbus_console_stats_lines_refused_total`, `choirbus_console_fleet_records_refused_total`) at the event, never shown; a group whose report arithmetic fails is skipped, no series |

## 6.44 An agent's own bot in Mattermost

**Purpose:** The central bridge posts each agent's copies of a home over NATS under a Mattermost bot of that agent's own instead of the one bridge bot, so people see who wrote without an attribution line (issue #151 step 1, the registrar's move into the bridge, ADR-016's addition of 2026-11-24, point 3). **Built on the bridge's side** (`services/bridge/internal/agentbots`), off until `CB_BRIDGE_MM_ADMIN_TOKEN_FILE` is set; the hosts' side, which drops such copies, is RT-6.36 step 4. Turn it on only once the hosts run a release with that rule, or every agent reads each NATS post twice. Whatever falls back (step 2 to 6) still goes out as the one bridge bot's copy, and hosts drop those only with `CB_MM_BRIDGE_BOT` set (RT-6.36 step 4): the hosts keep `CB_MM_BRIDGE_BOT` set as today, besides the release that drops the agents' own bots' copies.

**Trigger:** A NATS post or reaction of an agent in a home the bridge mirrors (RT-6.36), while agent bots are on.

**Participants:** Central bridge; Mattermost REST API, NATS server (the presence stream and the login service's account records, RT-6.42 step 3)

**Quality goal illustrated:** `#secure` Least privilege — the system-admin token makes and keeps the agents' bots and removes their reactions when their own token cannot, nothing else, the bot that posts and reads stays without admin rights, and a bot the bridge did not make is never posted with

### Sequence

1. **Start** (`config.Load`, `agentbots.Bots.Check`). With `CB_BRIDGE_MM_ADMIN_TOKEN_FILE` set, `CB_BRIDGE_MM_BOT_PREFIX` is required and must be a username's start (`[a-z][a-z0-9._-]{0,31}`, the pattern the hosts decode); `CB_BRIDGE_MM_HIDE_BOT_DM` defaults to true. The bridge refuses to start when the admin token equals the posting token, when the admin account (`users/me`) is no bot with `system_admin` in its roles, or when the posting bot holds any role beyond `system_user`, `system_post_all` and `system_post_all_public` (so no `system_admin`, `system_manager`, `system_user_manager`, `system_read_only_admin` or custom role). The admin token goes through the registrar's Mattermost adapter (`mattermost.New`), which follows the bridge's `http` rule (`CB_BRIDGE_MM_ALLOW_HTTP`) and no redirect; only the bot management uses it.
2. **The owner.** At each post or reaction of an agent in a mirrored home, the bridge reads the agent's presence owner (RT-6.37 step 1: the subject's owner token, `~` as `.`), a person's anchor, and takes the owner's Mattermost account from the login service's account records (`presence.account.mattermost.<user id>.v1`, which the bridge reads anyway, RT-6.42 step 3): the one account whose record binds that anchor, which must be an active person (`HumanByID`). The owner is never looked up as a username, so whoever holds the anchor as a Mattermost username owns nothing by it. No presence, the owner `-`, no record that binds the anchor, more than one, or the records not read yet: the bridge bot posts, as before, and the next message tries again. When the record moves the anchor to another account, the bot's client is dropped and the bot checked again (step 3), so a bot bound to the earlier account is not used.
3. **Find or create.** The bot is `<CB_BRIDGE_MM_BOT_PREFIX><NATS name>`. Not there: the admin bot creates it (`CreateAgent`, its welcome DM hidden with `CB_BRIDGE_MM_HIDE_BOT_DM`), with the description `bus bridge copies of <name>` as the bridge's mark, and binds the owner (`BindOwner`). There: it is used only when it is an active bot whose description is exactly that mark for this agent and that is owned by the account of step 2 (one the admin bot still owns is bound now); this check runs before every mint, so after each start too. Anything else, a person's account, another's bot of that name or a bot whose owner differs from the record included, is logged and the bridge bot posts.
4. **Its token.** The bridge revokes the bot's active tokens it minted before (every page of the bot's token list, read first) (their description `bus bridge (RT-6.44)`) and mints a new one (`IssueCredential`), kept in memory only: nothing at rest but the admin token file, nothing in the KV bucket.
5. **Join.** The admin bot adds the bot to the team and that home (`JoinChannels`), once per home after each start.
6. **Post.** The copy goes under the agent's bot (an `mm` client with its token, no redirect followed) without the attribution line, mentions defused as before, with `<prefix>nats_id` always and `<prefix>shown_as`, `<prefix>nats_root` as for the bridge bot (RT-6.36 step 4). The agent's reactions go under its bot, one per sender, and each is recorded in the KV bucket beside the bridge bot's counts (per message, emoji and sender), so its removal goes to the account that added it: with the bot's client while it has one, else, or when that fails, with the admin token as the bot (also within the backoff or without an owner now); a failure there is retried, never dropped. A removal also takes the sender out of the bridge bot's count, in case it reacted before its bot was there, and the bridge bot's reaction goes when that count reaches 0. A reaction or post already gone (404) counts as removed, and the counts are still cleaned. The bridge's signal names the prefix (`bot_prefix`, RT-6.42 step 7).
7. **Concurrency.** One name is set up by one caller at a time; the other homes' readers wait for it and then use the same bot.

### Error Handling

| Step | Failure | System Response |
|------|---------|----------------|
| 1 | A prefix without the admin token, an admin token without a prefix, a prefix off the pattern, the same token twice, an admin that is no bot with `system_admin`, a posting bot with a role beyond `system_user`, `system_post_all` and `system_post_all_public` | The bridge refuses to start, naming the setting, never a token |
| 2 | No account record binds the owner's anchor, or more than one does | The bridge bot posts for that agent, logged once; tried again at its next message, never by username |
| 2 | The record's account is no active person in Mattermost | The bridge bot posts for that agent; tried again after the backoff (5 min) |
| 2 | The record moves the anchor to another account | The bot's client is dropped; the bot, still owned by the earlier account, is not used (step 3) |
| 3 | A bot of that name the bridge did not make for this agent, or owned by another account than the record names | Logged; the bridge bot posts for that agent, tried again after the backoff |
| 3, 4, 5 | Mattermost refuses or is out of reach while creating, binding, minting or joining | The bridge bot posts this message; that name waits for the backoff, so an outage makes no storm of calls |
| 6 | Mattermost refuses the bot's token (401, 403) | The bot's token is dropped and the reader retries the message, which the bridge bot posts until the backoff is over; then the bot is found and minted again |
| 6 | A rate limit (429), a 5xx or a timeout on a post or reaction of the agent's bot | The reader retries the message under the same bot; no new token |
| 6 | The removal of the bot's reaction fails with its client | The admin bot removes it as the bot; if that fails too, the reader retries, the record kept |
| 6 | A message Mattermost refuses for good (400, 404, 413) | Skipped as for the bridge bot (RT-6.36) |
